# devhost shared UX pattern reference

Status: draft for review, revision 7.
Revision 7 settles every gap and cross-flow disagreement the 19 Phase 8 reconciliation units reported, recorded item by item in section 3.8 (`R7-1` to `R7-88`): one sign-in sentence (`HOLD-7`), a rejected configuration drawn as a condition (`FB-8`), docked chat and the log panel below 900 px (`SURF-5`), `devhost service restart --all` as Restart Stack's terminal form instead of a `MENU-6` exception, the stack directory's hostname in `O36`'s Host clause, focus after a pick, a menu and an unprompted reload (`KEY-6`, `KEY-10`, `KEY-12`), **Select Again** rebinding a cited mark number (`SEL-9`), retired settings that warn (`FAIL-6`), the source menu's own switch (`SEL-11`), the proxy line and trust sentence (`WORD-12`), the listener-move protocol (`FB-13`), the toolbar's segment order (`BAR-5`), IDs `SD-1` to `SD-7` for 2.4's decision rule, one fixture set and the other wireframe conventions (section 2.15, `WF-1` to `WF-6`), the labels `WORD-2` lacked, corrected quotes in `CC-26`, `CC-27` and `CC-30`, and contract changes `CC-41` to `CC-51`. Revision 6 records the product owner's final Phase 7 answers of 2026-11-25 in sections 5 and 5.2 — leaving zero entries awaiting the owner across `O1` to `O63` — reverses `O7` (external services never block worktree switches and need no warning), reverses `O18` (adds `devhost upgrade` while keeping shipped `devhost restart-all` and project-scoped `devhost stack restart`, withdrawing the `devhost stack replace` rename), settles `O30` through parallel foreground and daemon shutdown inside the configurable 30 s default, reverses `O36` (one shared devhost passkey under a single owned HTTPS domain with phone support and `devhost pair` as fallback), decides `O39` (honor `bindHost = "127.0.0.1"` on macOS from port 1024 upward, keep all interfaces below 1024 with a startup warning), adds `O62` (extend `devhost stack info` and add `devhost status` as a short alias) and adds `O63` (keep strict configured-hosts-only TLS for arbitrary unknown HTTPS hostnames, keep routes and certificates active for recently stopped stacks within 24 hours, and keep typo suggestions on plain HTTP and in the stack directory); it also migrates all backend implementation paths from the retired Go codebase to Rust `main` at `190a8bb5` (Go retired; `process-containment-01`, `worktree-switch-14`, `process-containment-02` and `manifest-hot-reload-2-01` are now fixed on `main`), noting per owner decision q82a1 that the 19 historical current-flow diagrams and 5 research notes remain frozen at `edb5c0fc` while this reference, all 19 proposals and all proposed diagrams target Rust `main`. ## 1. Purpose

This document is the one design reference for every devhost flow. Every flow proposal and every wireframe must conform to it. A flow that needs to behave differently must name the exception here, in section 2 or section 3, with its reason; a deviation without a named exception is a defect in the proposal or wireframe, not a design choice.

How to use it:

- Section 2 states the canonical patterns. Each rule has an ID (for example `SEL-1`) so proposals, wireframes, tickets and tests can cite it. A section this product does not need keeps its heading with "Not applicable:" and the reason, so section numbers stay stable.
- Section 3 records how each cross-flow disagreement found by the audits was settled. Entry IDs match the audit decision IDs (`selection-01`, `review-12`, ...).
- Section 4 lists, for each flow, what its proposal and wireframe must change.
- Section 5 lists the product calls that evidence could not settle, each with a recommended default that flows assume until the product owner decides, and records the product owner's reviews.
- Section 6 records how each critique issue was handled.

Ground rules applied throughout:

- Product direction wins, as the project states it: devhost runs a project's local services behind shared HTTPS hostnames and injects devtools into their pages, and the devtools stay isolated from the page they serve (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`: "The injected devtools UI must remain visually isolated from the host page"), with one explicit product-owner exception (`O16`, 2026-11-18): devhost prefixes the app page's tab title while a waiting agent or failure persists and restores the app's own title when it clears (`ACT-7`). The product owner's settled intents are product direction, never defects, and no rule questions them: a failed service leaves the rest of the stack running, and a detached start stops on a failure before readiness; devhost never opens a browser; devhost never answers or pre-approves an agent's approval or question (standing approvals on the decision card are granted by the person through the agent CLI's own standing-approval option, `O12`); **Delivery unconfirmed** and the refusal to resend a chat message are intended; the config editor is off by default; a worktree switch never falls back silently to another worktree; **Hold Reloads** also holding the app's own `location.reload()` is a documented limitation; external services (`managed = false`) show status only; human output and agent output (`AGENT=1`) differ on purpose; chat never asks for API keys in the browser; every manifest setting applies through hot reload, never "restart to apply"; Caddy listens on all interfaces by default, as documented. The owner's decisions on the chat edit mode and Disable Reloads are `O1` and `O2` in section 5. Revision 4 added the product owner's own rules of 2026-11-18, `HOLD-8`, `ACT-14` and `FB-12`; under `HOLD-8`, the product owner's answers of 2026-11-18 updated two earlier intents that confined a feature to this machine: with the config editor on, authenticated remote browsers (signed in with the shared passkey or paired, `O36`) read and save configuration (`O38`, `O54`), and Watch Live Activity also opens on the stack directory's hostname for this machine and authenticated remote browsers while `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` keeps working on this machine and app hostnames never host it (`O55`). Revision 5 adds the product owner's vocabulary rule `WORD-11` (worktree names every working copy, main's included) and records the product owner's answers of 2026-11-18 removing chat's worktree edit mode (`O1`) and removing editor support entirely (`O50`, `O51`, `O60`). Revision 6 settles every remaining decision (`O7`, `O18`, `O30`, `O36`, `O39`, `O62`, `O63`) and migrates backend references to Rust `main` (`190a8bb5`).
- Where flows already share a convention, it is kept unless there is concrete evidence it is worse; that evidence is stated in the decision.
- Decisions (choices, confirmations, conflict reviews) live in panels, dialogs or pages, never inside an outcome notice. An outcome notice carries one outcome sentence and links.
- Where a rule changes a project contract or feature-roster entry, the rule says so and section 2.14 quotes the text it replaces under a `CC-n` ID. Contracts are cited by quoted text, never by line number, because line numbers drift. Code evidence in rationales and in section 3 cites `path:line` at the revision the status line names.
- The backend owns domain operations. The services runtime (`apps/devhost/src/services/`) and the Rust control server (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/`) decide and perform every write (restart, start, stop, switch, reload, save, chat) and publish the state every surface reads; the injected toolbar, the status pages and the CLI request operations and render what the backend reports, never their own guess. A pattern that needs backend work lists it under `Backend contract:`, and a feature that depends on missing backend records ships with them.
- Security exposures are out of scope, except who can reach chat and the control API, which the product owner's rule `HOLD-8` (revision 4) brings into this reference: no protection may confine a feature to this machine, and the agent-chat redesign makes remote execution safe for use from another environment instead (owner item a2, answered a2a2). `O36` (decided 2026-11-25, owner item q46a6) decides how devhost tells the person's own browser in another environment from every other client — one shared WebAuthn passkey under a single owned parent HTTPS domain (with phone support as a hard requirement), synced via its non-secret public key in `config.toml`, with one-time `devhost pair` links as fallback — and `HOLD-7` describes what an unauthenticated remote browser sees; findings `network-exposure-01`, `proxy-settings-01` and `serve-folder-04` are the evidence those entries cite.

Terms used below:

- **Stack**: one running devhost unit described by a root manifest and its includes, as `CONTEXT.md` defines it. A **serve session** is a manifest-free `devhost serve` session; a rule about stacks covers serve sessions only where it says so, and 2.12 settles what surfaces call one.
- **Service**: a process or route a stack declares. A **managed service** is one devhost starts and stops; an **external service** (`managed = false`) runs outside devhost, which only checks it.
- **The page**: an app page served through one of the stack's hostnames, with the toolbar injected. **The page's service**: the service whose route answered the page (`SEL-3` says how it is resolved); **its dependencies**: that service's `dependsOn` chain as the backend resolves it.
- **Worktree**: any working copy a repository's services run from, whether the repository's main worktree or one of its linked Git worktrees (`WORD-11`). **Selected worktree**: the worktree a repository of the stack runs from now; **configured worktree**: the one the manifest names.
- **Target**: what an action acts on: one or more services, stacks, worktrees, conversations, files or page elements. The **target set** of an action is every target it will touch, including the ones it reaches without naming them (a dependency started with a service, every service a switch restarts).
- **Entry point**: anything that starts an action: a control, a menu item, a key, a command, a link or a control-API route.
- **Surface**: a place devhost shows something, as 2.4 lists them: the toolbar's bar and notice stack, toolbar panels and anchored popovers, menus, docked chat and other docked panels, the Configuration window, the crash view, status pages and Watch Live Activity, the terminal's human output and agent output (`AGENT=1`). An **outcome notice** is a notice in the toolbar's notice stack that reports something that already happened; it is devhost's toast.
- **Write**: any operation that starts, stops, restarts or signals processes; changes files or configuration (a manifest file, the devhost config file, the project's own files through an agent); switches a repository's worktree; asks a coding agent to act (a chat message, an answered approval or question, the Disable Reloads request); or changes the machine's certificate trust. **Read**: anything else, including dry runs (`--dry-run`), listing, finding, filtering, opening a panel and previewing a request.
- **Disruptive**: a write with at least one of these effects: (1) it stops or restarts every service of a stack or of a repository at once (**Restart Stack**, a worktree switch, a hot reload whose restart set covers a repository); (2) it stops, restarts or takes offline a stack other than the page's own, or every stack through the shared proxy (**Stop Stack** on another stack's worktree, `devhost stop --worktrees`, `devhost restart-all`, `devhost upgrade`, `devhost caddy stop`, a devhost config file change that moves every stack's URLs); (3) it ends, hides or discards work in progress (a working or waiting agent, an unsaved draft, page context, a held reload); (4) a coding agent acts on a request devhost wrote on the person's behalf (Disable Reloads); (5) it changes the machine's certificate trust. Restarting or starting services named one by one (a row's **Restart**, **Restart Changed** with its target named, **Start**), saving a manifest file whose reload restarts only the services it names, and sending a message the person wrote are writes that are not disruptive by themselves. Being disruptive decides what the action must show of its reach (2.1, 2.2) and its review (2.5); which disruptive writes ask first, and how, is 2.5's.
- **Unavailable action**: an action that exists for a target but cannot run now (another operation in progress, a lost connection, nothing to act on). **Inapplicable action**: one that can never run for this target in its current kind (restarting an external service). `MENU-3` treats them differently.
- **Human output** and **agent output**: the two contracts of devhost's terminal output, for people and for agents (`AGENT=1`); both are surfaces these rules cover.
- **Mod**: ⌘ on macOS, Ctrl on Windows and Linux. devhost's existing page-wide bindings use physical Alt and Ctrl, not Mod; 2.11 lists them.
- **Operation**: a write the backend runs on a stack or on the machine (a start, stop or restart, Restart Stack, a switch, a reload or save, the Disable Reloads request, a Caddy start or stop), from any origin: this page, another page, the terminal, an agent, or a change to a file on disk that the stack applies (`ACT-1` says what the backend can tell apart). Its **outcome** is how it ended for each target; 2.7 says how both are reported.
- **Condition**: a state that lasts until something changes it (a rejected configuration, a held or blocked reload, an agent waiting for the person, a lost connection, a blocked repository). A **condition notice** reports one, where an outcome notice reports an operation. A stack's **attention state** is every service state and condition that needs the person (`ACT-7`).
- **The page's modal dialog**: a native modal dialog of the page (`:modal`), a visible page element with `role="dialog"` or `role="alertdialog"` and `aria-modal="true"`, or any change by the page that makes the devtools host `inert` or `aria-hidden`; every rule treats the three alike.
- **Examples** (revision 7): one fixture set for every proposal, wireframe and example here (`WF-1`): the machine devbox; the stacks Acme Shop (services web, api, worker, admin and docs), Acme Admin and Acme Platform; the serve sessions notes and demoapp; the worktrees main, fix-nav and hotfix-shop. Proposals and this reference write the product's default local suffix (`shop.localhost`, `admin.shop.localhost`, the stack directory at `devhost.localhost`); wireframes write the same labels under `.acme.test` (`shop.acme.test`, `devhost.acme.test`); the relay form is the label under `[remote].domainSuffix = "--devbox--preview.acme.test"` in both (`shop--devbox--preview.acme.test`). `hotfix-shpo` is the deliberate typo of `O46`'s examples.

## 2. Canonical patterns

<!-- Every subsection uses the same labelled paragraphs in this order. Rules are numbered per prefix; an inserted rule
     gets a letter suffix (REV-1a) instead of renumbering, and a withdrawn rule keeps its number marked withdrawn. -->

### 2.1 Selection

devhost has no multi-select list: its selection is targeting, deciding which services, stacks, worktrees, conversations and page elements an action acts on, and showing that set before the action runs.

Rules:

- **SEL-1 Every action names its target set before it runs.** A control's label, or one line beside it, names its target set: by name when it has one or two members, by count and unit otherwise, with every member listed in its tooltip and accessible description. An entry point without a label (a key, the badge's reload icon) acts on the same set as the control it stands for and names that set in the outcome it reports (2.7). The set shown and the set acted on come from one selector, so they cannot differ. Example: with `api` and `worker` marked changed and `web` running unchanged, the Services button names "2 changed" and its tooltip lists `api, worker`; Alt+Ctrl+R restarts exactly those two and its outcome names them (exact words per 2.12).
- **SEL-2 Restart Changed acts on the changed services and names its fallback.** Its target set is every service marked changed (2.10 says when the mark is set and cleared; changed and failed are independent, so the badge's reload icon stays while an error shows). With nothing changed it targets the page's service, else the stack's primary service, and names it in the label ("Restart docs" on the docs page); this is `O4` (decided 2026-11-18). While another service restarts, it queues like any restart of a different service (`SEL-7`); while the stack restarts or a repository switches, it is unavailable with its reason (`MENU-3`), and its key reports why it did nothing instead of being swallowed. Contract: `CC-6`.
- **SEL-3 The page's service is the implicit target of page-scoped surfaces.** The page's service is the service of the route that answered the page, as the backend resolved it: for a hostname with path routes, the route whose path matched; for a hostname a manifest `serve` service answers, that service; on devhost's own status page about a service, that service. Page-scoped surfaces act on it and its dependencies and name it: the crash view, Restart Changed's fallback (`SEL-2`), **Reload blocked** (it names the changed files under the page's service's watch paths) and page context, which tells the agent the page's URL, service and worktree. Stack-wide views (the Services panel, the minimap and the pinned log panel, the stack directory) show every service and say so; narrowing them is a filter (`SEL-10`).
- **SEL-4 One worktree label and one worktree list everywhere.** A worktree (`WORD-11`) is labelled by its branch, or "Detached" with its 7-character commit ID, followed by its path shortened with `~`; the absolute path is a copyable detail, never the label. The same label names a worktree in the toolbar, on status pages, on stack directory cards (after the stack name), on both sides of a switch in the terminal, in the running agents list ("worktree root" for the worktree's top folder, never ".") and in chat's conversation list. The list (`SURF-14`) puts the main worktree first, then linked worktrees by branch; every row shows its short commit ID and its running, configured, unavailable and in use badges; each badge's reason is text in the row's accessible description, never only on hover, and the highlighted row's remedies sit in a details line below the list (`KEY-10`). Find matches branch and path fuzzily and commit IDs by exact prefix; a hexadecimal query that matches nothing says that no worktree has HEAD at it. A selected worktree that was deleted or unregistered is never preselected: its row shows disabled with the reason, and the lead action becomes returning to the configured worktree (`MENU-5`). Exact words per 2.12.
- **SEL-5 A worktree switch names everything it reaches.** A switch changes one repository's worktree and restarts every managed service of the stack, so its control says so on every surface: "Switch and Restart Stack", with one line naming the stack, the repository and the target worktree (`SEL-4`). It keeps the run's service selection, as the docs promise for the whole run, and so does a start that restores a linked worktree; its outcome names each selected name the target's manifest does not define. Its reach also covers what the old worktree's page and chat hold, and the control names each item that exists before the switch runs: working or waiting agents of the old worktree, an unsaved Configuration window draft, page context, and a held reload, which the page reload after the switch discards (2.5 decides whether and how that asks). Conversations stay bound to their worktree and stay listed on every page of the stack, where their approvals and questions can be answered and their turns stopped; a message goes only to a conversation of the page's worktree (`SEL-8`, `CC-38`). External services (`managed = false`) never block a repository's worktree switch and need no warning before or after the switch (`O7`, decided 2026-11-25): switching restarts every managed service from the target worktree's folder while external services stay untouched and remain marked external in the Services panel.
- **SEL-6 Terminal commands resolve stacks by one rule and print their set.** `devhost stop`, `devhost stack restart` (project-scoped, `O18`), `devhost stack info` (alias `devhost status`, `O62`) and `devhost stack find` resolve a path the way `devhost stop` does: the nearest manifest from the current folder upward or `--manifest`, and `.`, `..` and existing folders count as folders; `stack find` keeps substring matching for other words. A command that acts on more than one stack (`stop --worktrees`, `restart-all`, `upgrade`'s stack-replacement step, `caddy stop`) prints its set before it acts: each stack's name, its worktree (`SEL-4`), its PID and whether it runs in the foreground or detached, plus, for `caddy stop`, the hostnames it takes offline. `--dry-run` prints the set and exits 0 without acting. Human output names stacks, with the PID as a detail; agent output lists the same set in its own contract (2.12). A stack's key in URL paths (`/__devhost__/stacks/<stack>/…`) is its name's slug, plus six hexadecimal characters of its root manifest's path only when two stacks share a slug; `self` and `ping` are reserved and always take the suffix (revision 7). Whether and how they ask is 2.5's.
- **SEL-7 The run's service selection is visible and changeable while it runs.** The Services panel counts every service of the stack and lists the services the run left out in their group, as dimmed "not started" rows with their hostname and **Start**; **Start** names the dependencies it also starts, and a panel with two or more not-started services offers a start-them-all action (`BAR-2`). Starts and restarts of different services queue instead of disabling one another: the services runtime holds the queue and publishes each queued operation in the operation record (`ACT-1`), so every tab, the terminal and `devhost stack info` (short alias `devhost status`, `O62`) show it, it survives page reloads, closed tabs and a lost stream, and any surface that shows it can cancel it with **Cancel Restart** or **Cancel Start** (`WORD-2`); each row shows queued, then starting, then its state, and a row never disappears and never draws running services as down while it starts. The terminal reaches the running stack the same way with `devhost service start|stop|restart <service|group>…`, which waits for health checks and prints each result. A `devhost start` naming services the running stack left out starts them into it and prints what it started (`O6`). Contract: `CC-7`, `CC-8`, `CC-11`, `CC-37`.
- **SEL-8 A message names its recipient before Send.** Before Send, the composer shows the conversation the message goes to (its title, or that Send starts a new one), the harness and model as a chip that opens Chat Settings, and the edit mode with the `SEL-4` label of the worktree the message edits beside the mode label ("This worktree (live) · fix-nav ~/acme/.worktrees/fix-nav") (`O1`, `O3`). A conversation of another worktree than the page's (after a switch, or picked from the list) shows its transcript, its decision cards and **Stop Turn**, but takes no message from this page, because the server sends only into the worktree the page runs (`CC-38`): in place of Send the composer names that conversation's worktree and offers **New Conversation** in the page's worktree with the draft kept; devhost never moves a conversation to another worktree. The harness is fixed for a conversation; another harness is a new conversation, offered as such. Page context, pasted images and the draft go to the conversation the composer shows; the Disable Reloads request goes to the agent its popover names (`O2`). The conversation list names each conversation by its first message, with harness and model, worktree (`SEL-4`), start time and state, sorted by last activity, with the ID behind a copy action; it lists the conversations of every worktree of the stack, the page's worktree first.
- **SEL-9 One element resolver, stable mark numbers.** Select Elements, holding Alt and Alt + right-click resolve the element under the pointer with one resolver and highlight it before the click. Each marker and drawing keeps its number for the life of the draft: numbers are never reused or shifted, gaps are allowed (the one exception: **Select Again** on a number the message cites binds the new pick to that same number, revision 7), and drawings take numbers from the markers' counter and show them on the page. A `#n` in the message that no longer refers to a pick is flagged under the composer before Send, by one of two questions: "Send with #2 missing? Its element is no longer on this page." for a pick whose element left the page, and "Send with #1 missing? It is not in page context." for a mark the person removed (revision 7). Page context shows, per pick, what it will send: the element path, nearby text, the URL and the resolved source file or "no source found". Undo reverses the last page-context action. Marks stay anchored (`SURF-12`); picks reach the page wherever devhost draws nothing (`SURF-11`).
- **SEL-10 Lists filter as you type and say what they matched.** Every list of targets with a filter (the stack directory, the worktree list, the Services panel once it overflows, the log panel's service filter) filters on the page as the person types, keeps group headers with their counts, highlights the matched text, and in an empty result says what was searched with a way to clear it. Each list states its match keys: stack directory cards match stack name, worktree, service names and hostnames, never the shared devhost config file's path; worktrees match per `SEL-4`; services match name, hostname and group. Under configured-hosts-only TLS (`O63`, decided 2026-11-25), arbitrary mistyped HTTPS hostnames fail the TLS handshake so no on-demand certificates are minted; recently stopped stacks keep their configured HTTPS routes and certificates active for `O15`'s 24-hour window so revisiting a stopped HTTPS tab renders the stopped-stack page; on plain HTTP (when `[caddy].httpEnabled = true`), the Caddy 404 names the requested host and path, offers up to three close running hostnames (`O46`) with the path kept, and groups the hostnames that exist by stack and worktree; the stack directory offers the same typo recovery via live filtering. Refresh is a control of its own, never a side effect of filtering.
- **SEL-11 A switch or setting names its reach and whose it is.** A control or setting that turns something off names everything it turns off, and turns off nothing else. The toolbar renders whenever any segment has something to show, and one master switch, `[devtools].enabled = false`, removes it; turning a segment off never turns off **Hold Reloads** or another segment. Third-party integrations have one switch each under their existing master key, whose description says it also removes the Redux inspector; the pressed Redux entry shows its monitor, and closing it is a control of its own. Markdown's **Show source** applies to one file, or is a visible "Rendered | Source" mode that says it applies to every Markdown page on the host. Personal preferences belong to the person, with the manifest as the default: layout per `SURF-7`; key bindings from `[devtools.shortcuts]` in the devhost config file overriding the manifest binding by binding (`O17`, `CC-39`), and `machineName` in the devhost config file (`O61`, `CC-39`); chat's harness, model, open state, side, draft and **Hold Reloads** per stack and page origin, never per worktree, so a worktree switch keeps them (`CC-9`). Editor settings (`[devtools.editor]`, `DEVHOST_EDITOR`) are removed completely (`O51`); the source menu keeps one switch of its own, `[devtools.componentSource].enabled` in the manifest (on by default), which turns off Alt + right-click and Shift+F10 in Select Elements and nothing else, and `[devtools].enabled = false` removes it with the toolbar (`FAIL-6`, `CC-41`; revision 7). A manifest edit reports its reach: every reload names each service it restarted and why (2.7); its default restart scope is `O9` (decided 2026-11-18).

Rationale: VS Code and JetBrains name a rerun action's target configuration, and Tilt's trigger names the resource it updates; Docker Desktop shows a partly started Compose project as running 3 of 6 with Start on each stopped container, and `docker compose up <service>` adds a service to a running project; VS Code, JetBrains and GitHub Desktop show a branch name first with a short commit ID, search by branch, path or commit, and never preselect a missing repository; Chrome DevTools and React DevTools resolve and highlight the element under the pointer before the click, and Figma and Linear keep comment pins numbered and anchored; Cursor and Claude Code's IDE chat show the model and mode before Send; `docker system prune` and `kubectl` name their target set and offer a dry run; VS Code separates user settings from workspace settings. Project contracts: the glossary's Stack; `stacks/worktrees.md` (a switch stops every managed service); `guides/stack-lifecycle.md` (the names a run started with last for the whole run); `stacks/recovery.md` (Restart Changed's documented fallback, changed here). The page's service already scopes the crash view and **Reload blocked**, so `SEL-3` keeps that shared convention and moves the one outlier, Restart Changed's fallback, to it. Findings: `services-panel-2-01`, `services-panel-2-02`, `journey-agent-fix-03`, `stack-scale-08` (Restart Changed); `startup-recovery-01`, `source-and-minimap-04` (the page's service); `worktree-switch-01`, `worktree-switch-03`, `worktree-switch-04`, `worktree-switch-05`, `stack-directory-03`, `reload-and-agents-05` (worktrees); `chat-checkout-switch-01`, `chat-checkout-switch-02`, `chat-checkout-switch-03`, `manifest-hot-reload-2-03` (a switch's reach); `stack-run-stop-06`, `stack-run-stop-07`, `stack-run-stop-08`, `notices-and-feedback-04`, `worktree-switch-07` (terminal sets); `services-panel-2-05`, `stack-scale-06`, `stack-run-stop-01`, `agent-cli-journey-03` (the selection); `chat-compose-01`, `chat-compose-03`, `chat-conversations-01`, `chat-conversations-08` (recipients); `page-context-01`, `page-context-02`, `page-context-04`, `page-context-06`, `page-context-07` (picks); `stack-directory-04`, `stack-directory-07`, `stack-scale-05` (filters); `toolbar-shell-02`, `toolbar-shell-03`, `third-party-devtools-03`, `third-party-devtools-05`, `serve-folder-08`, `source-and-minimap-03`, `manifest-hot-reload-01` (reach of settings).

Use it for every action that has a target: service, stack and worktree actions in the toolbar, on status pages and in the terminal; chat sends; page picks; filters; switches and settings. Do not use it for what an action shows while it runs or after it ends (2.7, 2.8), or for whether it asks first (2.5).

States: a target set of one, of two, and of many members; an empty target set; Restart Changed with changed services, with nothing changed (its fallback named) and unavailable while a restart runs; a page with no service of its own (devhost's own pages, the stack directory); a worktree list with a deleted selected worktree, an in-use worktree and a blocked repository; a switch with and without working agents, drafts, page context and a held reload; a stack with services not started, one queued start and one start in progress; a filter with matches, with no match, and hiding members of a set action (`BAR-3`); a message to an open conversation and one that starts a new conversation; a pick with and without a source file, and a mark whose element left the page; a blocked worktree row highlighted, with its remedy reached by Tab from the find field.

Keys: Alt+Ctrl+R restarts Restart Changed's target set; holding Alt starts Select Elements; Alt + right-click opens the source menu; filters take focus when their panel opens; see 2.11.

Accessibility: every control's accessible description lists its target set; a set's count and members are text, not only dots or color; worktree rows expose the label, the short commit ID and every badge with its reason as text; a queued or starting row announces its state politely without moving focus; filters announce the number of matches; the chip naming the harness and model is a button with that text as its name; picks announce their number and the resolved element, and each highlight move its element's role, name and position (`KEY-12`).

Backend contract: the document injection names the page's service for path routes and `serve` services (`SEL-3`); a worktree switch passes the run's requested service names, as Restart Stack and hot reload already do (`SEL-5`), and never blocks on an external (`managed = false`) service (`O7`); the services runtime accepts a start into the running selection from `devhost start` and from the new `devhost service` commands, runs one such operation at a time, and holds the queue itself: each accepted start or restart of another service is published as queued in the operation record, in order, and can be cancelled by its ID (`SEL-7`, `CC-37`); the published state carries each service's changed mark and the files behind it (`SEL-2`, `SEL-3`); the agent list and chat sessions report their worktree so lists can label it (`SEL-4`, `SEL-8`); the session list answers every session of the stack and the chat socket attaches to any of them, while `send` keeps its check of the page's worktree (`SEL-5`, `SEL-8`, `CC-38`); the control server's layout record holds layout preferences (`SURF-7`); the devhost config file accepts `[devtools.shortcuts]` (`O17`, decided 2026-11-18), `machineName` (`O61`, settled 2026-11-18) and shared-domain WebAuthn passkey configuration (`O36`, decided 2026-11-25) in `apps/devhost/src/config.rs` and its schema (`editor_schema`), and the control server sends the page each merged shortcut binding with its source (`SEL-11`, `CC-39`); `[devtools.editor]` and `DEVHOST_EDITOR` are removed (`O51`).

Component primitive: one shared target-set selector per action (the pattern `selectRestartServiceNames` already follows); the shared worktree list (`SURF-14`); the existing `ToolbarPopover` panels, chat composer and Chat Settings dialog from `@assistant-ui/react` and `@alexgorbatchev/shadcn`; library work: none.

Contract change: `CC-6` (Restart Changed's fallback), `CC-7` (the run's selection changes while it runs), `CC-8` (starts queue instead of disabling one another), `CC-9` (chat settings persist per stack, not per worktree), `CC-11` (the `devhost service` commands), `CC-12` (multi-stack commands print their set and take `--dry-run`), `CC-34` (`stack find` resolves folders), `CC-37` (queued operations and their cancel route in the control API), `CC-38` (chat sessions across the stack's worktrees), `CC-39` (the devhost config file holds the person's key bindings and machine name; editor parts withdrawn in revision 5 under `O51`); `CC-36` (serve sessions in `--all`'s printed set, from 2.8); roster TB-01, SVC-03, SVC-05, WT-01, WT-02, WT-06, STK-03, STK-06, CHAT-01, CHAT-05, CTX-01, CTX-02, SRV-03.

### 2.2 Selection summary and command hints

devhost has no selection bar. Its summaries are the toolbar's Services trigger, which summarizes the stack, and the counts on actions that act on a set (Restart Changed, starting every not-started service, Restart Stack, the terminal's multi-stack commands); its command hints are the key bindings shown on controls.

Rules:

- **BAR-1 The Services trigger summarizes the whole stack.** The trigger shows one dot per service while the dots fit on the bar's row, else a fixed-width summary (`SURF-10`). Either form covers every service of the stack, the services the run left out included, and shows the states that need the person first; which states it counts and their words are 2.10's and 2.12's. Its accessible name is the summary as text. Activating it opens the Services panel.
- **BAR-2 A set action shows its set's size and members.** A control that acts on a set lists the members in its tooltip and accessible description, read from the selector the action uses (`SEL-1`), and shows the set's count: in its label when the label is a verb with its target (Restart Changed and start every not-started service: **Restart 2 Changed**, **Start 5 Services**), while a stack-wide write keeps its operation's name as its label (**Restart Stack**, **Switch and Restart Stack**, **Return to Configured Worktree**, `WORD-2`) and states the count in the statement or reach line `REV-2` gives it, the line beside the control that `SEL-1` allows. Example: on a stack of 6 managed services, the Services panel's footer reads **Restart Stack**, its tooltip lists the 6 services, and its first press states "Restart all 6 services with new automatic ports? …"; in the crash view the button reads **Restart Stack** and its reach line beside it carries the same count. A terminal command that acts on several stacks prints the set first (`SEL-6`). An empty set makes the control unavailable with its reason (`MENU-3`), except where `SEL-2` names a fallback target.
- **BAR-3 A filter never changes a set action's target silently.** While a filter hides members of a set that a visible action acts on, the action acts only on the members the filter shows, and its label says how many are hidden and left out. Example: with 5 services not started and the Services filter "api" showing 3 of them, the start-them-all control reads **Start 3 Services** with "2 more hidden by the filter, not included" under it (exact words per 2.12); clearing the filter restores **Start 5 Services**.
- **BAR-4 Command hints come from the binding's one definition.** A control that has a key binding shows the key in its tooltip and exposes it as `aria-keyshortcuts`, both read from the same definition as the key's handler and the docs, so a hint never names a key that does something else. A hint appears once per control; the devtools contract forbids repeated indicators. Bindings and their notation are 2.11's.
- **BAR-5 One segment order.** The bar reads, from the stack name on: the stack name, Services (with **Restart Changed** beside it while services changed, `SEL-2`), Worktrees, CPU, RAM and Disk, Logs, Config, the third-party launchers, Running agents, Hold Reloads, Chat. A segment that is off or has nothing to show is absent and the rest keep their order; the fit sequence (`SURF-10`) changes their form, never their order; an unavailable item stays in its place (`MENU-3`) (revision 7, toolbar-shell's order).

Rationale: The devtools visual contract asks for no repeated labels or indicators and for visually distinct states. Docker Desktop summarizes a partly started project as running 3 of 6, Tilt and Docker Desktop keep per-state counts beside groups, and VS Code drives a command's shortcut hint and handler from one keybinding definition. Findings: `stack-scale-04`, `services-panel-2-05` (the trigger and its counts); `services-panel-2-02`, `services-panel-2-03`, `stack-run-stop-07` (set actions name their set). `BAR-3` has no finding yet because no set action and no services filter exist today; it settles, before either ships, the gap the selection audit names.

Use it for the Services trigger, every control or command that acts on a set, and every key hint. Do not use it for the meaning of states and counts (2.10, 2.12) or for the bindings themselves (2.11).

States: dots that fit; a summary instead of dots; a set of one, of many, and empty; a set partly hidden by a filter; a control with and without a binding.

Keys: hints show the bindings of 2.11.

Accessibility: the trigger's accessible name is its summary text; a set's members are in the control's accessible description; `aria-keyshortcuts` carries the binding.

Backend contract: none beyond 2.1's.

Component primitive: the existing bar segment and `ToolbarPopover` trigger; tooltips from `@alexgorbatchev/shadcn`; library work: none.

Contract change: none; roster TB-03, SVC-03.

### 2.3 Menus and entry points

Rules:

- **MENU-1 Each target has its stated entry points, all reachable without a pointer.** A service: its row in the Services panel, the pinned log panel's status line, which names the service it filters (revision 7), the crash view and the recovery page for it (which hand off to the restart the Services panel owns), `devhost service start|stop|restart` (`SEL-7`) and the control API. The stack: the stack name menu on the bar (**Move to**, **Reset to Manifest Default**, collapse, and **All Stacks on devbox**, which opens the stack directory and names the machine by `O61`'s name), **Restart Stack** in the Services panel and the crash view, and the terminal. A worktree: the worktree list in the Worktrees panel and on status pages (`SURF-14`), and the terminal. A page element: Select Elements, holding Alt, Alt + right-click for its source menu, and the drawing tools. A conversation: the conversation list in chat's header (`SEL-8`). Every entry point that opens a menu or panel also has a keyboard path (2.11).
- **MENU-2 Headings label two or more items.** A group heading sits only over two or more items. A lone item joins its closest related group instead of getting a heading of its own. The one exception is the destructive last item, which stands alone after a separator and without a heading. When a context hides items and leaves a group with one item, that item drops its heading and stays in place after a separator.
- **MENU-3 Inapplicable actions are absent; unavailable actions are disabled with a visible reason; nothing that cannot work looks live.** An action that can never run for this target (**Restart** on an external service) is absent, and the surface says once, as visible text, why devhost cannot do it. An action that exists for the target but cannot run now (another operation in progress, a lost connection, nothing to act on, a blocked repository) stays in place, disabled, with its reason as visible text beside it or in the panel's status line and as its accessible description, never only in a hover title. While the status stream is lost, every write control is disabled with the reason that devhost is not reachable from this page. Under the page's own modal dialog the toolbar shows its paused state (`SURF-11`). A key whose action is unavailable reports why in an outcome notice instead of doing nothing silently. Starts and restarts of different services queue rather than disable one another (`SEL-7`). A gesture devhost takes from the page answers even when it has nothing to show: Alt + right-click on an element with no known source opens the source menu saying so. A feature that is off says how to get what it gives: with the config editor off, **Edit** gives way to the file and line with **Copy** (`MENU-4`; editor support is removed under `O51`). What can block an action in devhost, and how the person rechecks it, is 2.6's.
- **MENU-4 An entry point to a problem opens the problem.** Every surface that reports a manifest or devhost config file problem leads to the file and line that hold it, from one structured error (file, line, column, key, message). **Edit** in a rejection notice opens the Configuration window on that file at that line, marked, with the rejection shown in the window until a reload is accepted (`SURF-4`), and the notice names the file and line. The crash view and a failed service row offer **Edit** beside **Restart**, opening the service's definition. With the config editor off, each of these shows the file and line with **Copy** instead of **Edit** (devhost has no editor integration, `O51`). A worktree's unavailable reason offers **Edit** on the manifest at its line in that worktree when the config editor is on (else the file and line with **Copy**), and **Copy Error**, in the worktree list's details line for the highlighted row (`KEY-10`). A rejected devhost config file change names `config.toml` and the line.
- **MENU-5 A recovery surface offers the actions that can fix its cause, led by the likeliest.** By cause, with the lead action first:

  | Cause | Lead action | Also offered | Absent |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | The service exited | **Restart** the service | **Edit** its definition (`MENU-4`), the logs (`SURF-13`); after a failed retry with the same result, **Edit** leads | — |
  | A manifest change was not applied because a service exited during its reload (revision 7) | **Logs** of the failing service (`SURF-13`) | **Edit** with the config editor on, else the file and line with **Copy**; **Copy Error** | **Restart** before the manifest is fixed |
  | The selected worktree is deleted or unregistered | return to the configured worktree | choose another worktree (`SEL-4`) | **Restart**, disabled with its reason until a worktree is chosen |
  | A fresh worktree needs setup | the command that sets it up, named from the failing service's output, to run in that worktree, then **Restart** (`O32`) | that service's output | — |
  | An external service is unreachable | check again | why devhost cannot restart it | **Restart**, logs |
  | The service is starting | none: progress until ready (2.7) | the latest output | — |
  | The service was not started in this run | **Start** the service, as the Services panel does (`O10`) | what else it starts | — |
  | The app answered with a 404 or 5xx | **Retry**, with the app's own document shown (`SURF-16`) | **Restart** the service as a secondary action; the worktree list collapsed | — |
  | No route for the hostname (Caddy 404 on plain HTTP; recently stopped HTTPS routes render the stopped-stack page, `O63`) | the close hostname with the path kept (`SEL-10`, `O46`), or the stopped stack's start command (`O15`) | **All Stacks on devbox** (`O61`) | — |
  | devhost's file server has no such file | the nearest listing | close names | **Restart**, worktree list, logs |
  | Any cause, on a read-only surface (the stack directory, `O10`; revision 7) | a link to the page that performs the lead action, with its terminal command and **Copy** | **Check Again**, the one action that runs in place | every write |

- **MENU-6 One name, one behavior, on every surface.** An action with one name does the same thing on every surface that offers it: the toolbar, status pages, the terminal and the control API. Every lifecycle action a browser surface offers has a terminal command: restarting, starting and stopping a service (`devhost service`, `SEL-7`) included, and the crash notice's terminal line names that command instead of saying devhost waits for a restart. The toolbar's **Restart Stack** restarts services inside the running stack while shipped `devhost stack restart` (project-scoped), `devhost restart-all` (all stacks) and `devhost upgrade` replace the running devhost supervisor process; 2.12 keeps their help and output explicit about supervisor replacement (`O18`, updated 2026-11-25). The control API answers a mistyped or inapplicable service with a documented status and one JSON error shape (`422` with `{error, service}`, `CC-37`). The browser still cannot stop its own stack (`O10`, decided 2026-11-18). The terminal form of **Restart Stack** is `devhost service restart --all`, which restarts every managed service inside the running stack and ships with `CC-11`'s commands; the label and the command differ because `devhost stack restart` keeps its shipped meaning (`O18`), and the command's help says "Restart Stack in the browser". The crash line and the recovery page name it, so no lifecycle action a page offers lacks a command (revision 7, refusing the proposed exception).

Rationale: Docker Compose, Overmind and Tilt offer the same per-service verbs in the terminal and the UI, and JetBrains and VS Code pair every run-panel action with a command and a key; Vite and Next.js overlays link each error to its file and line, and VS Code's Problems panel jumps to the line and column; GitHub's merge box lists every unmet requirement beside the disabled button, Docker Desktop hides actions that cannot apply to a container, and VS Code shows a disabled command's precondition; Tilt's resource page leads with the failing step and offers its trigger only for the resource that failed. Project contracts: the devtools visual contract (disabled, error, active and neutral states visually distinct; no repeated indicators); ownership notes in the flow table (services-panel owns restarting a service; startup-and-recovery's buttons hand off to it). Findings: `journey-new-project-02`, `notices-and-feedback-02`, `manifest-hot-reload-2-04`, `toolbar-shell-07`, `chat-conversations-09`, `page-context-06`, `source-and-minimap-02` (unavailable actions); `config-editor-09`, `manifest-hot-reload-02`, `journey-crash-to-fix-01`, `journey-crash-to-fix-02`, `worktree-switch-02`, `proxy-settings-04`, `stack-run-stop-09` (problem entry points); `error-pages-03`, `error-pages-04`, `serve-folder-06`, `worktree-switch-05`, `worktree-switch-06` (recovery actions); `agent-cli-journey-03`, `agent-cli-journey-07`, `stack-run-stop-02`, `stack-directory-06` (one name, every surface); `agent-cli-journey-08` (control routes the docs leave out, `CC-37`).

Use it for every menu, every entry point, every disabled or missing action and every recovery surface. Do not use it for confirmations (2.5) or for the outcome an action reports (2.7, 2.8).

States: a menu with all its groups; a menu where a context hides items and leaves a lone item after a separator (`MENU-2`); an available, an unavailable (with its reason) and an inapplicable action; every write control while the status stream is lost; the paused toolbar; each recovery cause in `MENU-5`; a problem with the config editor on and off.

Keys: the stack name menu, the source menu and every panel open from the keyboard; arrow keys move within a menu; Escape closes it and returns focus to its trigger; see 2.11.

Accessibility: menus use the menu pattern (`role="menu"`, items as `menuitem`, a heading as a labelled group); a disabled item stays focusable in its menu and exposes its reason as its accessible description; the visible reason beside a disabled control is the same text; the source menu opened by Alt + right-click takes focus and returns it to the page element's position when it closes.

Backend contract: one structured error (file, line, column, key, message) from `apps/devhost/src/manifest/` and `apps/devhost/src/config.rs`, published with every rejection and start failure (`MENU-4`); the published service state carries the cause that `MENU-5` keys on (exited, worktree unavailable, needs setup, external unreachable, starting, not started) (2.10); the `devhost service` commands (`SEL-7`), which reach the running stack through its control server on the loopback `control_port` that `apps/devhost/src/caddy/stacks.rs` records in the state directory; a stop operation in the services runtime, which today stops services only inside a restart or a reload (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs`), with its `stop-service` route; documented status codes and one JSON error body, `{error, service}`, on every write route instead of today's plain-text 500 (`MENU-6`, `CC-37`).

Component primitive: `@base-ui/react`'s Menu for the stack name menu and the source menu, because its disabled items stay focusable, or `@radix-ui/react-dropdown-menu` items rendered with `aria-disabled` and a guarded `onSelect` instead of `disabled`, which Radix removes from focus and type-ahead; the existing `ToolbarPopover` panels; the shared recovery view (`SURF-14`); library work: the project's `Button` (`packages/devhost-ui/src/components/ui/Button.tsx`) gains an unavailable state that renders `aria-disabled="true"`, ignores activation and points `aria-describedby` at the visible reason (`MENU-3`, `HOLD-1`), because today it passes only the native `disabled`, which drops a focused control's focus to the body.

Contract change: `CC-11` (the `devhost service` commands), `CC-37` (their routes, status codes and error body in the control API); `CC-4` (the recovery table's lead actions, from 2.4), `CC-8` (an unavailable Start, from 2.1); roster TB-02, SVC-01, SVC-02, REC-02, REC-03, REC-04, REC-05, REC-06, WT-03, WT-05, CFG-06, SRC-01.

### 2.4 Surfaces

devhost's surfaces: the toolbar's bar and its notice stack (together, the dock), in the page's top layer; toolbar panels and anchored popovers; menus; docked panels (docked chat, the pinned log panel, a library's docked devtools panel); the Configuration window; the crash view; full pages (status pages on the stack's hostnames, the stack directory, Watch Live Activity); outcome notices; and in the terminal, human output and agent output (`AGENT=1`).

Decision rule. Ask in order and use the first surface that fits; flows cite each question by its ID, `SD-1` to `SD-7` (revision 7), never as "`SURF-4` rule 4":

- **SD-1 Panel or popover.** A status list, picker, small choice or confirmation tied to one bar control or notice, read or answered and then dismissed by clicking away or Escape? → A toolbar panel or an anchored popover; a confirmation for a control inside an open panel expands in place in that panel (`SURF-2`).
- **SD-2 Menu.** A list of commands for one target (a page element, a service, a stack, a worktree)? → Menu (2.3).
- **SD-3 Crash view.** Must the person deal with it before the page can be used at all, because the page's service or one of its dependencies exited? → The crash view, devhost's only modal dialog over an app page, which the person can minimize (`SURF-3`); page-context's crop preview is the one other modal, opened by the person (`SURF-3`, revision 7).
- **SD-4 Docked panel.** Does the person keep working in the page while it stays open beside it? → Docked panel: docked chat, the pinned log panel (`SURF-5`).
- **SD-5 Configuration window.** A focused task that needs more room than a docked panel while the page stays visible behind it (editing the stack's manifest files or the devhost config file)? → The Configuration window (`SURF-4`).
- **SD-6 Full page.** Its own address, or an answer devhost gives in place of the app (no route, a service not ready or failed), or a page that must work without the toolbar? → Full page: a status page, the stack directory, or Watch Live Activity in its own tab (`SURF-15`, `SURF-16`).
- **SD-7 Notice.** Only a report of something that already happened? → An outcome notice in the notice stack in the browser, one outcome line in the terminal (2.7).

Rules:

- **SURF-1 One corner order.** The bar and its notice stack form the dock in the toolbar's corner; the notice stack sits beside the bar, between it and every other devhost surface. A toolbar panel, docked chat and the Configuration window are placed and sized so they never overlap the notice stack, in either corner placement, so no devhost surface covers a notice. No devhost surface covers the controls of another open devhost surface: an open toolbar panel, the running agents list included, paints above docked chat (`CC-1`). While a library's devtools panel is docked at the page's bottom edge, the dock moves onto that panel's top edge (to the top of the window when the panel is taller than half of it), so no devhost element overlaps the panel; the person's own placement (`SURF-7`) returns when the panel closes. A library panel docked at the right edge shifts the dock and docked chat inward by its width, as the minimap strip does (`SURF-5`); one docked at the top edge moves a dock placed in a top corner below it (revision 7). Check at every acceptance viewport (`SURF-9`): with Services, Worktrees or Running agents open, and with chat docked compact or expanded, native hit testing at the centre of every notice button returns that button; with the Configuration window open, every notice's text stays visible.
- **SURF-2 Anchored panels and in-place confirmations.** Toolbar panels (Services, Worktrees, Running agents, the third-party launchers) are popovers anchored to their trigger: one open at a time, dismissed by clicking away or Escape, with focus returned to the trigger. A popover opened from a row of a panel (the in-use popover of a Worktrees row) anchors to that row and closes with its panel. A confirmation appears where its control is: a control inside an open panel expands in place in that panel (**Restart Stack** in Services, **Stop Stack** in the in-use popover), and the panel stays open through the operation's progress and outcome; a control on the bar or in a notice opens a popover anchored to it (the Disable Reloads popover anchored to the hot reload notice, `O2`). devhost never asks in a modal dialog, in an outcome notice or through the browser's `confirm()`. Which writes ask, and the question's content, default and keys, are 2.5's.
- **SURF-3 The crash view.** When the page's service or one of its dependencies exits, the crash view opens as a modal dialog over the app page, because the page cannot work without its service; any other exit stays in the Services panel. It never takes the person's work from them: while focus is in a text field (the page's or devhost's), while docked chat holds a draft or a decision card, or while an agent of the page's worktree works or waits, it opens minimized, as the crash notice, and expands only when the person activates that notice; expanded, by itself or from that notice, it focuses its heading, never a button (`KEY-6`). Docked chat and the notice stack stay operable over it, moved into its top layer as over the page's own dialog (`SURF-11`); the page and the bar are inert. The person can minimize it, with Escape or a visible **Minimize** control, to the crash notice, which names the service and reopens the view; while it is minimized, the page and the toolbar (Config, Services, Chat) respond. Escape never dismisses the failure: the view stays minimized until the person reopens it, and the next exit opens it again by the same test. It closes by itself only when the exited services run again or leave the run. An action started in the view reports its progress and outcome in the view, which stays open after a failed attempt, and in the crash notice while minimized. It never opens over devhost's own status page for the same failure: the status page and the crash view are one shared recovery view (`SURF-14`), shown once. One other modal surface exists over an app page: page-context's crop preview, which the person opens, which holds focus, and which Escape closes without sending (revision 7). Contract: `CC-2`.
- **SURF-4 The Configuration window.** **Config**, and **Edit** from any entry point (`MENU-4`), open the Configuration window: a modal dialog with `aria-modal="true"`, the page and the rest of the toolbar inert while it is open, Tab cycling inside it, and focus returned to the control that opened it. Its height is capped so it never overlaps the notice stack, which stays visible beside it. It carries the context it was opened for in a banner at the top of the window, so nothing the person needs while editing stays behind it: for an exited service, its `FAIL-3` evidence (the exit code or signal with its time, the cause sentence and its frozen last output lines) with **Restart** first (`MENU-5`, revision 7) and **Show All Output**, which expands the shared recovery view's log (`SURF-14`) inside the window; for a rejected reload, the structured error (`MENU-4`) with the failing lines marked in the file and, when a crash caused it, that service's last lines. **Edit** in the crash view first minimizes the crash view (`SURF-3`). While the window is open, a report that needs the person (a failure, an agent waiting, a rejected configuration) is announced from inside it (`FB-9`) and shown as one line in its header saying that its notice waits in the notice stack, which the window makes inert (`KEY-7`). Closing keeps unsaved drafts, as today.
- **SURF-5 Docked panels overlay the page in one edge order.** Docked chat and the pinned log panel overlay the page; they never push, resize or restyle the app's layout, because the devtools stay isolated from the page they serve. Along the right edge, from the window edge inward: the minimap strip, then the pinned log panel when it is open, then the dock and docked chat, which shift inward by the width of whatever lies outside them, as the toolbar shifts for the minimap today. A library's docked devtools panel keeps its edge (`SURF-1`). Below 900 CSS pixels wide, docked chat and the pinned log panel never sit side by side: the one opened earlier folds to its bar control (**Chat** keeps the turn's state, **Logs** its count of new lines) and returns as it was, with its draft, scroll and filter, when the later one closes; folding is not closing, so docked chat still closes only by its own **Close** (`KEY-6`, `O8`; revision 7).
- **SURF-6 Docked chat keeps its composer and decisions in view.** In every chat state (compact, expanded, dragged, after a window resize) and at every acceptance viewport, the panel's header, the composer, Send, the edit mode control (`O1`) and any pending approval or question are inside the viewport: a moved or expanded panel sizes itself to the room it has or moves back into view. A pending approval or question is a card pinned between the conversation and the composer, outside any scroll area, with every answer visible without scrolling in compact chat at 1280x800. A dragged panel never covers the bar's row.
- **SURF-7 Placement is the person's choice; the manifest sets the default.** The toolbar's corner (`[devtools.status].position`) is the team default. A person moves the toolbar by dragging the bar to another corner or to bottom centre, or with **Move to** in the stack name menu, and collapses it; **Reset to Manifest Default** returns both. The control server saves the person's toolbar placement, its collapsed state and chat's dragged position per manifest in devhost's state directory, never in the manifest and never in browser storage, which is separate per hostname, so the layout is the same on every hostname of the stack and a teammate still gets the manifest's default. Saving and reading the layout never depends on chat being on, and a serve session keeps its layout per served folder the same way. devhost's own temporary move above a docked library panel (`SURF-1`) never changes the saved choice. Contract: `CC-5`.
- **SURF-8 Collapsing or dismissing never hides a pending state.** A collapsed toolbar hides its segments, never a pending condition: every actionable notice stays visible in a one-line form beside the collapsed badge, and the badge shows the stack's whole attention state (2.10), not service health alone. The Chat button and the running agents trigger show an agent that waits for the person as a state distinct from working, in their look and their accessible name. Dismissing a notice whose condition persists leaves a marker on the control that owns the condition until the condition ends: a dismissed **Reload blocked** leaves a held marker with its count on **Hold Reloads** until a reload clears it. A dismissed rejection's marker sits on **Config** while the config editor is on, else on the stack name, which is always on the bar, as does a dismissed untrusted-authority condition's (`ACT-7`); a marker appears only once its notice is dismissed, because while the notice shows it is the one report (`FAIL-4`) (revision 7). Watch Live Activity and the stack directory's cards show an agent waiting for an answer until it is answered (`ACT-6`).
- **SURF-9 Contained surfaces and acceptance viewports.** Every surface keeps its header and its actions (Close, Revert, Save, Send, a notice's buttons) in view whatever its body holds; only the body scrolls, and long paths and tokens wrap without horizontal overflow. A body devhost cannot read (an HTML document where it expected JSON) is never shown as markup (2.13). Completions, menus and popovers open inside the viewport and are never clipped by their surface. The acceptance viewports are 1600x1000, 1280x800 and 900x900 CSS pixels, in the light and the dark theme, and 1280x800 at 200% zoom (640x400 CSS pixels, WCAG 1.4.4); at each, every surface fits the viewport with every action reachable and no text clipped, and the bar keeps one row with 40 services (`SURF-10`); full pages reflow at 320 CSS pixels wide without horizontal scrolling (WCAG 1.4.10). A page element that takes focus is never entirely hidden by a devhost surface (WCAG 2.4.11): the dock and docked chat step aside to the other end of their edge while it has focus, a temporary move like `SURF-1`'s that is never saved, and the person hides the pinned log panel from the page without moving focus (Alt+Ctrl+O, `KEY-12`). Every pointer target is at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels or has the spacing WCAG 2.5.8 accepts instead, a trigger's dots counting as one target. Narrower windows follow `O8` (decided 2026-11-18: chat, approval prompts and notices also get a phone layout down to 320 CSS pixels).
- **SURF-10 A panel that outgrows a popover changes form.** The Services trigger shows one dot per service while the dots fit on the bar's single row at the current window width; when they would wrap the bar, it shows a fixed-width summary instead (its counts follow 2.10 and 2.12). When a toolbar panel's rows overflow its height, it gains a filter field focused on open (`SEL-10`), keeps group headers with their counts (services group by service group or include file), pins the rows that need attention above the rest, and keeps its panel-level actions in a footer outside the scrolling area. The in-use popover shows one **Open Stack** action, with the other routes under a disclosure. The thresholds are this fit test, not a service count, so the same rule holds at every viewport.
- **SURF-11 devhost takes page input only where it draws.** Only the visible bar, notice cards, open panels and docked surfaces take pointer input; the empty part of the toolbar's box passes clicks and picks to the page. While the page's own modal dialog is open (section 1, scripted ones included), the notice stack and the announcer (`FB-9`) move into that dialog, as chat's mount already does for a native one, so its buttons work and its reports are heard, and the rest of the toolbar shows an explicit paused state whose reason is one line in the moved notice stack ("Paused while this page's dialog is open"), because the paused bar is inert and exposes no name. A popover the page opens never covers an open devtools panel, which stays anchored. devhost's own modal surfaces (the crash view, the Configuration window, the crop preview) and its menus, the source menu included, open above the page's dialog in the top layer and move into it as the notice stack does; the page's dialog is inert behind a devhost modal and active again when it closes (revision 7). Contract: `CC-3`.
- **SURF-12 Marks stay on what they mark.** Every mark devhost draws on page content (selection markers, drawings, source-highlight outlines) is anchored to the element it marks as an offset to that element, and is re-measured on scroll, resize, layout change and DOM mutation, including the re-render of a hot update. When its element leaves the page, the mark says the element is gone instead of floating over another element.
- **SURF-13 Logs are read in a pinned panel; the minimap is a peek.** The minimap's hover preview stays a peek. A person reads service output in the pinned log panel, a docked panel (`SURF-5`) opened by clicking the strip, by a **Logs** control on the bar or by its key (2.11): resizable, scrollable through every retained line, selectable and copyable, filtered by service (every service by default, `SEL-3`), searchable, and reachable and closable by keyboard. Its lines are one focusable scrolling element that is never a live region (`role="log"` would read a busy server aloud): new lines are never announced, and **Jump to Latest** carries the count of new lines; its resize handle is a focusable separator (`role="separator"` with its value) that the arrows resize, Home and End jump, and a click steps (WCAG 2.1.1, 2.5.7). The recovery view's logs (`SURF-14`) follow the same rule. A rejected reload's notice keeps the failing service's captured output for as long as the notice shows. The crash view and the recovery page open their logs at the end, with the exit summary pinned above them, from the shared recovery view (`SURF-14`).
- **SURF-14 One component for one state on both page types.** When a status page and the toolbar show the same state, both render it with one shared component and the same words: the worktree list (`SEL-4`), the recovery view (its cause sentence, its actions per `MENU-5` and its log rendering) and the service state (2.10). The component ships in both style entries, the status pages' document stylesheet and the devtools Shadow DOM stylesheet, because the two page types keep separate stylesheets by contract.
- **SURF-15 Every surface leads to the next one.** The terminal hands off by printing URLs, because devhost never opens a browser: `devhost start` and `devhost stack list` print the stack directory's URL, which the toolbar's stack name menu and the Caddy 404 page also link. **Edit** goes to the file and line of the problem in the Configuration window (`MENU-4`); with the config editor off, the surface shows the file and line with **Copy** (editor integration is removed under `O51`). A service row links only to an address that answers (its page route, else its health URL) and otherwise shows the route as copyable text. Every lifecycle action a browser surface offers also has a terminal command (`MENU-6`). Chat always edits the active worktree in place, so no finished turn leads to a worktree switch (`O1`, `UNDO-5`). Links open in the same tab, except Watch Live Activity, which opens in its own tab on `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` on this machine and on the stack directory's hostname for this machine and paired browsers, never on an app's hostname (`O55`, decided 2026-11-18).
- **SURF-16 When the app answered, the page shows the app's answer.** A page load that the app itself answers with a 404 or a 5xx shows the app's own document, with the toolbar injected and a notice naming who answered (the service and its worktree) and the status, with **Retry** and, when worktrees are on, a collapsed "Is this route in another worktree?" holding the worktree list (`SEL-4`). devhost never re-requests that URL on a timer, because each request runs the failing route again: it reloads the page once per sign that the answer may have changed (the page's service restarted or became healthy, or a watched file of it changed, `ACT-8`), and that reload is held like any other while **Hold Reloads** is on. devhost's recovery page replaces only answers devhost produced and answers for a service that is not running: no route, not started, starting, exited, blocked by its worktree, an unreachable external service, or the proxy's own 502. devhost's file server answers a missing file with its own 404 inside the file browser (breadcrumbs, the nearest listing, close names), never with the recovery page. A request that is not a page load gets the app's own response; when the app cannot answer it because its service is not running, Caddy hands the request to devhost, which answers a short status (503 with `Retry-After` and a body naming the service and its phase) instead of Caddy's empty 502. When the app answers a page load with an empty body or a body that is not HTML, which the toolbar cannot be injected into, devhost wraps it in its own document showing the status and the body verbatim (revision 7). This is `O5` (decided 2026-11-18). Contract: `CC-4`.

Rationale: The WAI-ARIA dialog pattern makes everything outside a modal dialog inert, so devhost uses a modal only where the page cannot be used anyway (the crash view) or where focus must not wander into the page (the Configuration window), and carries the context such a dialog needs inside it. VS Code's docked panes, status bar and notifications never overlap one another; browser DevTools' docked panes do not either. Vite and Next.js keep the server's response and draw their overlay over the app's page, the Next.js dev overlay collapses into a badge that reopens it, and Laravel Herd and Valet pass an app's own error pages through. The Next.js dev indicator, TanStack Devtools and the Vercel toolbar let each person move the toolbar; VS Code separates user settings from workspace settings. Tilt prints its web UI's URL when it starts. Project contracts: the devtools stay visually isolated from the host page; Restart Stack stays in both Services and the crash recovery dialog; chat's position is saved on the server per manifest because browser storage is separate per hostname (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`); devhost never opens a browser (owner rule) and Watch Live Activity stays off every app's hostname (`O55`). Findings: the corner, `notices-and-feedback-03`, `chat-compose-04`, `config-editor-11`, `reload-and-agents-05`, `third-party-devtools-01`; the crash view, `startup-recovery-01`, `journey-crash-to-fix-01`, `error-pages-02`, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-01`; the Configuration window, `config-editor-10`, `config-editor-09`, `journey-crash-to-fix-02`; docked chat, `chat-compose-02`, `journey-agent-fix-02`; placement, `toolbar-shell-02`; collapsed and dismissed states, `toolbar-shell-01`, `chat-approvals-03`, `reload-and-agents-04`, `reload-and-agents-06`; scale, `stack-scale-04`, `stack-scale-05`, `worktree-switch-07`; containment, `notices-and-feedback-01`, `manifest-hot-reload-02`, `config-editor-04`; the host page, `page-context-03`, `toolbar-shell-07`; marks, `page-context-02`, `source-and-minimap-08`; logs, `source-and-minimap-07`, `journey-crash-to-fix-03`, `stack-scale-03`, `chat-conversations-05`; one component, `worktree-switch-03`; hand-offs, `stack-directory-06`, `services-panel-02`, `agent-cli-journey-03`; the app's answer, `error-pages-03`, `error-pages-04`, `error-pages-05`, `serve-folder-06`, `agent-cli-journey-04`. The fit test in `SURF-10` replaces the findings' "about 8" and "about 10" services, which no measurement supports.

Use it for every devhost surface in the browser and for the terminal's two output contracts. Do not use it for the app's own UI, for a library's devtools panel beyond where devhost places itself around it, or for Watch Live Activity's content, which agent-chat and running-agents own.

States: the dock alone; the dock with one to many notices; a toolbar panel open, with a nested popover or an in-place confirmation; docked chat compact, expanded, dragged and re-fitted after a resize, with a pending decision card; the pinned log panel open beside docked chat; the Configuration window open with no banner, with an exited service's banner showing its last lines and then all its output, and with a rejected reload's banner; the crash view open, minimized to its notice, opened minimized while the person types or chat is in use, and reopened by the next exit; a library panel docked at the bottom with the dock moved above it; the toolbar collapsed with pending notices; the toolbar paused under the page's native modal dialog and under a scripted one (a Radix Dialog fixture); a failure arriving while the Configuration window is open; the page's own 404 or 5xx with devhost's notice; a recovery page; the file server's own 404.

Keys: Escape closes or minimizes the topmost transient devhost surface and never answers a question or dismisses a failure; **Move to** and every panel are reachable without a pointer; the pinned log panel has a key. See 2.11.

Accessibility: Toolbar panels and anchored popovers are non-modal and labelled by their trigger; focus moves into them on open and returns to the trigger on close. The crash view is a modal dialog whose accessible description is the cause sentence; when it opens expanded, focus moves to its heading (`tabindex="-1"`), with the lead action as the next Tab stop, and returns, on minimize or close, to the control focused before it opened, else the Services trigger; opened minimized, its crash notice is announced once as a failure (`FB-9`). The crash notice carries its state in its accessible name; the paused toolbar's reason is a line in the moved notice stack (`SURF-11`). The Configuration window is a modal dialog labelled by its title; focus moves to the file's text on open and returns to the control that opened it. Docked chat is a non-modal region labelled "Chat"; the pinned log panel is a region named by its filter whose lines are never a live region (`SURF-13`); a decision card that arrives is announced as 2.7 says, without moving focus out of the composer. Every surface keeps the devtools contrast contract (4.5:1 text, 3:1 control boundaries, both themes) and forced-colors rendering.

Backend contract: a layout route of the control server, `/__devhost__/layout` (`GET` and `PUT`, with an `Origin` required for `PUT` as on today's panel route), answers whether chat is on or off and keeps the toolbar's corner, its collapsed state and chat's dragged position per manifest, or per served folder for a serve session, in the state directory; it replaces `/__devhost__/chat/panel`, which answers 404 while chat is off and keeps nothing without a manifest (`SURF-7`). The document injection stops replacing a page load the app answered with a 404 or 5xx, keeps the app's body and status, and tells the injected toolbar who answered (service, worktree, status) (`SURF-16`); each host route (`apps/devhost/src/caddy/routes.rs`) gains a `handle_errors` for requests that are not page loads, which passes a request the app could not answer to the service's document injection server, and that server answers 503 with `Retry-After` and a short body naming the service and its phase; a running app's own responses, errors included, still pass through untouched (`SURF-16`, `CC-4`). The crash view's and the recovery page's shared view needs the exit summary and the cause from the published service state (2.10). **Edit** entry points and file-and-line reports need the structured error of `MENU-4`.

Component primitive: the native Popover API with CSS anchor positioning (`ToolbarPopover`) for toolbar panels, nested popovers and anchored confirmations; a native `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()` for the crash view and the Configuration window; `FloatingSurface` and the existing chat placement hook for docked chat and the pinned log panel; one shared recovery view and one shared worktree list under `packages/devhost-ui/src/`, rendered by both entries; library work: none in `@alexgorbatchev/shadcn`, `@base-ui/react` or `@radix-ui/react-dropdown-menu`.

Contract change: `CC-1` (toolbar panels paint above docked chat), `CC-2` (the crash view can be minimized and never opens over devhost's own status page for the same failure), `CC-3` (what the toolbar does above the page's modal dialogs), `CC-4` (the app's own 404 and 5xx documents), `CC-5` (toolbar placement and collapse are the person's); `CC-20` (reversible dismissals, from 2.9); roster TB-02, TB-06, REC-03, REC-04, REC-05, LOG-01, LOG-02.

### 2.5 Review before write

devhost keeps no plans in the browser. A person reviews a write by reading the reach its control names (2.1), by answering a question when the write asks one, or by editing a request before an agent receives it; the terminal prints the set first and offers `--dry-run` (`SEL-6`); the backend checks what it can before it stops anything. This section decides which writes ask, the one form every question takes, and what shows before a chat message, a request devhost writes for an agent, or an answer to an agent leaves the page.
