Rules:

- **REV-1 Which writes ask.** A write asks first only as this table says; every other write runs on one press after its control has named its reach (`SEL-1`, `SEL-5`, `SEL-11`), and reports its outcome (2.7).

  | Write | Asks |
  | --- | --- |
  | Restart one service, Restart Changed and its key, Start and starting every not-started service, Restart Stack in the crash view or on a recovery page, Retry of a failed switch, Stop Turn, Close Conversation, a chat Send, a save from the Configuration window or any editor | never, unless it would end, hide or discard other work (`REV-3`; a start or restart ends no agent's work); a Send also asks when its draft cites a mark that is gone (`REV-6`) |
  | Restart Stack in the Services panel, Return to Configured Worktree | every time, in place (`REV-2`) |
  | Switch and Restart Stack after picking a worktree | only for other work (`REV-3`); picking is the first step (`REV-2`) |
  | Stop Stack on another stack's worktree | every time, in place in the in use popover or the owner card |
  | `devhost stop --worktrees`, `devhost restart-all`, `devhost upgrade` | only when the set would end an agent's turn: `[y/N]` in a terminal, refused without one or under `AGENT=1`, unless `--yes` (`REV-7`, `O18`, `O33`) |
  | Disable Reloads | every time: the editable request is the question (`REV-5`, `O2`) |
  | `devhost caddy trust`, `trust-remote`, `untrust`, the trust step of a Caddy start | every time, and only a person at a terminal can answer (`REV-8`) |

- **REV-2 A stack-wide write takes two deliberate steps.** A write that stops or restarts every managed service of a stack runs only after a second step that states its reach: the number of services, that automatic ports change, the worktree it runs from and how long it took last time. A single control (Restart Stack in the Services panel, where it sits near Restart Changed, and Return to Configured Worktree) expands in place into that statement on its first press; in the crash view and on a recovery page, where the page's service is already down and Restart Stack is the remedy on offer, it runs on one press with that statement as its reach line (`SEL-1`), and a Retry of a failed switch runs on one press naming the same target; a worktree switch's first step is picking a worktree, and the switch button, carrying its reach line (`SEL-5`), is the second. Enter in the worktree list's find field highlights a row and never starts a switch. The choice that changes nothing takes the place of the pressed control, so a double click or a double press cancels. Example: on a stack of 6 managed services (web, api, docs, worker, admin and mail) whose last Restart Stack took 14 s, the first press shows "Restart all 6 services with new automatic ports? Took 14 s last time." with **Restart Stack** and **Cancel**, and the question's accessible description names the six (`REV-4`; exact words per 2.12).
- **REV-3 Other work is named, and the write asks for it.** Before a write ends, hides or discards work in progress that is not its own target, its control names each item, and the write asks while any exists: an agent working or waiting for the person in a stack the write stops or replaces, or in a worktree a switch or Return to Configured Worktree moves the page away from (starting or restarting services ends nothing of an agent's, Restart Stack included, whose own `REV-2` step stays); a message in **This worktree (live)** that would start a conversation's agent in a worktree where an agent chat did not start works or waits (in **Make tickets** the composer names that agent and sends without asking); unsaved Configuration window drafts the write would discard or put out of reach; the page state **Hold Reloads** protects, when the write reloads the page anyway; page context that will not survive the reload (`UNDO-2`). The question's first choice leads to the work (**Answer First** opens the pending request; **Review Drafts** opens the Configuration window), and for a second agent it is **Cancel**, which keeps the draft. While the agent scan is incomplete, the second-agent question asks too, naming the agents devhost could not check, instead of sending as if none ran (revision 7). An action whose own target is the work (Stop Turn, Close Conversation, Cancel) never asks and is recoverable instead (`UNDO-4`). A write made outside devhost's UI cannot ask; its outcome names what it ended (2.7). Example: while Claude Code works in this worktree, Alt+Ctrl+R restarts the changed `api` and `web` without a question, and the outcome names them.
- **REV-4 One form for every question.** A question is one sentence naming the verb, its targets as `SEL-1` names a set (by name when one or two, otherwise by count and unit, with every member in the question's accessible description), the consequence and what will be lost; its buttons are verbs that name the action (**Restart Stack**, **Stop Acme Shop**, **Switch Anyway**), never OK or Yes. Initial focus goes to the choice that changes nothing, which takes Enter; the write is never the default. Escape cancels a question and returns focus to the control that asked (`KEY-8`): an in-place question leaves its surface open, and a popover that holds only the question closes. A click elsewhere in the question's surface cancels it the same way, unless the click lands on another control, which takes focus and acts. Whatever closes that surface (a click outside it, Tab past its last control or Shift+Tab before its first, `KEY-7`) cancels the question with it, and focus goes where that close sends it (`KEY-6`). Example: with Restart Stack's question open in the Services panel, Escape or a click on the panel's heading cancels it and focuses **Restart Stack**; a click on **Restart api** cancels it and restarts api; a click on the page closes the panel and the question. A question appears where its control is (`SURF-2`), never in a modal dialog, an outcome notice or `confirm()`, and after the person confirms, the same place shows the progress and the outcome (2.7): the confirm button stays in its place as the busy control and keeps focus (`aria-busy`, `aria-disabled`, its label naming what runs, "Restarting Stack · 2 of 6 started"), each target's progress shows beside it, and the outcome that replaces it takes focus on its sentence, never on an action (`FB-9`, `KEY-11`). In the terminal the set prints first and the question ends in `[y/N]`: an empty answer, end of input or anything but y or yes is No.
- **REV-5 Disable Reloads shows its request before an agent gets it.** As `O2` decides, pressing **Disable Reloads** sends nothing: it opens a popover anchored to the hot reload notice (`SURF-2`) naming the agent that will receive the request (harness, model, and the conversation it joins or that it starts one), the worktree it edits (`SEL-4`) and the request itself, written for the detected dev server and editable. Its edit mode is **This worktree (live)**, shown and fixed: no worktree creation, commit or merge (`O1`). Focus starts in the request, where Enter inserts a line break; only **Send** sends the text as edited; **Cancel** and Escape send nothing and leave no conversation or process behind; **Hold Reloads** is offered as the way without an agent. The visible label stays **Disable Reloads**, and its accessible description says an agent receives the request. When no agent can receive it, the popover still shows the request with **Copy**, and Send is unavailable with its reason (`HOLD-5`). The request starts a new conversation unless the person picks an open one the popover names (`O27`, decided 2026-11-18); for a dev server with no verified switch the popover says so and offers **Hold Reloads** first (`O25`, decided 2026-11-18); the request may change a service's `command` only when the switch is a command-line flag, never `watch` (`O26`, decided 2026-11-18). agent-chat owns the strings the popover shares with the composer (the approval line, the second-agent question, `HOLD-5`'s reasons), and the popover quotes them; the reloads-disabled mark and a pending request live on the server with the request's record, never in browser storage, so a browser in another environment cannot reload past them (`O56`, `CC-45`; revision 7).
- **REV-6 A chat Send never asks; the composer shows how the message is handled.** Before Send the composer shows, as state the person can change: the recipient (`SEL-8`); the edit mode and the worktree it edits (`O1`, `O3`, `SEL-8`), with the instructions devhost adds, the team's `prompt` first and then the mode's own (`O35`, decided 2026-11-18), behind a disclosure and a line when it differs from the previous message's mode (`O20`, decided 2026-11-18); whether the agent asks before commands and edits, as its CLI is configured (a warning style when it never asks; `O13`, decided 2026-11-18); the page context it carries, item by item (`SEL-9`); and during a turn, that the message joins the running turn without interrupting it. Each sent message records its mode and its page context as sent. Only two questions can stand before a Send: a second agent in **This worktree (live)** (`REV-3`), asked when the conversation's agent would start, never again for its follow-ups while the same other agent works, and a mark the draft cites that no longer exists (flagged per `SEL-9`), which asks with **Select Again** first and **Send Anyway**. During a turn a message goes into the running turn at once, as both agents take it (`reference/chat-api.md`), so only a message still waiting on this page for an earlier send's acknowledgement can be taken back into the composer; once the agent's CLI has it, only **Stop Turn** cancels it, with the turn (`UNDO-4`).
- **REV-7 A multi-stack command asks only when it would end an agent's turn.** A command that acts on more than one stack or takes every stack offline (`devhost stop --worktrees`, `devhost restart-all`, `devhost upgrade`'s stack-replacement step, `devhost caddy stop` while any stack runs) prints its set (`SEL-6`) with the chat conversations each stack will end, then acts, unless the set would end an agent's turn that works or waits for the person (`REV-3`): then it asks `[y/N]` naming those turns when standard input and output are both a terminal and `CI` is not set, and otherwise, or under `AGENT=1`, it refuses, exits non-zero and prints one line telling the agent to ask the person, as `REV-8` does. `--dry-run` prints the set and exits 0 without acting. A script or installer meets the question, or the refusal, only while a turn would end; one started from a person's terminal can meet the question there, so the documented upgrade step tells scripts to pass `--yes` (`CC-12`); with no turn in progress `devhost restart-all` runs unattended, and `devhost caddy stop`, which ends no turn, never asks. `--yes` is the one flag that skips a devhost question; no command takes `--force` for it. A command acting on the one stack it resolved (`devhost stop`, project-scoped `devhost stack restart`) does not ask, and its outcome names the conversations it ended (2.7). This is `O33` (decided 2026-11-18) and `O18` (updated 2026-11-25). Contract: `CC-12`.
- **REV-8 Only a person changes the machine's certificate trust.** `devhost caddy trust`, `trust-remote`, `untrust` and every other step that asks for a password, `devhost caddy privileged-ports` included (revision 7), refuse under `AGENT=1` before any `sudo`, with one line telling the agent to ask the person to run the command in a terminal, and the trust step of a Caddy start runs only when a person can answer, as today. `trust-remote` prints the certificate's subject, its expiry and the SHA-256 fingerprint of the DER certificate, then asks `[y/N]` (`--yes` skips it). `trust-remote`'s SSH target is the host its argument names, never `machineName`, which only labels (`O61`, revision 7). Every install prints the root's name, its fingerprint and the command that removes it (`UNDO-5`). Every agent devhost starts runs with `AGENT=1`, so these refusals reach chat agents too. Contract: `CC-13`.
- **REV-9 The person answers an agent's approval or question; devhost never does.** devhost relays every approval and question and never answers or pre-approves one (owner rule). The decision card (`SURF-6`) leads with a plain question naming the tool and its target ("Edit header.tsx?"), then what will change (a worktree-relative path and a unified diff for an edit; the exact command, its working directory and the agent's stated reason for a command), then **Deny**, **Allow Once**, the agent CLI's own standing approval (such as Claude Code's "don't ask again" or Codex's approval for the session, when the CLI offers one, `O12`, reversed 2026-11-18) and **Stop Turn**, with the native request collapsed under Details. The person, never devhost, grants a standing approval through the CLI's own option; later matching requests are then answered by the CLI without prompting or a card. No answer is a default: an arriving card never takes focus from a field being typed in, and the card is one Tab stop: Tab or Shift+Tab into it, from the composer included, focuses its question (named by the question, described by the change), the arrow keys move to Details, **Deny**, **Allow Once**, the standing-approval option and **Stop Turn**, and Tab leaves the card. **Deny** can carry a note for the agent: Claude Code receives it as the denial's message, and Codex, whose answers carry no text, receives the decline and then the note as a message into the running turn, shown in the conversation as the person's message with its delivery state and without the instructions devhost adds (`REV-6`, `REV-12`); an empty note denies at once. Several pending requests show their count and are answered in arrival order. Each answer leaves a line where it was given ("You denied: Edit header.tsx · 13:23") that survives reloads, and a denied call never shows as done. The card is the one place to answer: every waiting marker (`SURF-8`), a waiting agent on a stack directory card (`ACT-6`) and a waiting conversation of another worktree (`SEL-8`) lead to it in one activation; Watch Live Activity shows the wait and cannot answer. Contract: `CC-16`, `CC-38`.
- **REV-10 A hot reload is reported, never reviewed.** A save from any editor applies through hot reload without a question (owner rule), except for `REV-3`'s other work: a save that turns the config editor off while other files hold drafts. Beside **Save**, the Configuration window states what the draft will do, from the backend's check of it: each service it restarts and why (changed, depends on a changed service, same repository), each service it starts or stops, and for the devhost config file the stacks whose URLs move; "Restarts nothing" when nothing restarts. It also states the save's other effects, a return to the configured worktree and the config editor turning itself off, and names the page's own service first when the save restarts it. These are statements beside **Save**, which stays available, never a question, except for `REV-3`'s other work. Every reload, saved from any editor, reports what it did (2.7).
- **REV-11 Check everything checkable before stopping anything.** A write that restarts services (a reload, Restart Stack, a switch, a start) runs every check it can before it stops a service: manifest validation, port and hostname claims, a probe of each new fixed port on its bind host naming the program that holds it, the switch target (registered, available, manifest loads, service folders exist), and the other stack's identity before every signal, as today. A failed check refuses the write with nothing stopped and names the check and what holds it (2.8). A service that was failing before the write and is unchanged by it is never a condition of the write's success (`UNDO-1`).
- **REV-12 One request, one operation, never replayed.** devhost never resends a write whose outcome it does not know: a chat message shows **Delivery unconfirmed** (owner rule), and a restart, switch or save whose response was lost takes its outcome from its record, found by its operation ID (`ACT-1`), instead of posting again, and a request repeated with an ID the server already holds returns that record and runs nothing again. A request made while an operation runs is never dropped silently: a start or restart of another service queues in the services runtime and shows queued on every surface (`SEL-7`); a restart of a service that is already restarting, or a stack-wide write while another operation runs, is refused with its reason (`HOLD-3`); a newer accepted manifest supersedes the reload in progress, which then announces nothing of the replaced edit, and one line says so; a Configuration window save made during a reload waits for it, is reported with its own reload ("Replaced by your next save" when a later save supersedes it) and keeps its outcome with its file; it resolves from the file versions each finished reload read: applied or rejected when a finished reload read its version, replaced when a later version was read first. A restart of a service in a running reload's set that the reload has not reached yet queues behind it with **Cancel Restart**, and once the reload restarts it the refusal above applies; a second Configuration window save while one applies waits behind it and says so, never disabled; no GET request changes state, and routes only devhost's own pages read live under `/__devhost__/`, documented as internal (revision 7). `devhost stop` during a start cancels the start and says so.
- **REV-13 A stale view never acts.** The server keeps refusing writes based on stale state: a save whose base version differs from the file, a signal to a stack whose identity changed, a switch to a target no longer available, an answer to a request already answered or cancelled. The page shows such a refusal with the current state and keeps the person's draft (`UNDO-3`). An open question re-reads what it names when that changes, and closes with its reason when its action becomes unavailable (`HOLD-1`).

Rationale: The line most tools draw is to ask when an action reaches beyond what the person is looking at or ends work they cannot get back, and to act at once otherwise: Docker Desktop, Tilt and IDE run panels restart on one click and ask before deleting; Git clients ask before a checkout only when local changes would be lost or carried; JetBrains asks "Stop and Rerun" before replacing a single-instance run. Terraform, `docker system prune` and `git clean` print their set before shared changes and take `-auto-approve`, `-f` or `-n`; ssh shows a fingerprint before trusting a key; mkcert and Valet install a root only on an explicit command and document removing it. Apple's Human Interface Guidelines ask for verb buttons and never a destructive default. Claude Code, Codex CLI and Cursor show the diff or command above the choice, let a refusal carry an instruction, and keep the mode and model in the input row without confirming each send; Cursor's Add to Chat puts text into the composer without sending it. Vite, Next.js and Tilt apply a config save at once and report what restarted; Docker Compose and Kubernetes never stop healthy services because an unrelated one fails; Terraform and Helm refuse an overlapping run with a reason, and ETag-style versions refuse a stale write, as the config save already does (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/config_files.rs:288-295`). Today the only question anywhere is chat's running-agent prompt, which makes the risky choice primary (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/agentChat/components/ChatDecisionPrompt.tsx:21-55`); Restart Stack, Stop Stack, switches and Disable Reloads act on one click. Owner rules and decisions: devhost never answers or pre-approves; it never resends a chat message; settings apply through hot reload; `O1` and `O2`. Findings: `services-panel-2-03`, `error-pages-01` (Restart Stack); `chat-checkout-switch-02`, `worktree-switch-03`, `worktree-switch-06`, `worktree-switch-07` (switches and Stop Stack); `stack-run-stop-07`, `notices-and-feedback-04`, `first-run-setup-03`, `first-run-setup-04`, `agent-cli-journey-06` (the terminal); `config-editor-05`, `config-editor-08`, `manifest-hot-reload-01`, `manifest-hot-reload-05`, `proxy-settings-04` (saves and reloads); `reload-and-agents-01` (Disable Reloads); `journey-agent-fix-01`, `chat-approvals-06`, `chat-compose-01`, `chat-checkout-switch-04`, `chat-conversations-08`, `chat-compose-2-02`, `page-context-07`, `journey-agent-fix-04` (before Send); `chat-approvals-01`, `chat-approvals-02`, `chat-approvals-05`, `chat-approvals-07`, `chat-approvals-03`, `chat-checkout-switch-01` (answers); `chat-approvals-04`, `chat-conversations-02`, `chat-compose-2-03` (chat controls); `interrupted-operations-05`, `config-editor-12`, `config-editor-13`, `manifest-hot-reload-2-05`, `services-panel-2-02`, `chat-conversations-09` (overlapping requests); `notices-and-feedback-02`, `worktree-switch-05`, `worktree-switch-09`, `config-editor-07`, `stack-directory-07` (stale views).

Use it for every write a person starts in devhost, in the browser and the terminal, and for every request or answer that reaches an agent. Do not use it for what blocks a write (2.6), for progress and outcomes (2.7, 2.8), or for the target set a control names (2.1).

States: a write that runs at once; Restart Stack's first press in the Services panel expanded into its question, then progress in the same place, and its one press in the crash view; a switch with no other work, and with an agent waiting, unsaved drafts, held reloads and page context named; Stop Stack's question; the second-agent question with the draft kept; a multi-stack command acting at once, asking because a turn would end, refused without a terminal and under `AGENT=1`, with `--yes` and with `--dry-run`; a trust command refused under `AGENT=1`; the Disable Reloads popover with the request edited, with Send unavailable and its reason, and after Cancel; the composer before a first Send, during a turn, with page context and with a flagged mark; a decision card with a diff, a command, a question, several pending, a note on Deny, and the line an answer leaves; the card reached from the composer with Shift+Tab, its question focused; Save with its reach and with "Restarts nothing"; a refused stale save with the draft kept; a queued restart, a refused duplicate restart and a superseded reload; focus after confirming a question, on the busy confirm button and then on the outcome's sentence.

Keys: Enter takes a question's safe choice and Escape cancels it; Enter in a find field never starts a switch; Enter in the Disable Reloads request inserts a line break; the popover's **Send**, **Deny**, **Allow Once** and the CLI's standing-approval option have no key of their own, while Enter sends from the composer and denies from a Deny note the person typed (`KEY-9`). See 2.11.

Accessibility: an in-place question is a group labelled by its sentence and, when the sentence counts its targets, described by their names (`REV-4`), with focus moved to its safe choice and returned to the control that asked when Escape, **Cancel** or a click elsewhere in its surface cancels it, else sent where its surface's close sends it (`KEY-6`); the Disable Reloads popover is a non-modal dialog named for the agent that receives the request; a decision card is a group named by its question, one Tab stop entered on that question, announced once when it arrives without moving focus (2.7); terminal questions are plain text on one line; every answer's record is text.

Backend contract: the configuration check returns what a draft would do (the restart set with each reason, starts, stops, moved URLs), for the Configuration window and `devhost reload --dry-run` alike (`CC-37`), which `Runtime::check_config` cannot today because the set is computed only inside the reload (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:217`, `apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:717`); a reload probes new fixed ports before stopping anything and supersedes an older reload (`REV-11`, `REV-12`); one operation record names the operation in progress and its progress, and refuses a stack-wide write while one runs (`HOLD-3`); the approval projection carries the edit's path and diff, the command with its folder and any standing-approval option the agent CLI offers (`O12`), and the session's journal records each answer with its note as one of devhost's record frames (`UNDO-8`, `CC-40`); a Deny note reaches Claude Code as the denial's `message` and Codex as a message sent into the turn after the decline (`turn/steer`, or a new turn when that one has ended), without the workflow instructions; the composer reads the harness's approval behavior; follow-ups keep today's delivery into the running turn; every agent devhost starts runs with `AGENT=1`, which `apps/devhost/src/devtools/chat/process.rs:60-74` does not set today; the CLI gains the question, `--yes` and `--dry-run`, and reads which agent turns work or wait in each stack of a set (`REV-7`), the trust refusals, `trust-remote`'s fingerprint and `devhost caddy untrust`.

Component primitive: in-place questions built from the existing `Button` inside `ToolbarPopover` panels and the status pages' shared components; the Disable Reloads popover as a `ToolbarPopover` anchored to the notice with the composer's text area; the decision card reworked from `ChatApprovalRequest` with `@assistant-ui/react`; terminal questions in `apps/devhost/src/cliout.rs`; library work: none.

Contract change: `CC-12` (terminal questions, `--yes`, `--dry-run`), `CC-13` (certificate trust), `CC-14` (the second-agent question), `CC-15` (Disable Reloads, `O2`), `CC-16` (approvals, `O12`), `CC-17` (Restart Stack and Stop Stack ask); `CC-10` (`O1`), `CC-38` (requests of another worktree's conversation answered from this page, from 2.1), `CC-37` (a repeated operation ID, from 2.7), `CC-40` (answers and modes in the session's journal, from 2.9); roster SVC-04, WT-02, WT-03, STK-05, STK-07, SET-03, SET-04, SET-05, RLD-01, CHAT-03, CHAT-05, CHAT-06, CHAT-07, CHAT-08, CFG-04, CFG-05, CTX-05.

### 2.6 What can block an action

Nothing outside devhost locks a stack; what blocks an action is devhost's own state: (1) the page lost its stack's status stream; (2) another operation runs on the stack (its start or shutdown, Restart Stack, a repository switch, a reload, a queued restart); (3) a repository's selected worktree is unavailable (external services never block worktree switches, `O7`); (4) chat cannot take a message: it is off in the manifest, no agent CLI is installed, or the page's chat session is disconnected; (5) a control a browser cannot use where it is: under `HOLD-8`, every control `O36`'s check gates, on a page opened from another environment by an unauthenticated browser that has neither signed in with the shared passkey nor paired (`HOLD-7`, `O36`, decided 2026-11-25). The page's own modal dialog pauses the toolbar (`SURF-11`), and a feature that is off says how to get what it gives (`MENU-3`). A rejected configuration blocks nothing (`HOLD-6`). How a blocked action looks is `MENU-3`'s; this section says what blocks, the reason, and how the block clears.

Rules:

- **HOLD-1 One reason, where the action is, cleared by the state that caused it.** Every blocked action shows its blocker as visible text (`MENU-3`): one sentence naming what blocks, what it blocks and the way out (a command, a control, or that devhost is retrying), said once in the surface's status line and given to each blocked control as its accessible description. The block clears by itself when the published state that caused it changes, without a page reload, and nothing it blocked is replayed (`REV-12`). Where only the person can know that the condition ended (an external service, a worktree restored on disk), the surface offers **Check Again**. An open question whose action becomes blocked closes and says why (`REV-13`). Two named exceptions (revision 7): the Disable Reloads popover shows its `HOLD-5` reason in place of **Send**, as its own status line; and a third-party launcher's own condition (the library absent, an unsupported version, the person's choice) is the launcher's state, not a block of 2.6: a short state beside its name, with the sentence in its tooltip, its accessible description and its Devtools row.
- **HOLD-2 A lost connection blocks every write and is not an outage.** From the moment the page's status stream closes, every write on the page (Restart, Restart Changed and its key, Start, Restart Stack, switches, Stop Stack, Save, chat Send, Disable Reloads' Send) is unavailable with one reason, and the services keep their last known state drawn as unknown, never as down (2.10). The reason names the cause the backend sent before the stream closed: "Acme Shop stopped. Start it with devhost start in ~/acme" after a stop, "devhost's proxy is not reachable" after `devhost caddy stop`, the new address with **Open** (path and query kept) after an HTTPS port change; otherwise "Lost connection to devhost; retrying". The page retries with back-off and offers **Retry Now**; drafts, page context and queued operations stay (`UNDO-2`, `SEL-7`). Restart Stack keeps the page's stream open, as `stacks/recovery.md` promises.
- **HOLD-3 One stack-wide operation at a time, named while it runs.** A stack-wide write (Restart Stack, a switch, Return to Configured Worktree) is unavailable while any other operation runs on the stack, and the control API refuses it with `409` naming that operation (`CC-37`); a per-service write (Start, Restart, Restart Changed) queues in the services runtime behind a per-service operation or a reload (`SEL-7`), and is unavailable while the stack starts, shuts down or runs a stack-wide operation (`CC-8`). Each reason names the running operation and its progress, for example "Restart Stack is running · 3 of 6 started". A save waits for the reload in progress (`REV-12`). Chat, page context and every read stay available during service operations. In the terminal, a command refused because an operation runs names it and exits non-zero, and `devhost start` keeps waiting for an unsettled start of the same manifest, as today.
- **HOLD-4 An unavailable worktree blocks its repository, never silently, and a read stops nothing.** When a repository's selected worktree is deleted, unregistered or no longer loads, devhost starts none of that repository's services and never falls back to another worktree (owner rule). The repository is blocked, said once as a sentence ("The worktree ~/acme/.worktrees/fix-nav was deleted; its services still run from it"), never a raw error chain; each of its services that does not run shows as blocked, every **Restart** of its services is unavailable with that reason, and the lead actions are returning to the configured worktree or choosing another (`MENU-5`, `SEL-4`). A worktree that cannot be switched to stays listed, disabled, with its reason and remedy on its row. External services (`managed = false`) never block worktree switching and need no warning (`SEL-5`, `O7`, decided 2026-11-25). The block clears when the person switches or the worktree loads again (**Check Again**), and a rejection the missing worktree caused clears with it. devhost notices a deleted worktree when it next reads the worktree list (opening the picker, a status page's first check, **Refresh Worktrees**), as today, but that read stops nothing: processes that still run keep running from the deleted folder until the recovery the person chooses restarts the repository (`REV-1`, `REV-2`) or they exit (`O43`, decided 2026-11-18). A rejected manifest edit never makes its worktree unavailable (`HOLD-6`; `worktree-switch-14`, now fixed on Rust `main` at `190a8bb5`).
- **HOLD-5 Chat that cannot take a message says why, and running conversations stay reachable.** When chat is off in the manifest, no agent CLI is installed, or the chat session is disconnected, every way to send to an agent (Send, Disable Reloads' Send, sending page context) is unavailable with that reason and its way out (the `[devtools.chat]` setting, the CLIs devhost looks for, reconnecting); the draft and page context stay, and Disable Reloads still shows its request with **Copy** and offers **Hold Reloads** (`REV-5`). Turning chat off never strands an agent: conversations already running keep running, as documented, a notice names each with its state, and its approvals can still be answered (`REV-9`) and its turn stopped until it ends. Only new conversations and new messages are refused. Which clients may reach those routes is `O36`'s check (decided 2026-11-25: this machine, browsers signed in with the shared passkey, or paired browsers). Contract: `CC-18`, `CC-38`.
- **HOLD-6 A rejected configuration blocks nothing, and actions say what they run.** While a manifest file or the devhost config file is rejected, the stack keeps running the accepted manifest and the running shared Caddy settings, and every action stays available, as `stacks/recovery.md` documents for Restart Stack. A control that starts services from the running manifest (Restart Stack, Start, Restart, Restart Changed) says so in its reach line and names the change not applied ("uses the accepted manifest; devhost.toml line 12 is not applied", exact words per 2.12). A switch checks the target worktree's own manifest and refuses when it does not load (`REV-11`).
- **HOLD-7 A control a browser cannot use here says so before the person tries it.** Under `HOLD-8`, a browser in another environment uses every control once it signs in with the shared devhost passkey or pairs with a one-time link, as this machine does (`O36`, decided 2026-11-25); until it signs in or pairs, every control the check gates is unavailable with one reason from the moment its surface opens ("Sign in with passkey or pair this browser to use devhost here", the one sign-in sentence on every surface, which a surface that gates one thing may end with what it gates instead, such as "to check stacks from here" (`O47`); revision 7 settles this wording, offering **Sign In with Passkey** when the shared passkey is configured for this domain and how to get a `devhost pair` link; `HOLD-1`), the app itself stays usable and nothing starts. A signed-out page shows only the stack name and this condition notice: no service state, logs, readouts or other notices, because the status and log streams answer only authenticated clients (`O36`); the full pages devhost serves in place of the app (`SURF-16`) still show their status and sentence (revision 7). The config editor stays off by default (owner rule); with it on, an authenticated remote browser reads and saves as this machine does (`O38`, `O54`, decided 2026-11-18), and the files route tells the page whether this client may save. Editor support is removed completely (`O50`, `O51`), so there is no editor-only exception. Revision 3's form of this rule, "A write taken only from this machine says so before the person tries it", kept config saves to this machine and left every other write's reach outside this reference; `HOLD-8` and `O54` replace it.
- **HOLD-8 Using devhost from another environment is a primary use case, so no feature is held to this machine.** The product owner's comment of 2026-11-18, verbatim: “bindhost on macos probably should be fixed... the main usecase of devhost isn't just "on this machine" but "on any machine"... so while you in env A and start devhost, im in env B and can still use the features. So the solution for the remote execution needs to still work with that somehow”. As a rule: a person who starts devhost in one environment (this machine) uses every feature of its pages from a browser in another environment (another machine on the network, a phone, or through devhost's relay). No protection confines a feature to this machine: where a feature can run code, change what runs or read configuration, devhost makes it safe for that browser instead of refusing it. This machine stays trusted: a browser on this machine keeps every feature it has today. On macOS, `bindHost = "127.0.0.1"` is honored from port 1024 upward, and below port 1024 (including default 443) keeps listening on all interfaces with an explicit startup warning naming the fix so existing port-443 setups keep starting (`proxy-settings-01`, `O39`, decided 2026-11-25). It covers every page and route a stack or a serve session serves: chat and its approvals, the Disable Reloads request, the worktree routes, Restart, Start and Restart Stack, the config routes, the stack directory and Watch Live Activity (editor support is removed completely under `O50` and `O51`, so no screen-only editor exception remains). How devhost tells the person's own browser in another environment from every other client is `O36` (decided 2026-11-25: one shared devhost passkey under a single owned parent HTTPS domain with phone support, plus `devhost pair` as fallback); the agent-chat redesign builds it, together with the macOS `bindHost` fix, and no standalone loopback-only fix ships first (owner item a2, section 5.1). Every remote-use decision under this rule (`O36`–`O40`, `O47`, `O54`, `O55`, `O59`, `O61`, and the withdrawal of `O50`/`O51`/`O60`) is now decided. `HOLD-1` to `HOLD-6` are unchanged: a write blocked for another reason is blocked in every environment alike, with the same reason.

Rationale: GitHub's merge box lists every unmet requirement beside its disabled button; VS Code Remote shows "Reconnecting…" and holds the window instead of acting on a dead backend; Figma and Linear show an offline banner rather than marking content broken; Terraform and Helm name the operation that holds the lock. Today devhost blocks without saying so or does not block at all: a lost stream draws every service down and leaves every Restart enabled, whose error is then the routing page's HTML (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/serviceStatusPanel/hooks/useServiceHealth.ts:73-92`, `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/shared/postControlAction.ts:16-29`); the runtime refuses work during start and shutdown with a reason no page shows (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs`, `apps/devhost/src/services/control.rs`); the switch is disabled during any restart with no reason (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/serviceStatusPanel/components/WorktreePicker.tsx:53-60`); the restart key is swallowed while a restart runs (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/components/App.tsx:209-229`); turning chat off makes every chat route answer 404 while its agents keep running (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/chat/transport.rs:46-52`), although `guides/annotations.md` says running conversations keep running. Findings: `services-panel-2-04`, `notices-and-feedback-02`, `journey-new-project-02`, `notices-and-feedback-01`, `proxy-settings-03` (the lost connection); `services-panel-2-02`, `manifest-hot-reload-2-05`, `config-editor-12` (operations in progress); `worktree-switch-02`, `worktree-switch-05`, `worktree-switch-14`, `stack-directory-08`, `manifest-hot-reload-2-03` (worktrees); `chat-approvals-08`, `chat-approvals-03` (chat); `config-editor-08` (drafts kept through a block).

Use it for every condition that makes an existing action unable to run now, in the browser and the terminal. Do not use it for actions that can never apply to a target (`MENU-3`), for questions a write asks (2.5), or for how service and connection states are drawn (2.10).

States: the lost connection with each known cause and with none, retrying and **Retry Now**; each operation in progress with a blocked stack-wide write and a queued per-service write; a repository blocked by a deleted worktree, before and after **Check Again**; chat off with a running and a waiting conversation still reachable; no agent CLI installed; the chat session disconnected with the draft kept; a rejected manifest with Restart Stack's reach line naming the line not applied; a page opened from another environment by an unauthenticated browser (with **Sign In with Passkey** when configured, plus pairing fallback), and the Configuration window in an authenticated remote browser there (`HOLD-7`, `O36`, `O54`).

Keys: a blocked control keeps its place in the tab order and announces its reason; **Retry Now** and **Check Again** are ordinary buttons. See 2.11.

Accessibility: each blocked control exposes its reason as its accessible description, the same text the status line shows; a new block is announced politely once, and its clearing once ("Connected again"); focus never moves when a block starts or ends, and a focused control that becomes blocked keeps focus (2.11).

Backend contract: the status stream sends the reason before it closes for a stop, a proxy stop or a port move, with the new origin for a port move (`HOLD-2`); the control server keeps pages' streams open across Restart Stack (`HOLD-2`); the published state carries the operation in progress with its name and progress and the blocked reason per repository as a sentence (`HOLD-3`, `HOLD-4`); a read of the worktree list publishes a deleted selected worktree as blocked and stops nothing (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/server.rs:374-463`, `apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:489-536`, `apps/devhost/src/services/worktrees.rs:360-370`, `worktree-switch-14` now fixed on Rust `main`, with `O7` removing the external-service switch block); with chat off, the routes a running conversation needs to show, answer and stop stay available until it ends, and no new conversation starts (`HOLD-5`, `CC-38`); the config editor's file listing tells the page whether this client may save (`HOLD-7`); shared-domain WebAuthn passkey verification (`webauthn-rs` 0.6.x) plus `devhost pair` fallback and 30-day idle sessions gate remote requests (`O36`, `O59`).

Component primitive: the status-line text and disabled-with-reason states of `MENU-3`; the existing reconnecting socket (`openReconnectingWebSocket`) with back-off and a manual retry; library work: `Button`'s unavailable state (2.3).

Contract change: `CC-18` (chat off keeps running conversations reachable); `CC-8` (queued starts, from 2.1), `CC-26` (a lost connection is unknown, from 2.10), `CC-37` (`409` for another operation, from 2.3), `CC-38` (running sessions while chat is off, from 2.1); roster TB-07, SVC-02, SVC-03, SVC-04, WT-02, WT-05, CHAT-12, CFG-04, SET-08.

### 2.7 Progress and outcome feedback

devhost reports every operation (section 1) from its record (`ACT-1`): progress while it runs, then one outcome. This section says where both show, what an outcome states and certifies, how long it stays, how reports share the notice stack, what is announced, and how the terminal's two outputs report. What a failure or a partial result says is 2.8's; service and stack states and conditions are 2.10's; exact words are 2.12's.

Rules:

- **FB-1 Progress shows in one place at a time, and always somewhere.** While an operation runs, the surface that started it shows its progress in place while it is open: a panel, the crash view or the Configuration window lists each target with its step (queued, stopping, starting, waiting for its health check with the elapsed time and the timeout, running, failed), updated at least once a second, and the pressed control shows its verb in progress with the elapsed time. Whenever no open surface on the page shows the operation (its surface was closed, or it started in another tab, the terminal, an agent or a file change on disk), the notice stack shows it as one activity line: the operation, its target, the targets done of the total and the elapsed time. The rows it touches show their transitional state on every surface (`ACT-3`). While docked chat is closed, a chat turn's progress shows on the **Chat** button (working, waiting for you, then "reply ready" until chat opens), not as an activity line, because a turn can last minutes; a waiting agent still raises its condition notice (`ACT-7`) (revision 7). Example: Restart Stack confirmed in the Services panel on a stack of 6 managed services, the panel closed 5 s later with 2 services started: the activity line reads "Restart Stack · 2 of 6 started · 5 s" until the outcome replaces it (exact words per 2.12).
- **FB-2 Exactly one outcome, where the progress was.** The outcome replaces the progress in the same place: in the surface that started the operation while it is open (a panel stays open through progress and outcome, `SURF-2`; the crash view, `SURF-3`; beside **Save** in the Configuration window), otherwise as an outcome notice in place of the activity line; never both on one page. The surface that started it shows the outcome in its own place: a panel in its status line, a popover in place of its buttons, docked chat as the turn's summary (revision 7). An operation this page did not start gets an outcome notice only when it failed or its target set included the page's service (`SEL-3`); otherwise its rows and the log (`FB-7`) show it. The outcome of an operation that reloaded the page (a switch, Restart Stack from a recovery page) shows on the page that loads next in the same tab, found in the record by the operation ID the page stored before it reloaded (`ACT-1`); no page greets its load with an older operation's success.
- **FB-3 An outcome states what happened to each target.** It names the operation by the name of the control or command that started it (`MENU-6`), its target set (`SEL-1`), what changed and the time it took. A write that restarted services names each one with its reason (changed, depends on a changed service, same repository) or says that nothing restarted (`REV-10`, `O9`); a stack-wide write adds how many services run of the stack's total and which automatic ports changed; a switch names both worktrees (`SEL-4`). Example: a save of devhost.toml that changes api's command, on a stack of 6 services where worker depends on api, applied in 3.1 s: "Applied devhost.toml: restarted api (changed) and worker (depends on api) · 6 of 6 running · 3.1 s" (exact words per 2.12). Partial results and failures follow 2.8.
- **FB-4 An outcome names the work it ended, discarded or left behind.** When an operation ended, hid or discarded work in progress that was not its target, its outcome names each item: the chat conversations a stop ended and whether one was working, because a stop never waits for a turn to end (each stays listed as ended and can be resumed, `UNDO-4`); agents left working or waiting in the worktree a switch moved away from (still listed and answerable, `SEL-5`); Configuration window drafts a save put out of reach, with the setting that brings the editor back; page context a reload could not restore (`UNDO-2`); a held reload it discarded. This holds for writes that asked first (`REV-3`) and for writes made outside devhost's UI, which could not ask.
- **FB-5 A success certifies the end state, never a step.** An operation reports success only once the end state it names can be observed: a start or restart once each target passed its health check, with no leftover of a killed run answering in its place (`ACT-11`), or, with a `process` health check or none, still runs after the time `O28` sets (1 s, decided 2026-11-18); a save or reload once the reload that read that saved version ended with its services in the states the outcome reports (`REV-12`); a switch once the target worktree's services run (`UNDO-1`); a stop once no process, route or port claim of the stack remains (2.8 otherwise); `devhost serve --port` once the port accepts connections; a devhost config file change once the new listener answers; `devhost caddy start` once Caddy listens. A chat Send never claims a delivery the agent did not confirm (**Delivery unconfirmed**, owner rule). A Disable Reloads request is pending until its conversation's turn ends: the hot reload notice keeps its slot with the request's state ("Request sent to Claude Code · working"), then shows the result in place of **Disable Reloads**: what devhost observed, the dev server's reload client gone or still loaded with the restart the change needs and **Restart** for that service, never a state the page cannot show (`O24`, confirmed 2026-11-18); devhost restarts nothing by itself to apply it. That conversation settles the result whichever one is open (`O2`). Until the end state shows, the operation is in progress (`FB-1`).
- **FB-6 Successes close by themselves; failures stay until handled.** A success outcome notice closes by itself after the time `O14` sets (reading time: 1.5 s plus 0.3 s per word, 4 to 15 s, decided 2026-11-18), never while the pointer or focus is in it or while the page is hidden, and a notice that arrives while the browser window is not focused pauses its countdown until the person next focuses the browser window (reading `document.hasFocus()`, not `blur` alone; notices that arrived while the window was focused keep counting, `O53`, decided 2026-11-18); a success shown in the surface that started it stays until the person leaves that surface. A failure stays, in that surface and as a notice, until the person dismisses it or the same operation succeeds on the same target; status-stream messages, reconnects, closing the surface that started it, switching files and page reloads never remove it, because the record keeps it (`ACT-1`). The **Undo** that replaces a dismissed notice (`UNDO-3`) stays for 4 s (`O14`). A dismissed or closed outcome stays readable in the log (`FB-7`). Every action or link in a notice that closes by itself has an untimed equivalent, named per kind: an outcome's text and links in its log line (`FB-7`), a dismissed hot reload notice in **Show Reload Notice** in the Hold Reloads popover (`UNDO-3`), a switch's worktree link in the Worktrees panel (`UNDO-5`); the notice's accessible description says where it stays ("also in the log"), so its time is never a time limit (WCAG 2.2.1). A failure of an action the page itself runs, such as a third-party launcher that did not open, has no operation record: a page reload clears it and its log line keeps it, the one exception (revision 7).
- **FB-7 The stack's log is the record of past operations.** Every operation's outcome, from any origin, is one line in the stack's log with its time and its origin when that was not the page reading it ("from another tab", "from the terminal", "from an agent", "after devhost.toml changed on disk", `ACT-1`; exact words per 2.12), and the pinned log panel (`SURF-13`) offers devhost's own lines as a source in its service filter, so an outcome that closed or was dismissed can be read again. devhost keeps no second activity list. Every stack writes its per-manifest log, a foreground stack included, so these lines exist wherever the stack was started (`FB-11`). Contract: `CC-21`.
- **FB-8 One notice stack, in one order, at most three at once.** Condition notices (`ACT-7`), activity lines and outcome notices share the notice stack, ordered: (1) what needs the person or blocks writes (the crash notice, a failure, a rejected configuration, an agent waiting, the lost connection); (2) activity lines; (3) other conditions (**Reload blocked**, the hot reload notice with a pending Disable Reloads request); (4) successes; newest first within a group. At most three notices show; the rest fold into one "N more" control that expands the stack in place, and the collapsed toolbar keeps the one-line form of `SURF-8`. An open surface never covers or absorbs the stack (`SURF-1`): docked chat sits beside it. Example: with the crash notice of api, a Restart Stack activity line, **Reload blocked** and two successes live, the stack shows the crash notice, the activity line and **Reload blocked**, then "2 more". A rejected configuration is a condition, never a failure: its notice, its one-line form and the Configuration window's rejection banner take the condition style and glyph (`WF-3`), while a crash that caused it keeps its own failure notice; a Disable Reloads result waiting on a restart belongs to group (3), neutral, with its actions (revision 7).
- **FB-9 Each report is announced once, and focus stays where the person is.** One announcer per page is the only live output: it owns two visually hidden regions, a polite one (`role="status"`) and an assertive one (`role="alert"`) for failures only, and announces once each operation's start and outcome, each chat turn that ends (replied, stopped or failed, with the reply's first sentence or "reply ready", and the files changed when `O22` lists them), each service that fails or recovers, each condition that starts or ends, and each agent that starts waiting. Reports that arrive within one second merge into one announcement, which goes to the assertive region when any of them is a failure. Notice cards carry no live role (each is a group named by its sentence), so inserting or updating one announces nothing by itself; progress steps, ticking text (elapsed times, step counts) and streaming chat text sit outside every live region (the transcript is `aria-busy` while a reply streams). The announcer lives in a host of its own, which devhost moves into the topmost modal dialog while one is open (the crash view, the Configuration window or the page's own, section 1), so no modal silences it. A report never moves focus: the control that started an operation keeps focus while it runs (`aria-disabled` and `aria-busy`, never `disabled`), a confirmed question's button included (`REV-4`), and when an outcome replaces the content that held focus, focus moves to the outcome's sentence (a `tabindex="-1"` element named by it), never to an action, with the outcome's actions next in Tab order and never to the page body. Status pages put their heading before any status code in reading order.
- **FB-10 Human output shows what it waits for and ends each operation with one line.** `devhost start` prints each URL as its route is published, then one ready line with the elapsed time and the primary URL first, naming the worktree a repository runs from when it is not the configured one, as after an interrupted switch (`SEL-4`); a detached start names the stack within a second and keeps one status line updated on a terminal (the service it waits on, its health check, the elapsed time); `devhost stop` and Ctrl-C name the services still stopping with the time left before devhost forces them, and say that a second Ctrl-C forces it; `devhost caddy download` prints the version and transfer progress; `devhost caddy start` and a serve session name what runs or is served, the listener and how to stop it, and `devhost start` adds the stack directory's URL (`SURF-15`). Every operation on a running stack, from any origin, prints one outcome line (`FB-3`) in the stack's terminal and log, and a superseded reload one line saying so (`REV-12`). Lines report what changed: the full URL list prints at the first start and on request (`devhost stack info`, alias `devhost status`, `FB-11`, `O62`), never again after each action. Contract: `CC-22`.
- **FB-11 Agents and scripts read the same outcomes in their own contract.** Agent output (`AGENT=1`) carries every outcome and state that human output carries, in its own layout (owner rule): every error is one `devhost: ERR:` line holding every issue, and a failure line in the stack's log carries `ERR:` as a warning carries `WARN:`. `devhost stack info [--json] [--wait]` (with `devhost status` as a short alias for the exact same command, `O62`, decided 2026-11-25) prints the stack and each service's state with its cause and since when (`ACT-2`), its URLs, changed-file markers, any manifest-reload rejection state and the last operation's outcome, and exits 0 when every started service runs, or 3 naming each one that does not (1 stays a failure of the command itself); `--wait` first waits for the running operation to end. `devhost reload` applies the manifest on disk to the running stack and exits with its outcome: the services it restarted, or the file, line and key of a rejection, or the service that exited with its last output line (`FAIL-3`). `devhost reload --dry-run` prints what a reload would do and changes nothing: each service it would restart with its reason, starts, stops and moved URLs, or the rejection with file, line and key (`REV-10`); with `--stdin <file>` it checks that manifest file's candidate text from standard input instead of the disk, so an agent or an editor integration sees a save's reach before writing it. `devhost logs [-f] [service…]` finds the stack's per-manifest log and follows it across restarts; the file holds one format, which `devhost logs` renders for people. A request that is not a page load gets devhost's short 503 while its service is not running (`SURF-16`). Every agent devhost starts runs with `AGENT=1` (`REV-8`) and `DEVHOST_CHAT_SESSION` (`ACT-1`). Contract: `CC-23`.
- **FB-12 A chat turn that changes code ends with the change in the running app.** The product owner's comment of 2026-11-18 on the chat edit modes, verbatim: “the goal of the chat feature is - i ask for a change - i wait - i see the change.” As a rule: every chat turn that changes the project's files, including the turn of a Disable Reloads request (`REV-5`), ends with the person seeing the change in the running app — or with the one step left and its reason named — whether they use it on this machine or from another environment (`HOLD-8`). The exception is a turn that changes no files, such as an answer or a **Make tickets** turn. A turn whose change has not reached the running app when it ends (the agent was stopped, a check failed) is never reported as done: its end says that the change is not in the app and names the step that remains and why (`FB-5`, `FAIL-2`). The product owner's answers of 2026-11-18 settle every chat call under this rule: under `O1` (q10a6), chat has no worktree mode — the agent always edits whichever worktree the person has active, main's included, and never creates another worktree; `O20` (reworked for mode switches between **This worktree (live)** and **Make tickets**), `O24` (Disable Reloads result naming **Restart** when needed), `O35` (team `[devtools.chat].prompt` first, then the mode's instructions for `live` or `tickets`) and `O57` (reloads held by **Hold Reloads** apply once at turn end unless focus is in a text field) are decided as approved; under `O56` (q66a2, reversed), **Disable Reloads** wins, so once it takes effect devhost never auto-reloads after a turn and each turn that changed files ends with "Reload to see the change" and **Reload Page**; and under `O58` (q68a2, reversed), the person restarts changed services from the turn's summary ("api runs the old code until it restarts" with **Restart Changed**; services without `watch` paths go unnamed). While chat is closed, the turn's outcome notice carries "Reload to see the change" with **Reload Page** (revision 7). Section 5.1 records each verdict.
- **FB-13 A listener move shows once the new listener answers.** When a devhost config file change moves the shared proxy's ports or bind host, the server sends `listeners-moving` with the old and new address of each listener (each bind host, and plain HTTP when it is on); open pages show the activity line "Moving to port 4443" and keep working on the old address while it answers; once every listener confirms, `listeners-moved` gives the outcome "Moved to https://shop.localhost:4443/ · Open", whose **Open** keeps the path and query, and names any listener that did not move; `listeners-failed` keeps the old listeners and says why (`FAIL-2`). No page shows a move before the new listener answers (revision 7, proxy-settings' protocol; notices-and-feedback follows it).

Rationale: VS Code shows a long task where it started and in its status bar, and keeps dismissed notifications in a notification center; Tilt and Docker Desktop show per-resource state and elapsed time in place; `docker compose up --wait`, `kubectl rollout status` and Vite's "ready in" line certify readiness, not a step; VS Code and JetBrains let routine successes fade and keep actionable results; VS Code caps visible notifications at three; the WAI-ARIA `status` and `alert` roles and WCAG 4.1.3 set the politeness; Vite prints deltas and reprints URLs on request; `docker compose ps --format json`, `tilt wait` and `kubectl wait` give scripts a waitable, exit-coded state. Today progress lives only inside the popover that started it (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/serviceStatusPanel/components/ServiceStatusPanel.tsx:345-368`, `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/shared/components/RestartStackButton.tsx:66-74`); a row's error is cleared by the next status message (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/serviceStatusPanel/hooks/useServiceHealth.ts:55-90`); "Saved and applied." follows whichever reload is counted next (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/configEditor/hooks/useConfigEditor.ts:117-131`); every notice is `role="alert"` (`packages/devhost-ui/src/components/ui/Alert.tsx:22-31`); a page restart prints no completion line (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs`); agent output marks only an error's first line (`apps/devhost/src/cliout.rs:234-261`). Findings: `notices-and-feedback-03`, `services-panel-2-03`, `services-panel-2-05`, `worktree-switch-09`, `config-editor-05`, `config-editor-12`, `startup-recovery-02`, `manifest-hot-reload-01`, `interrupted-operations-05` (progress and its home); `services-panel-2-02`, `journey-crash-to-fix-06`, `worktree-switch-04`, `reload-and-agents-02`, `reload-and-agents-08`, `journey-agent-fix-03`, `source-and-minimap-02`, `stack-directory-07` (outcomes); `chat-conversations-03`, `chat-checkout-switch-01`, `chat-approvals-08`, `chat-conversations-08`, `journey-agent-fix-04`, `config-editor-08` (work ended); `interrupted-operations-01`, `interrupted-operations-02`, `serve-folder-01`, `proxy-settings-02`, `manifest-hot-reload-05`, `worktree-switch-06` (what success certifies); `notices-and-feedback-01`, `error-pages-02`, `config-editor-13`, `chat-conversations-09`, `page-context-09` (lifetime); `chat-compose-04`, `config-editor-11`, `toolbar-shell-07` (several reports); `keyboard-and-screen-reader-01`, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-02`, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-03`, `chat-approvals-03` (announcements); `stack-run-stop-03`, `stack-run-stop-04`, `stack-run-stop-06`, `interrupted-operations-03`, `stack-scale-07`, `first-run-setup-07`, `serve-folder-02`, `chat-conversations-05` (human output); `agent-cli-journey-01`, `agent-cli-journey-02`, `agent-cli-journey-04`, `agent-cli-journey-05`, `stack-run-stop-05`, `agent-cli-journey-06` (agent output).

Use it for every operation devhost runs, from any surface or origin, and for the notice stack's order and the terminal's progress and outcome lines. Do not use it for whether an operation asks first (2.5), what blocks it (2.6), what a failure says (2.8), or how states and conditions are drawn (2.10).

States: an operation in progress in its open surface, and as an activity line after that surface closed; one started in another tab, the terminal, an agent and a file change on disk; a success in its surface, as a notice, and after the page reload it caused; a success that names restarts with reasons and one with "nothing restarted"; an outcome naming ended conversations and discarded drafts; a pending Disable Reloads request and both of its results; a failure kept across a reconnect and a page reload; the notice stack with three notices and "N more", and collapsed; a busy control keeping focus; each terminal line, human and agent.

Keys: a focused control keeps focus while its operation runs; "N more" and every notice action are ordinary buttons. See 2.11.

Accessibility: one announcer per page, with a polite region and an assertive one for failures, is the only live output and announces starts, outcomes, ended chat turns, failures and recoveries, conditions and waiting agents once each, from inside the topmost modal dialog while one is open (`FB-9`); notices carry no live role; a busy control is `aria-busy` and stays focusable; an activity line's progress is text, never only a spinner; status pages announce their heading, not a bare status code.

Backend contract: the operation record of `ACT-1` with the caller's operation ID, each target's step at least once a second, the origin, each target's outcome and the end time, kept until the next operation of the same kind on the same target; every write route takes that ID, answers `202 Accepted` with the record before the operation runs, and answers a repeated ID with the existing record (`REV-12`, `CC-37`); each page sends its page ID with every request, the CLI marks its own requests, and every agent devhost starts runs with `DEVHOST_CHAT_SESSION` set to its conversation, which the CLI passes on, so an origin names an agent only when devhost knows it (`ACT-1`); a save's outcome resolved from the reload that read its saved version (`REV-12`); the Disable Reloads request settled from its own conversation; every stack writing its per-manifest log; one outcome line per operation in the stack's terminal and log; `devhost stack info` extended with reload-rejection state, changed-file markers, `--json`, `--wait`, exit code `3` and short alias `devhost status` (`O62`), plus `devhost reload` and `devhost logs`; one log format per file; one `ERR:` line per agent-mode error.

Component primitive: one notice stack component with condition, activity, outcome and failure variants, built on the existing `Alert`; one announcer in a host of its own with visually hidden `role="status"` and `role="alert"` regions; outcome and progress lines in `apps/devhost/src/cliout.rs`; library work: `Button`'s unavailable state (2.3), which also renders busy (`aria-busy`) and keeps focus; the project's own `Alert` (`packages/devhost-ui/src/components/ui/Alert.tsx`) takes no live role (a group named by its sentence) instead of always `alert`.

Contract change: `CC-21` (every stack writes its log), `CC-22` (human progress and outcome lines), `CC-23` (agent output, `devhost stack info` / `devhost status`, `devhost reload`, `devhost logs`), `CC-37` (operation IDs and `202 Accepted` in the control API); `CC-30` (the two outputs, from 2.12), `CC-35` (a waiting `devhost stop` names what still stops, from 2.10); roster TB-03, TB-07, SVC-02, SVC-03, SVC-04, SVC-05, WT-02, CFG-04, RLD-01, MAN-03, STK-01, STK-02, STK-04, STK-09, SET-02, SET-04, SRV-01, SRV-04, LOG-01.

### 2.8 Partial results and failures

A devhost operation often reaches several services or stacks, and its targets can end differently. This section says what a partial result reports, what keeps running, what a failure report contains and never contains, what evidence a process failure carries, how a repeated failure is reported, and how a deliberate stop differs from a failure. Which action a failure leads with is `MENU-5`'s; where the report shows and how long it stays is 2.7's.

Rules:

- **FAIL-1 A partial result reports every target, and what works keeps running.** When an operation acts on several targets and only some succeed, its outcome lists each target with its own result (restarted, started, failed with its cause, left as it was) and how many services of the stack's total run, and is never reported as a plain success or a plain failure. One service that cannot start never takes the others down: the healthy services keep running and the failure stays on the failing service with its state, exit and logs (owner recovery contract; `UNDO-1`, `REV-11`); a detached start that stops on a failure before readiness is the owner's intended exception and says it stopped the stack. In the terminal, a command that acted on several services or stacks prints one result line per failed target, by name and worktree (`SEL-4`), and exits non-zero naming them. Example: Restart Stack on a stack of 6 managed services where api exits with code 1 before its health check: "Restart Stack: 5 of 6 running · api exited with code 1 before its health check", with api's lead actions (`MENU-5`) beside it (exact words per 2.12); a Restart Stack that devhost rolls back, with api failing again, reports the same way in the rollback's own words ("Restored the services as they ran before Restart Stack: 5 of 6 running · api still fails", `UNDO-1`).
- **FAIL-2 A failure says what failed, why, and the next step, in words.** A failure report names the target, the cause in plain words, the evidence that shows it (`FAIL-3`) and its lead action (`MENU-5`). A manifest or devhost config file problem names the file relative to the root manifest with line, column and key, shows the offending line, offers a close key when one exists, and says the stack still runs the accepted manifest (`MENU-4`, `HOLD-6`). No failure shows a response body, markup, a raw Rust error chain, Rust's transport error or the same path twice: a response devhost cannot read becomes one sentence naming what is not running and the command that starts it (`HOLD-2`, `SURF-9`), and the original text stays behind **Copy Error**. Every action that fails says so where it was started, chat and page actions included (Close Conversation, sharing a tab); a deliberate cancel is not a failure (`FAIL-5`). Example: a save that sets `port = "80a"` for api reads "devhost.toml line 12, column 8, services.api.port: expected a number. The stack still runs the accepted manifest." with **Edit** (exact words per 2.12).
- **FAIL-3 A process failure carries its evidence.** A failed service shows what devhost observed: the exit code, or the signal by name with its status (`WORD-8`), with the time and whether devhost sent it (`FAIL-5`); its own last output lines, kept per service and frozen at its exit so other services' output never pushes them out, opened at the exit (`SURF-13`); a failed health check's probe (the address it checked and the status it got, or that nothing answered, after how long of its timeout); a fixed port held by another program, with that program; and what devhost ran (command, folder, manifest file and line). A rejection caused by a service that crashed keeps that service's last lines with it. A daemon-lifecycle service that stops on its own prints a line in the terminal, and its page says what devhost knows. An agent process that dies ends its reply with a plain cause and what was kept, never as a finished reply.
- **FAIL-4 One cause, one report, cleared when the cause ends.** A failure whose cause repeats updates its own report (a count and the latest time) instead of adding another: a save rejected again with the same error prints one line saying so and naming what it restarted and rolled back, never nothing; a recovery page shows a repeated identical log block once with its repeat count. A failure ends when its cause does, never silently: a fix that clears a rejection replaces the rejection with an outcome ("Configuration restored · nothing restarted", `UNDO-1`), and a rejection tied to a deleted worktree clears when the stack runs a configuration from another worktree (`HOLD-4`).
- **FAIL-5 A deliberate stop, cancel or replacement is never a failure.** Services devhost stops for an operation (a restart, Restart Stack, a switch, a reload, a shutdown, a failed health check) are steps of that operation, never exits waiting for a restart; a service a person stopped reads stopped; one killed from outside names the signal, the time and the sender when known, and keeps that record after it restarts; one devhost had to force reads force-stopped. A foreground stack replaced by `devhost stack restart`, `devhost restart-all` or `devhost upgrade` (`O18`) prints who replaced it, the new stack's PID and its log, and exits 0; `devhost restart-all` lists serve sessions and leaves them running (`O31`, decided 2026-11-18). A Ctrl-C during a detached start's wait is a cancellation without an error tag. In chat, a turn the person stopped ends with a neutral line naming who stopped it and after how long (`UNDO-4`), Cancel in a question leaves a neutral line, and a tab picker the person closed leaves nothing; Watch Live Activity shows a stopped turn as stopped. A declined terminal question exits 1 with "Not done: you answered no"; Ctrl-C at a question exits 130 (revision 7). Contract: `CC-22`, `CC-24`.
- **FAIL-6 A retired setting warns and is ignored; an unknown one is rejected.** A key devhost retired (`[devtools.editor]`, `DEVHOST_EDITOR`, `O51`) is ignored with one warning naming its file, line, column and key and what replaced it, in the terminal, in agent output and in the Configuration window (a warning mark, never a rejection), so an upgrade never stops a stack from starting (`O18`); a key devhost never knew stays a rejection (`HOLD-6`). One retired key is still honored: `[devtools.editor].enabled = false` turns `[devtools.componentSource].enabled` off, with the same warning, so no one's off switch turns on with an upgrade (`SEL-11`, `CC-41`; revision 7).

Rationale: `docker compose up` reports each container and exits non-zero naming the failed ones; Kubernetes reports ready replicas of the total and keeps old replicas serving; Tilt marks each resource on its own; Vite and Next.js overlays and compilers show file, line, column and the offending line; VS Code keeps a notification's details behind "Show details"; systemd separates `code=exited` from `code=killed, signal=TERM` and keeps the result after a restart; Docker shows "Exited (137)" with its cause; JetBrains reports "interrupted by signal 2"; Claude Code marks an interrupted turn as interrupted. Today a failed rollback relaunches the whole previous set and leaves it down (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:860-900`); a control error shows any non-JSON body (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/shared/postControlAction.ts:16-29`); a rejection prints the raw Rust error chain and every exit, devhost's own stops included, reads as waiting for a restart (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:91`); the 502 page appends Rust's `hyper_util` transport error (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/document_injection.rs:409`); one stack-wide ring of 512 log entries holds every service's lines (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/live.rs:88,396`); a repeated rejection prints nothing (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs`); a stopped chat turn keeps the CLI's error as its status (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/chat/session.rs:444-456`). Findings: `error-pages-01`, `error-pages-02`, `services-panel-2-05`, `interrupted-operations-01`, `stack-run-stop-07`, `config-editor-05`, `worktree-switch-09`, `stack-run-stop-13` (partial results); `notices-and-feedback-01`, `journey-new-project-02`, `stack-run-stop-09`, `manifest-hot-reload-02`, `config-editor-03`, `worktree-switch-02`, `error-pages-07`, `first-run-setup-06`, `serve-folder-03`, `source-and-minimap-02`, `third-party-devtools-04`, `chat-conversations-09` (what a failure says); `journey-crash-to-fix-03`, `journey-crash-to-fix-04`, `journey-new-project-03`, `manifest-hot-reload-05`, `startup-recovery-03`, `stack-scale-03`, `manifest-hot-reload-2-02`, `chat-compose-2-04` (evidence); `manifest-hot-reload-04`, `manifest-hot-reload-07`, `worktree-switch-08`, `error-pages-05` (repeats and clearing); `worktree-switch-04`, `stack-scale-07`, `stack-run-stop-02`, `stack-run-stop-04`, `interrupted-operations-03`, `interrupted-operations-04`, `chat-compose-2-03`, `chat-approvals-04`, `page-context-09` (deliberate stops).

Use it for every operation that ends with some targets failed, every failure report in the browser and the terminal, and every stop or cancel someone meant. Do not use it for the action a failure leads with (`MENU-5`), where reports show and how long they stay (2.7), or the states services are drawn in (2.10).

States: a stack-wide restart with one failed service and the rest running; a rollback that restored all but one; a multi-stack command with one failed stack; a manifest error with the editor on and off; an unreadable response turned into a sentence with **Copy Error**; a crash with its last lines, an exit by signal from outside, a failed probe with its status, a held port with its holder; a repeated rejection; a rejection cleared by a fix and by a switch; a replaced foreground stack; a stopped chat turn and a cancelled question; a dead agent process.

Keys: **Copy Error** and every action beside a failure are ordinary buttons; see 2.11.

Accessibility: a failure's cause sentence is the accessible description of its notice, row or view, and the announcer reads failures in its assertive region (`FB-9`); per-target results are a list, not only colored rows; evidence lines keep their order and are readable as text.

Backend contract: the operation record carries each target's result (`ACT-1`); one structured error (file, line, column, key, message, the offending line and a close key) from `apps/devhost/src/manifest/` and `apps/devhost/src/config.rs` (`MENU-4`); log lines retained per service and frozen at its exit; each exit published with its code or signal, time and sender (devhost, a person, outside), and devhost's own stops marked so the terminal and log report them as steps; a failed health check's probe result; the holder of a fixed port; repeated rejections counted; a replaced foreground stack told who replaced it; a stopped chat turn recorded as stopped, not failed.

Component primitive: the shared recovery view (`SURF-14`) for the cause sentence, the evidence and the logs; **Copy Error** from the existing copy control; terminal lines in `apps/devhost/src/cliout.rs`; library work: none.

Contract change: `CC-22` (force-stopped lines), `CC-24` (devhost's own stops, signals and replaced stacks), `CC-36` (serve sessions keep running under `devhost restart-all`, `O31`); roster SVC-02, SVC-04, MAN-02, MAN-04, REC-02, REC-03, REC-08, LOG-02, STK-04, STK-07, STK-09, WT-02, CFG-04, CFG-06, CHAT-04, CHAT-05.

### 2.9 Undo and history

Most devhost writes start or stop processes, which can be done again but not taken back. Its undo is therefore of four kinds: devhost's own rollback when an operation fails; keeping work in progress through the reloads devhost causes; local undo of what the page holds (page context, a dismissed notice, a stopped turn, a closed conversation); and a named way back for writes devhost does not own (an agent's edits, a certificate install, a stopped stack). Its history is the record those leave: the lines a conversation keeps, each file's last save outcome and the stack's log.

Rules:

- **UNDO-1 A rollback restores what the operation found, and says so.** When a reload, Restart Stack or a switch fails, devhost restores the state it found, service by service: every service that ran before runs again, a service that was failing before keeps failing with its recovery page, and the set is never stopped as one unit because one member fails; a failed switch restores the previous worktree. Its outcome is one of its own, naming what the rollback put back, which differs by operation, then what runs now (exact words per 2.12, counts per 2.8); while it restores, its surfaces say the same in progress. With api failing in the operation and still failing once restored, on a stack of 6 services: after a reload, "Restoring the accepted manifest", then "Restored the accepted manifest: 5 of 6 running · api still fails"; after Restart Stack, which runs the accepted manifest before and after, so its rollback puts back the services as they ran, their automatic ports included, "Restored the services as they ran before Restart Stack: 5 of 6 running · api still fails"; after a switch from main ~/acme, "Returning to main ~/acme", then "Returned to main ~/acme: 5 of 6 running · api still fails".
