A save that undoes a rejected edit reports that the configuration is restored, and "nothing restarted" when nothing did.
- **UNDO-2 Work in progress survives every reload devhost causes.** A page reload (after a switch, Restart Stack on a recovery page, a crash, or the person's own) keeps chat's unsent text, in browser storage as today, and its pasted images and page context in a draft record on the control server per stack, page origin and conversation (each pick's element path and offsets and each drawing's shape, never a screenshot), capped at the 8 MiB a send may carry and removed once the send is acknowledged or the stack stops; marks come back when their element still resolves and are otherwise listed as lost with **Select Again** and **Remove** (`SEL-9`); sent messages with their delivery state (`CC-40`), and messages this page had not sent yet, back in the composer; queued starts and restarts of services, which the services runtime holds (`SEL-7`); and Configuration window drafts, kept on the control server per stack and file with the version they were opened at until the stack stops, restored on open with a line naming the files that hold drafts, and counted on **Config**. Each configuration draft carries a revision: a page writes it with the revision it last read, and when another tab changed it meanwhile, the window shows "Changed in another tab" with **Compare**, **Keep Mine** (which asks, `REV-4`) and **Use Other Version**, `UNDO-3`'s conflict view, instead of overwriting either. A screenshot share ends with the page, and the composer says it ended. A held reload is the one thing a reload cannot keep, so `REV-3` names it before a write reloads the page. Contract: `CC-19`.
- **UNDO-3 Nothing pending is cleared silently.** Turning **Hold Reloads** off while a reload is held never reloads the page by itself, because a toggle that reloads would discard a half-typed form, the page's or devhost's, without notice: the held reload stays as "1 reload pending" with **Reload Now**, and later reloads go through. Today's way to let a held reload through by turning the hold off (`guides/devtools.md`: "Use **Reload** on the notice, or turn the hold off") becomes that one press (`CC-20`); a person who wants to keep the page as it is keeps the hold on and dismisses the notice, which leaves the held marker (`SURF-8`). A file that changed on disk under a draft never replaces or drops the draft: the window offers **Compare**, **Keep Mine** (which asks, naming what it overwrites and showing the difference, `REV-4`) and **Use Disk Version**; without a draft it loads the disk text in place and says so. A refused stale write keeps the draft (`REV-13`). Dismissing the hot reload notice offers **Undo** in its place and can be reversed later with **Show Reload Notice** in the Hold Reloads popover; it is never a permanent hide. A dismissed condition notice leaves its marker until the condition ends (`SURF-8`). Contract: `CC-20`.
- **UNDO-4 Chat actions on their own target are recoverable instead of asking.** Stop Turn keeps the conversation and the draft and offers **Continue**; Close Conversation keeps the transcript, read-only, in the conversation list, and **Resume** continues the same conversation with its history; a message still waiting on this page for an earlier send's acknowledgement can be taken back into the composer (`REV-6`); **Cancel** on any question restores everything the question held, the draft included, and says the message was not sent (`REV-3`). A conversation a stop ended stays listed and resumable after its stack starts again, from the record `O23` keeps (`CC-38`).
- **UNDO-5 A write devhost cannot take back names its way back.** Its outcome says how to reverse it. An agent's edit in **This worktree (live)** stays in the active worktree's working tree (main's included; `O1` removed the New worktree mode on 2026-11-18): devhost never commits, stashes or reverts for the agent, the mode's instructions ask the same of the agent (`O19`), and each sent message records its mode (`O1`) so the person knows where the edit landed; **Make tickets** reports each ticket the agent filed, or changes no files and states that it cannot make tickets when it lacks that capability, with no ticket drafts (`O11`, `O21`, reversed 2026-11-18). Each finished turn lists the files the agent changed. In **This worktree (live)** its way back is the reverse patch of the agent's own edits, to copy, and a follow-up that asks the agent to undo the turn, never a whole-file `git restore` or `git checkout`, which would also discard the person's own uncommitted changes to that file; a file the record cannot reverse, such as one a shell command wrote, is listed with that warning (`O22`). A switch names the previous worktree and links to the worktree list with it highlighted. Stopping a stack prints the command that starts it again, with its folder. Every certificate install prints the command that removes it, and `devhost caddy untrust` removes the roots devhost installed (`REV-8`).
- **UNDO-6 The way back in the configuration is to what runs.** A rejected file is a state of that file: the Configuration window says the file on disk differs from what the stack runs, marks the lines that differ, and offers **Show Differences** and **Restore Running Version**, which writes the accepted text back and applies like any save (`REV-10`). For this, devhost keeps the raw text of every file the last accepted reload read, in memory for the stack's lifetime, and never writes it back by itself, because the rejected text is the person's work. **Revert** shows the version on disk, never the running one (`O29`). The editor's undo history survives a save, so an accepted save is undone by undoing the text and saving again, which reports its own reload; devhost offers no "Undo save" that would hide that second reload. The stack directory shows a rejected edit as not applied, with the running and the on-disk version. A manifest file of a worktree the stack does not run can be edited: the save is checked and kept, applies when the stack runs that worktree, and its outcome says so (revision 7).
- **UNDO-7 Page context undoes the last action.** **Undo** reverses the last page-context action, whatever it was (add, move or remove a mark, draw, clear), back to the start of the draft, so **Clear Page Context** is undone the same way; Send ends the draft's undo history. Its key and scope are 2.11's and never take the app's own undo.
- **UNDO-8 Every decision leaves a record where it was made.** The conversation keeps, on each sent message, its mode with the worktree it edited, its page context and the instructions devhost added (`REV-6`, `SEL-8`); on each answered request, the answer line (`REV-9`); on each stopped turn, that the person stopped it. devhost writes these as records of its own into the session's journal, sequenced with the agent's native events and replayed with them to every page that attaches (`CC-40`), never as activity log lines, which keep only IDs and outcomes. The Configuration window keeps each file's last save outcome with that file (`REV-12`), and the stack's log keeps every operation's outcome line with its time (2.7). These records survive reloads; no record offers an undo of its own.

Rationale: Terraform and Kubernetes report "rolled back to revision N" as an outcome of its own, and Kubernetes keeps old replicas serving until new ones are ready; VS Code's hot exit restores unsaved editors after a window reload, and VS Code and JetBrains offer Compare, keep mine or load the disk version when a file changes under an unsaved edit and keep the editor's undo history across saves; Gmail-style Undo follows a dismissal; Figma's and Linear's undo reverses the last action, whatever it was; Claude Code and Codex CLI keep the conversation after an interrupt. The owner settled that chat always edits the active worktree with no additional worktrees, commits or merges (`O1`, updated 2026-11-18 by q10a6) and that devhost never resends a message. Today a failed rollback stops every service it touched (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:860-900`), configuration drafts live only in React state (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/configEditor/hooks/useConfigEditor.ts:57-63`), turning the hold off clears a held reload (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/reloadHold/hooks/useReloadHold.ts:19-23`), and the hot reload notice's Dismiss is a permanent per-origin setting (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/shared/createDevtoolsSettingsStore.ts:13-32`). Findings: `error-pages-01`, `manifest-hot-reload-04`, `worktree-switch-04`, `worktree-switch-06` (rollback); `config-editor-08`, `journey-agent-fix-04`, `chat-compose-2-02` (reloads); `reload-and-agents-03`, `reload-and-agents-04`, `reload-and-agents-01`, `config-editor-07` (nothing cleared silently); `chat-conversations-02`, `chat-compose-2-03`, `chat-approvals-04` (chat); `first-run-setup-05`, `stack-run-stop-11`, `chat-conversations-03`, `journey-agent-fix-01` (ways back); `config-editor-05`, `config-editor-06`, `stack-directory-05` (configuration); `page-context-04` (page context); `chat-approvals-05` (records).

Use it for every way back from a write, a dismissal or a cancelled question, and for every record a decision leaves. Do not use it for the question before a write (2.5) or for how a failure is reported (2.8).

States: a rollback in progress and its outcome, with a failing service left failing; a reload restoring the draft, marks (restored and lost), sent messages' delivery states, unsent messages and configuration drafts; Hold Reloads turned off with a reload held, leaving "1 reload pending" with **Reload Now**; a disk change under a draft and without one; a draft changed in another tab; a dismissed notice with Undo, then brought back; a stopped turn with Continue; a closed conversation with Resume; a rejected file with Show Differences and Restore Running Version; each way back named in an outcome; page context undone through a clear.

Keys: the Configuration window's undo and page context's Undo; see 2.11.

Accessibility: after a reload, restored and lost marks are announced once with their counts; an Undo control is reachable from the keyboard while it shows and stays while it has focus; decision lines and modes are text in the conversation's reading order.

Backend contract: per-service rollback with a published "restoring" state (`UNDO-1`); configuration drafts on the control server per stack and file with their base version and a revision, refusing a write from an older revision (`UNDO-2`); a chat draft record on the control server per stack, page origin and conversation holding pasted images and page context, capped at 8 MiB, read under the config routes' `Host` rule and removed on an acknowledged send or at stack stop (`UNDO-2`); the session's journal records each message's mode, page context and the instructions devhost added, each answer with its note, and each stop as devhost's record frames beside the native events, replayed in `snapshot` (`UNDO-8`, `CC-40`), while activity log lines keep only IDs and outcomes, as `reference/chat-api.md` requires; the running manifest text for Show Differences and Restore Running Version (`UNDO-6`); the per-turn list of changed files with the reverse patch of the agent's edits, from its edit events once each harness's coverage is checked (`UNDO-5`, `O22`); `devhost caddy untrust` with a record of the roots each state directory installed, and a stopped stack's start command and folder (`UNDO-5`).

Component primitive: CodeMirror's history for the editor; one diff view shared by **Show Differences** and the approval card's diff (`REV-9`); the outcome notice's Undo (2.7); library work: the diff view, if neither CodeMirror's merge extension nor an existing component provides it.

Contract change: `CC-19` (work survives reloads, the draft routes), `CC-20` (dismissals and disk conflicts), `CC-40` (devhost's records in the session's journal); `CC-13` (`devhost caddy untrust`), `CC-14` (Cancel keeps the draft, from 2.5), `CC-16` (decision lines, from 2.5), `CC-38` (ended conversations resumable, from 2.1); roster SVC-04, MAN-03, MAN-04, WT-02, CFG-04, CFG-05, RLD-01, RLD-02, CHAT-04, CHAT-05, CHAT-09, CTX-02, CTX-03, SET-03, STK-04.

### 2.10 Background work and activity

devhost's background work is the services runtime itself: operations run on the server whatever started them, services change state on their own, and conditions arise from saves, agents and the proxy. The service and stack state model, which the template has no section for, lives here as the closest section: the operation record (`ACT-1`), service states (`ACT-2` to `ACT-5`, `ACT-8`, `ACT-9`), stack summaries and end states (`ACT-6`, `ACT-10`, `ACT-11`), conditions and the attention state (`ACT-7`), live state (`ACT-12`), how states are encoded (`ACT-13`) and which processes devhost signals (`ACT-14`). The running agents list and Watch Live Activity are agent activity; their waiting state follows `ACT-7` and `SURF-8`.

Rules:

- **ACT-1 One operation record, published to every reader.** The services runtime keeps one record per operation, whatever started it (`HOLD-3`, `REV-12`): its ID, which the caller generates and sends with the request (a lowercase UUID, as chat's `commandId`); its kind and target set; its origin as far as the backend can see it: this tab or another (each page sends its page ID with every request), the terminal (the CLI), an agent (the CLI under `AGENT=1`, named with its conversation only when devhost started it and set `DEVHOST_CHAT_SESSION`), or a file that changed on disk (the watcher, which cannot tell an editor's save from an agent's edit or a formatter); its start and end times; and each target's step and outcome. Every write route answers `202 Accepted` with the record before the operation runs (`CC-37`). The status stream sends it to every open page of the stack, the stack's terminal and log print from it, and `devhost stack info` (alias `devhost status`, `O62`) reads it, so every surface and every tab reports the same operation with the same progress (`FB-1`, `FB-2`); a page never infers a result by counting reloads or health messages. A record stays until the next operation of the same kind on the same target, which `devhost stack info` (`devhost status`) reports as the last outcome; a page that loads shows an outcome from it only for an operation ID it stored before reloading (in `sessionStorage`, so only in that tab) or for a failure not yet handled (`FB-6`), never a success older than `O14`'s time. Where the toolbar is off, the terminal and the log are the record (`FB-7`).
- **ACT-2 One service state, published with its cause.** Every surface (the Services trigger and panel, the crash view, status pages, the stack directory, the terminal, `devhost stack info` / `devhost status` and agent output) shows a service in the one state the runtime publishes, never a state of its own derivation, with its cause and since when. States: **not started** (left out of this run, or stopped by a person since; the cause says which); **starting** (`ACT-4`); **running** (passes its health check, or runs when it has none); **restarting** and **stopping** (devhost is replacing or stopping it, `ACT-3`); **failed** (could not start, or fails its health check while its process runs); **exited** (its process ended on its own, with its code or signal, `FAIL-3`); **blocked** (its repository's worktree is unavailable and it does not run, `HOLD-4`, `MENU-5`; a service still running from a deleted worktree stays running under the repository's blocked condition; a fresh worktree that lacks setup shows its failing service as exited with that cause, `O32`); and for an external service, **reachable** or **not reachable** (`ACT-9`). Independent of the state, a service can be **changed** (`ACT-8`), and on a page that lost its stream every state is **unknown** (`ACT-5`). A running service whose dependency failed or exited stays running and names that dependency. No surface shows a default for a state it did not receive. Each state has one word on every surface and in both outputs (2.12). Contract: `CC-25`.
- **ACT-3 A transition belongs to the services it touches.** While devhost changes services (a restart, Restart Stack, a start, a switch, a reload, a rollback, a shutdown), each touched service shows restarting, starting or stopping with its step (`FB-1`), a rollback says what it restores in its operation's words (`UNDO-1`: "Restoring the accepted manifest" after a reload), and every other service keeps its own state: starting one service never redraws the stack as down, and a service being replaced is never drawn as failed or counted as down. A stack that is shutting down shows stopping, published before its first service stops, with the count stopped ("Stopping Acme Shop · 3 of 6 stopped", exact words per 2.12). During a switch, the repository's services show that they are switching to the target worktree (`SEL-4`), never running from the old one. A first start and a restart share one look.
- **ACT-4 Starting is not failing.** A service is starting from its launch until its first passing health check, or until the health check's timeout fails it; while it waits for a dependency, it says which one. Starting never looks like failed or exited: the trigger counts it as starting ("0 of 2 running · 2 starting"), and its row and status page show the health check's target, the elapsed time and the timeout. Its status page shows only the current attempt's output and says what started the attempt (`FAIL-3`).
- **ACT-5 A lost connection is unknown, never down.** When a page loses its status stream, every service keeps its last known state drawn as unknown (hollow and neutral, with the time it was last seen), and the trigger shows the connection's state instead of a count; failure looks and words stay for failures the server reported. The lost connection is a condition notice naming its cause, with **Retry Now**, and every write is blocked (`HOLD-2`). When the stream returns, states update in place, the page says once that it is connected again with the stack's state ("Connected again · 6 of 6 running"), and the notices the outage raised clear with it (`ACT-7`). No delay hides a disconnect: the routine drop at the end of Restart Stack is removed at its source (`HOLD-2`). Contract: `CC-26`.
- **ACT-6 A stack summary counts the whole stack and leads with what needs the person.** A stack's summary counts every service of its manifest, the services the run left out included (`SEL-7`, `CC-7`), on the Services trigger, each stack directory card and in `devhost stack info` (`devhost status`, `O62`), and names first the states that need the person: "3 of 6 running · 1 failed · 2 not started" (exact words per 2.12); a changed service counts as running. A stack is in one stack state, the first that applies: **stopped** (`ACT-10`); **stopping**; **proxy offline** (`ACT-10`); **failed** (a started service failed, exited or is blocked); **starting**; **changing** (an operation runs, `ACT-3`); **running** (every started service runs, services left out by choice included). On a page that lost its stream, the stack is unknown (`ACT-5`). The stack directory lists stacks in the failed state or with an agent waiting for the person first, counts both in its totals and its title (`O16`), and shows each waiting agent on its card as a link that opens a page of its stack with docked chat on that conversation's decision card, in one activation (`REV-9`, `CC-38`).
- **ACT-7 Conditions make up the attention state, and clear with their cause.** A stack's attention state is every condition that needs the person: a failed, exited or blocked service; a rejected manifest file or devhost config file; an agent waiting for an answer; a held or blocked reload; this machine not trusting devhost's certificate authority (`WORD-12`, revision 7); and on the page, the lost connection or the proxy offline. Each condition is a condition notice while it lasts (`FB-8`); it starts and ends with the published state, is announced once by the announcer when it starts and once when it ends (`FB-9`), and clears by itself when its cause ends: a rejection when the stack accepts a configuration (from any worktree, `FAIL-4`), **Reload blocked** when the page is reloaded or already current, a notice an outage raised when the outage ends. Dismissing a condition notice leaves its marker until the condition ends (`SURF-8`, `UNDO-3`). The collapsed badge shows the highest condition in this order: lost connection or proxy offline, then a failed, exited or blocked service, then a rejected configuration, then an untrusted certificate authority, then an agent waiting, then a held or blocked reload, then changed, else healthy; a routine restart is never shown as failed. A waiting agent shows as waiting, distinct from working, on Chat, the running agents trigger and Watch Live Activity (`SURF-8`). On devhost's own pages (status pages, the stack directory, Watch Live Activity) and on app pages alike, a waiting agent or a failure prefixes the browser tab's title while the condition persists (such as "(1) waiting · Acme Shop") and restores the page's original title when cleared (`O16`, reversed 2026-11-18); this prefix is the one form, and Watch Live Activity's own title is "Watch Live Activity · Acme Shop" before it (revision 7).
- **ACT-8 Changed means the running process does not reflect its watched files.** A service is changed while the content of its watch paths differs from their content at its process's launch, as the server alone computes it, so every tab, the terminal and `devhost stack info` (`devhost status`) read the same mark. Reverting an edit clears the mark, an edit made during a restart is compared with the new process's launch, and a restart clears it against that launch. When the page saw its dev server apply an edit in place, it adds a note of its own beside the mark ("applied in place by Vite's hot update"), and the published mark stays. Changed is independent of every state, so the mark and its restart icon stay beside a failure (`SEL-2`); an external service is never marked changed, because devhost cannot restart it. The mark names its files (`SEL-3`); the stack directory and `devhost stack info` (`devhost status`) show it as a detail of the service.
- **ACT-9 An external service shows reachability, never a lifecycle.** An external service (`managed = false`) is reachable or not reachable from devhost's own check, on every surface the same way, with the check, its last result and its time, and says it runs outside devhost; it offers no Restart, Start or logs and is never changed (owner rule: status only; `MENU-3`, `MENU-5`). Its status page says what devhost routes and how it checks it, with **Check Again**. It never blocks its repository's worktree switch and needs no warning when managed services switch (`O7`, decided 2026-11-25): managed services restart from the target worktree's folder while the external service stays untouched.
- **ACT-10 A stack's end is a state with its reason, kept for a while.** When a stack ends, its terminal prints one line naming the stack, the reason (stopped from a terminal, stopped from another stack's page with that stack's name, the idle timeout with its duration, replaced by `devhost stack restart`, `devhost restart-all` or `devhost upgrade` (`O18`), killed, or every service gone; a command or page that stops a stack records why beside the stack's record before it signals it) and the time; an open page shows the stack stopped with that reason and the command that starts it again, with its folder (`HOLD-2`), never crashed services; Watch Live Activity says the stack stopped instead of reconnecting. For the time `O15` sets, the stack's hostnames keep their configured Caddy routes and HTTPS certificates active (`O63`) and answer with a page naming the stack, when and why it stopped and that command, and the stack directory lists it as recently stopped (while arbitrary unknown HTTPS hostnames keep strict configured-hosts-only TLS, `O63`). A page whose stack was replaced reconnects to the new stack and says so once. devhost links Watch Live Activity only on the stack directory's hostname, on this machine too (`O36`, `O55`); the unlinked `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` page belongs to the replaced run's port, so it shows a replaced stack as an ended run with a link to the directory hostname's page, the one exception (revision 7). Each reason has one sentence on every page: "Stopped from the terminal at 14:02", "Stopped from Acme Admin's page at 14:02", "Stopped with Ctrl-C at 14:02", "Stopped after 30 min idle", "Replaced by devhost restart-all at 14:02", and on Watch Live Activity "Chat turned off at 14:02"; a stream closes with 1001 when its stack ends and with 1000 when a setting turned its feature off (`CC-46`, revision 7). While the shared Caddy is stopped, every running stack prints one line that it lost its proxy and one when its routes answer again, and its pages show the proxy offline. These pages offer commands to copy; devhost never opens a browser or starts a stack from them (owner rule). Contract: `CC-27`.
- **ACT-11 A reported state matches the processes that run.** A stack reads stopped only when no process of its services, no route and no port claim of it remains: `devhost stop` (and every stop caller unified under `O30`: `stop --worktrees`, `stop --session`, `stack restart`, `restart-all`, `upgrade`, `start --detach` cleanup, Worktrees **Stop Stack** and `serve` sessions) waits for that as long as `O30` allows (30 s by default, configurable `5s`..`10m`, stopping foreground services and daemons in parallel from `t = 0` without killing a stopping supervisor, coordinated with `O41`'s 20 s daemon deadline) unless the person forces it (`KEY-14`), and otherwise names each survivor (service, PID, port) and exits non-zero (`FAIL-1`). `devhost start`, `devhost stop` and `devhost stack list` find the leftover service processes of a stack that was killed, from the PIDs, ports and hostnames its record kept, and name each; before launching a service, `devhost start` refuses while such a leftover of the same manifest holds the service's port or hostname, naming it and the command that stops it, and `REV-11`'s probe names any other program on a fixed port, so a leftover answering a hostname is never reported as ready; a health check certifies that the endpoint answers, not who owns the process, because Docker's port proxy, a daemon lifecycle's `lifecycle.start` and a wrapper that later opens the port all serve from outside devhost's process tree; devhost signals only processes it verified as the service's own (`ACT-14`, `process-containment-01`, now fixed on Rust `main` `190a8bb5`); a Caddy that answers slowly is never declared stopped.
- **ACT-12 State is live wherever it shows; a snapshot says its age.** Every browser surface that shows service or stack state updates it in place from the published state while it is open: the Services trigger and panel, status pages and the stack directory. A view that can only show a snapshot (a CLI answer, the last known state while disconnected) says when it was taken. A status page that waits for its URL checks it again when the published state changes (the service restarted, became healthy, or a watched file changed), otherwise backs off to checks at 1, 2, 5 and 10 s and then every 30 s, and a hidden tab does not check at all. That back-off is for devhost's own status pages; a page showing the app's own error answer is never re-requested on a timer (`SURF-16`). Terminal output reports changes, not the whole stack (`FB-10`).
- **ACT-13 A state is told by shape and text, never by color alone, at any scale.** Each state has its own shape or icon and its word as text or in the accessible name, and stays distinct in forced colors: failed and exited differ from not started and external, unknown differs from failed, and a held reload and a readout alarm keep a shape. Every state look meets the devtools contrast contract (4.5:1 text, 3:1 control boundaries) in both themes. When the trigger's dots do not fit (`SURF-10`), its summary gives counts by state at a fixed width and names up to three services that need the person, then "+N". The terminal names a state with its word, never only with a color.
- **ACT-14 devhost signals only the processes it started for a service, by their identity, on macOS and Linux alike.** The product owner's comment of 2026-11-18 on the process-identity fix, verbatim: “should work on mac and linux”. As a rule: every signal devhost sends to a managed service's processes, for a stop, a restart, Restart Stack, a switch, a reload that restarts the service, a shutdown (the idle timeout included) or Ctrl-C (`KEY-14`), reaches only the processes devhost started for that service and their descendants, each recorded with its start identity (its PID and its start time) when devhost first sees it; an entry whose PID is gone or whose start time changed is dropped, and the identity is checked again just before each signal, so a later process that reuses one of those PIDs is never signalled (`process-containment-01`, now fixed on Rust `main` `190a8bb5` in `apps/devhost/src/services/containment.rs` with cross-platform macOS and Linux process-identity verification). A daemon that double-forked and was re-parented stays the service's and stops with it. The one exception is an external service (`managed = false`), which devhost never signals (`ACT-9`). It is how `ACT-11`'s "devhost signals only processes it verified as the service's own" holds, and it changes neither `O30`'s stop allowance nor `KEY-14`'s forced stop: a forced stop also signals only the processes that pass the check.

Rationale: Docker keeps process state and health apart and prints both ("Up 3 seconds (health: starting)", "Exited (1) 5 seconds ago"); Kubernetes keeps a pod Running but not Ready until its probe passes, marks pods Unknown, not Failed, when it loses their node, and keeps a pod Pending with a reason; Tilt keeps every resource's own state and a pending update after a file change; Docker Desktop labels a partly running project "Running (3/6)" and keeps stopped projects listed with Start; systemd reports a unit dead only when no process of its cgroup remains; VS Code Remote shows reconnecting with a countdown and keeps the last known state; the Next.js dev indicator keeps an issue count on its collapsed badge; VS Code compares against saved content, so undoing an edit clears the modified mark; WCAG 1.4.1 and 1.4.11 forbid state by color alone. Today one boolean carries every state (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/streams.rs`), the runtime folds blocked, failed and starting into it (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/live.rs`, `apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs`), the directory derives its own words and defaults to Running (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/directory.rs:98-118`), the recovery page tells the states apart from the runtime (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/live.rs:226-291`), a disconnect marks every service down (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/serviceStatusPanel/hooks/useServiceHealth.ts:73-82`), the collapsed dot reads any status false as down (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/components/App.tsx:418-424`), the changed mark is set on any file event and cleared only by a restart (`apps/devhost/src/services/watch.rs:42`), and `devhost stop` kills only devhost after 15 s (`apps/devhost/src/services/stop.rs:260-296`); the stack directory also never checks health, so a running process that fails its health check reads Running (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/directory.rs:98-118`). Findings: `stack-directory-02`, `stack-directory-08`, `agent-cli-journey-01`, `services-panel-02` (one state model); `journey-new-project-02`, `startup-recovery-02`, `agent-cli-journey-04` (starting); `services-panel-2-03`, `stack-scale-06`, `worktree-switch-09`, `manifest-hot-reload-01`, `toolbar-shell-10` (transitions); `services-panel-2-04`, `notices-and-feedback-02`, `proxy-settings-03`, `notices-and-feedback-04` (the lost connection); `services-panel-2-05`, `stack-scale-04` (summaries); `toolbar-shell-01`, `reload-and-agents-04`, `chat-approvals-03`, `reload-and-agents-06`, `worktree-switch-08`, `stack-directory-05` (conditions); `services-panel-2-01`, `journey-agent-fix-03`, `stack-scale-08`, `services-panel-2-06` (changed); `manifest-hot-reload-2-04`, `manifest-hot-reload-2-03` (external services); `stack-run-stop-11`, `stack-run-stop-06`, `chat-conversations-03`, `stack-directory-07`, `reload-and-agents-10`, `stack-run-stop-02` (end states); `interrupted-operations-01`, `interrupted-operations-02`, `interrupted-operations-03`, `process-containment-01`, `stack-scale-01`, `stack-scale-09`, `stack-run-stop-13` (ground truth); `error-pages-05`, `stack-scale-07` (live state); `keyboard-and-screen-reader-02`, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-04`, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-03`, `stack-scale-05` (encoding).

Use it for every display of a service's, a stack's or an agent's state, on every surface and in both outputs, and for every condition and the collapsed badge. Do not use it for an operation's progress and outcome text (2.7), failure reports (2.8), what blocks an action (2.6) or the words of each state (2.12).

States: each service state with its cause, and changed beside running and beside failed; a dependent of a failed service; a first start and a dependency wait; a restart, Restart Stack, a switch and a shutdown in progress; a lost connection with each cause and the reconnection; a stack summary with services not started, starting and failed; each stack state; each condition, dismissed with its marker, and the collapsed badge in each; an external service reachable and not; a stopped stack's hostname page, a recently stopped stack in the directory, a replaced stack, the proxy offline; a stop that leaves survivors; a snapshot with its age; 40 services as a summary; forced colors.

Keys: none of its own; the trigger and the collapsed badge are reached as 2.11 says.

Accessibility: each service's state and cause are text in its row and in the trigger's accessible name; state changes that need the person, and their recovery, are announced once (`FB-9`), and the reconnection once; focus never moves when a state changes; unknown, failed, exited and not started differ in shape as well as color and survive forced colors.

Backend contract: the published service state replaces today's boolean with the state, its cause, since when, a failed dependency, the changed files and an external service's last check (`ACT-2`, `ACT-8`, `ACT-9`), read by the toolbar, status pages, the stack directory, the terminal and `devhost stack info` (`devhost status`, `O62`); the operation record (`ACT-1`); a stopping state published before services stop (`ACT-3`); parallel foreground and daemon shutdown from `t = 0` with one termination signal and one shared process snapshot (`O30`, `apps/devhost/src/services/stack.rs`, `apps/devhost/src/services/containment.rs`); the changed mark computed on the server from each watched file's content at the process's launch against its content now (`ACT-8`); the stack directory reading the published state, each stack's waiting agents included, and updating while open (`ACT-6`, `ACT-12`); a stopped-stack record per manifest in the state directory, written by the stopping process, or by the next devhost command that finds a killed stack's owner dead, which then re-renders, under the route mutation lock, the configured Caddy stopped-stack routes (`O63`: keeping HTTPS routes and certificates active for `O15`'s 24 h window while keeping strict configured-hosts-only TLS for arbitrary unknown HTTPS hostnames), the unmatched-hostname 404 (`apps/devhost/src/caddy/files.rs`, `sync_not_found_site`, served on plain HTTP when `httpEnabled = true` with `O46`'s close-hostname suggestions) and the stack directory's `stacks.html` (`apps/devhost/src/caddy/files.rs`, `write_stack_directory_site`; `apps/devhost/src/caddy/stacks.rs`), each embedding every stop time so the page hides entries older than `O15`'s limit; the stack directory site, which today answers every path but `/` with 404, gains one path the open page polls for `ACT-12`'s live state (`ACT-10`, `O15`); each stack told when its proxy stops and returns; leftover-process detection from a killed stack's record, checked before each launch, with health checks that test only the endpoint (`ACT-11`); each process of a service recorded with its start identity, on macOS and Linux, and checked again before every signal (`ACT-14`, `apps/devhost/src/services/containment.rs`).

Component primitive: one service state indicator (shape, word, accessible name) shared by the toolbar, status pages and the stack directory, shipped in both style entries (`SURF-14`); the existing `ToolbarPopover` trigger for the summary; library work: none.

Contract change: `CC-25` (the published service state), `CC-26` (a lost connection is unknown), `CC-27` (a stopped stack's hostnames, configured-hosts-only TLS and the stack directory, `O15`, `O63`), `CC-35` (parallel shutdown and how long a stop waits, `O30`), `CC-7` (not-started services counted, from 2.1), `CC-23` (`devhost stack info` / `devhost status`, `O62`, from 2.7), `CC-37` (the operation record, from 2.7), `CC-38` (waiting agents in every worktree of the stack, from 2.1); roster TB-02, TB-03, TB-07, SVC-01, SVC-03, SVC-05, REC-01, REC-02, REC-06, REC-07, STK-04, STK-06, STK-08, MAN-07, AGT-01, AGT-02.

### 2.11 Keyboard map

devhost's keys live in four places: page-wide chords that the injected toolbar listens for on every page of a stack, keys inside devhost's own surfaces, Ctrl-C and questions in the terminal, and the keys of the libraries devhost launches, which this map lists so devhost never takes them. This reference writes Mod for ⌘ on macOS and Ctrl elsewhere; devhost's page-wide chords use the physical Alt and Ctrl keys on every platform (Option and Control on macOS) and are written Alt+Ctrl+<key>.

Rules:

- **KEY-1 Scopes.** A key acts in one scope only, the innermost one that holds focus, and a scope's keys act only while it is active:
  - **Page**: an app page or a devhost status page with the toolbar, while focus is outside text entry and no devhost menu or modal dialog is open. devhost's page-wide chords (`KEY-2`) act here, also while the page's own modal dialog is open; their outcomes show in the notice stack, which moves into that dialog (`SURF-11`).
  - **Text entry**: the page's own fields (`input`, `textarea`, `select`, contenteditable, and custom widgets whose role is `textbox`, `searchbox`, `combobox` or `spinbutton`, never `grid`; revision 7) and devhost's (the chat composer, the configuration text, find and filter fields, the Disable Reloads request, the Deny note, the crop fields). Printable keys type, page-wide chords are suspended, and only the keys the table lists for that field act; holding Alt alone still starts Select Elements in the chat composer, by `KEY-2`'s test.
  - **Dock**: the bar and its notice stack (`KEY-3`, `KEY-4`).
  - **Panel**: an open toolbar panel or anchored popover with focus inside it, an in-place question included (`SURF-2`).
  - **Menu**: an open menu with focus inside it (the stack name menu, the source menu).
  - **Modal**: the crash view or the Configuration window while open.
  - **Mode**: Select Elements or a drawing tool while active. Its keys (the arrows, Enter, Escape, Shift+F10, Mod+Z) act before those of the scope that holds focus, such as the annotation toolbar's toggle group, except in text entry.
  - **Chat**: docked chat with focus in it outside the composer.
  - **Full page**: status pages, the stack directory and Watch Live Activity, which use the document's standard keys and have no shortcuts of their own.
  - **Terminal**: a devhost command in the foreground.
  - **Library**: a third-party devtools panel devhost launches, which keeps its own keys.
- **KEY-2 Page-wide chords are one family, configurable, and never the only way.** devhost binds page-wide only Alt+Ctrl chords on the physical key, each configurable under `[devtools.shortcuts]`: Restart Changed (`restartServices`, Alt+Ctrl+R), focus the bar (`focusToolbar`, Alt+Ctrl+B), the pinned log panel (`logs`, Alt+Ctrl+O) and chat (`chat`, Alt+Ctrl+C), which opens docked chat with focus in the composer, or focuses the composer while chat is docked; there Escape returns focus to the element that had it (`KEY-8`), because chords stay suspended in text entry (`KEY-1`). A default never takes a chord that a browser, a common desktop or a library devhost launches reserves, except the documented Alt+Ctrl+R, which Windows Magnifier also uses to resize its lens while it runs, so Magnifier users rebind it (`O17`); devhost binds no single-character key outside a focused menu or list (WCAG 2.1.4). A chord event whose `key` is a printable character other than the bound key's own is typed text (AltGr, which is Ctrl+Alt on Windows), never a chord, so an editing widget devhost does not recognise as text entry keeps its characters. Every chord's action is also a control, so a chord the desktop or VoiceOver takes costs only the shortcut. Alt-hold selection starts only on the first pointer move while Alt alone is held, or once Alt alone has been held for the time `O34` sets; any other key pressed before that cancels it without drawing anything, so neither a chord that includes Alt nor a character typed with Option starts it; once started, it ends when Alt is released or another key is pressed (revision 7). Every value the manifest accepts fires, named and function keys included; a value devhost cannot use is reported with its file, line, key and the binding in effect, never replaced silently. A person's devhost config file overrides the manifest's bindings binding by binding (`O17`).
- **KEY-3 The dock is one chord away and is a landmark.** The notice stack and the bar form one landmark named "devhost", in DOM order matching their drawn order, so notices drawn above the bar come before it. Alt+Ctrl+B moves focus to the bar item focused last (else the first); pressed again it moves on to the pinned log panel while it is open, then to docked chat's composer while chat is docked, and from the last of these back to the element that had focus before the first press, as VS Code's Focus Next Part does; in the composer, where chords are suspended (`KEY-1`), Escape returns there instead (`KEY-8`). devhost's surfaces keep that order in the DOM and for Tab: notice stack, bar, pinned log panel, docked chat. While the toolbar is collapsed the chord focuses the collapsed badge; while the page's own modal dialog is open it focuses the notice stack, because the bar is paused (`SURF-11`).
- **KEY-4 The bar is one Tab stop.** The bar follows the toolbar pattern: one Tab stop; ArrowLeft and ArrowRight move between its items and Home and End jump to the ends; Tab leaves it. Every bar item is an arrow stop, the CPU, RAM and Disk readouts included, each a button that opens its details. Enter, Space, or the arrow that points where the panel opens (ArrowUp with the toolbar at the bottom, ArrowDown at the top) opens an item's panel or menu with focus inside (`KEY-6`). The stack name opens the stack name menu with **Move to**, **Collapse** and **Reset to Manifest Default** (`MENU-1`, `SURF-7`), and its accessible name includes the stack name it shows. Each notice's actions and "N more" are Tab stops in the notice stack (`FB-8`).
- **KEY-5 Keys go to whoever the person is typing to.** Keys typed into a devhost text field never reach the page's listeners in any phase, so a page's capture-phase shortcut never acts on them, as `guides/devtools.md` promises: a small classic script, which the document injection puts first in `<head>` as a file of its own (`/__devhost__/keys.js`, same origin as the toolbar's script, so a page's script policy treats both alike), registers window capture listeners for `keydown`, `keypress`, `keyup` and `beforeinput` before any page script; for an event whose `composedPath()[0]` is a devhost text field it stops the original (`stopImmediatePropagation`), dispatches at that field a clone that never leaves the shadow root (`composed: false`), so devhost's own handlers, React's and CodeMirror's included, run on it, and copies the clone's `preventDefault()` onto the original, whose default action, such as typing the character, otherwise proceeds. All text entry stays in devhost's Shadow DOM, because `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby` resolve only within one document and one shadow tree, so a combobox's field and its list, or a field and what names or describes it, never sit in different frames. A focused bar item or notice action keeps only Enter, Space, the arrows, Home, End and Escape, and every other key reaches the page; inside an open panel, menu or modal dialog devhost keeps every key. A panel or menu opened with the pointer returns focus, when it closes, to the page element that had it before the click, so the page's keys work after a click on the toolbar without a click on the page. A surface counts as opened with the pointer only when a `pointerdown` with `pointerType` mouse, pen or touch on its trigger preceded the activation; any other activation, by keyboard or by an assistive technology's click without that `pointerdown`, returns focus to the trigger. Contract: `CC-28`.
- **KEY-6 Focus moves on open and returns on close, never to the body.** On open, focus goes to: a panel's filter or find field when it has one (`SEL-10`, `SURF-10`), else its first control; a menu's first item; an in-place question's safe choice (`REV-4`); the Disable Reloads request (`REV-5`); the crash view's heading, never a button, with the cause sentence as the view's description and the lead action as the next Tab stop (`SURF-3`, `MENU-5`); the Configuration window's text (`SURF-4`); the chat composer when the person opens chat by its control or its chord, or activates either while chat is docked, which then closes only by its own **Close** (`KEY-2`). Nothing that arrives takes focus: a notice, a decision card (`REV-9`) or an outcome (`FB-9`); the crash view, the one surface that opens by itself, takes it only to its heading and only while the person is not typing or using chat (`SURF-3`). On close by any means (Escape, a click away, its own action, the service recovering), focus returns to the control that opened the surface; for a surface that opened by itself (the crash view) or with the pointer (`KEY-5`), to the element that had focus before; when that element is gone, to the surface's trigger, else the Services trigger. After a page reload that devhost caused from one of its controls (**Reload Now**, a switch, Restart Stack on a recovery page), focus starts on that control when the new page shows it, else on the sentence of the outcome that reports it (`FB-2`, read from the operation record), else on the bar item whose surface held it, never at the top of the document; restoring the person's place is not an arrival. After a reload devhost caused that no control started (a turn that changed files, `O57`), focus returns to the devhost element that held it before, the chat composer with its draft, else stays where the browser puts it, because the person was not using devhost (revision 7).
- **KEY-7 Modal surfaces hold Tab; every other surface lets it go.** The crash view and the Configuration window keep Tab cycling inside them and make the page and the bar inert; the Configuration window also makes the notice stack and docked chat inert, while over the crash view both stay operable inside its Tab cycle (`SURF-3`, `SURF-4`). Toolbar panels and anchored popovers are non-modal: Tab moves through their controls in visual order, and Tab past the last control or Shift+Tab before the first closes the panel and returns focus to its trigger, which cancels a question open in it (`REV-4`) and moves its progress to the activity line (`FB-1`). Docked chat is non-modal and stays open when focus leaves it. While the page's own modal dialog is open, chat and the notice stack are inside that dialog's Tab cycle and the bar is paused (`SURF-11`).
- **KEY-8 Escape closes the innermost layer and never answers, dismisses a failure or discards.** One press acts on the innermost layer that holds focus: a list inside a field (a completion list), a menu, an in-place question (cancelled), a nested popover, a panel or anchored popover (the Disable Reloads popover sends nothing, `REV-5`). In the chat composer, Escape leaves an active page-context mode, else returns focus to the page element chat took it from; it never stops a turn, which **Stop Turn** and its own key do (`KEY-9`, `UNDO-4`). In the Configuration window, Escape in the text moves focus to the File list, and Escape anywhere else closes the window with its drafts kept (`SURF-4`). In the crash view it minimizes the view to its notice (`SURF-3`). With focus on the page, it leaves an active page-context mode. Escape never answers an approval or a question (in the Deny note it returns to the card's question), never stops an agent's turn, never dismisses a failure or a condition notice, never discards a draft and never sends. devhost adds no Escape to a library's panel.
- **KEY-9 Enter starts a write only from a button, the composer or the Deny note.** Enter and Space activate the focused button, and in a question focus and Enter start on the safe choice (`REV-4`). In a field, Enter writes only in the chat composer, where Enter sends (during a turn the message joins it, `REV-6`), Shift+Enter inserts a line break, and Enter during input-method composition never sends; and in the Deny note, where Enter denies with the note. Elsewhere Enter never writes: in the worktree find field it picks the highlighted worktree without switching (`REV-2`), in a filter it does nothing more because filters act as the person types (`SEL-10`), in a crop field it applies the value, and in the Disable Reloads request and the configuration text it inserts a line break (`REV-5`). Apart from Enter in a Deny note the person typed, no key answers an approval: **Deny**, **Allow Once**, the agent CLI's standing-approval option (`O12`) and **Stop Turn** act only when pressed (`REV-9`). Stop Turn's own key, Alt+Ctrl+. (`stopTurn`, configurable like `KEY-2`'s chords), acts in the composer and docked chat only during a turn that waits on no decision card; while a card waits it says that the card's **Stop Turn** answers it.
- **KEY-10 Pickers, menus and target lists each have one keyboard model.** A picker (the worktree list, the File list, Chat Settings' harness, model and conversation) is a combobox, its field and list in one shadow tree (`KEY-5`): its find field holds focus when the list can overflow, ArrowUp and ArrowDown move the highlighted option, Enter picks it and Escape closes; options are never several Tab stops and hold no controls: an option's badges and their reasons are text in its accessible description, never shown only on hover (WCAG 1.4.13), and the highlighted option's remedies (**Edit** when the config editor is on or the file and line with **Copy** when it is off, **Copy Error**, **Check Again**; **Open in Editor** removed by `O51`) show in a details line below the list, outside it, headed by that option's label and reason, as the Tab stops after the find field, updated as the highlight moves (`SEL-4`, `MENU-4`). A menu follows the menu pattern: focus starts on its first item, the arrows move, Home and End jump, a letter jumps to the next item that starts with it, Enter or Space activates, and Escape, Tab or Shift+Tab closes it and returns focus as `KEY-6` says: to its trigger when it opened from the keyboard (for the source menu in Select Elements, the Select Elements toggle), and to the element that had focus before when it opened with the pointer (revision 7 withdraws "the element it opened on", which is often not focusable). A menu has no single-letter commands, because type-ahead owns letters: **Copy Path** is reached by typing c. A list of targets with actions (Services rows, the running agents list, the conversation list, stack directory cards) puts its filter first, then each row's actions as Tab stops in row order; rows are not Tab stops, and each action's accessible name names its target ("Restart api"). A decision card is one Tab stop entered on its question, whose arrow keys move between Details and its buttons, including any CLI standing-approval option (`REV-9`, `O12`).
- **KEY-11 A busy or blocked control keeps focus.** A focused control that starts an operation or becomes unavailable keeps focus: it is `aria-disabled`, and `aria-busy` while busy, never `disabled`, and its label says what runs ("Restarting api…") (`FB-9`, `HOLD-1`). After a failure, focus stays on the control while it exists and the failure becomes its accessible description; when the outcome replaces the control, focus moves to the outcome's sentence, never to an action, so a repeated Enter cannot start a second write (`FB-9`). A confirmed question's button stays in place as the busy control and keeps focus (`REV-4`). A key whose action is unavailable reports why (`MENU-3`). Send never takes Stop Turn's place, so a second press after a stop cannot send the draft.
- **KEY-12 Every pointer feature has a keyboard path.** Alt+Ctrl+O or **Logs** on the bar opens the pinned log panel with focus in it; Escape in the panel closes it, and so does the chord while it is open, returning focus per `KEY-6` from inside the panel and leaving it where it is on the page, so a page element the panel covers can be revealed without moving focus (WCAG 2.4.11); Alt+Ctrl+B's cycle reaches the open panel (`KEY-3`, `SURF-13`). **Move to** places the toolbar (`SURF-7`). Each readout opens its details from the bar (`KEY-4`). While Select Elements is latched on (its toggle, or Enter on it), a highlight starts on the element that had focus before devhost took it (`KEY-6`), else the page's main landmark, else the body's first visible element; ArrowUp moves it to its parent, ArrowDown to its first child, ArrowLeft and ArrowRight to its siblings; Enter picks the highlighted element; and Shift+F10 or the context-menu key opens its source menu (`SEL-9`). Focus stays on the toggle, whose accessible description names the highlighted element, and each move is announced politely (`FB-9`) with the element's role, name or text, and position ("button Add to cart · in form Checkout · 2 of 5"). A pick or drawing made with the pointer returns focus to the chat composer, which the pick opened or kept open; a keyboard-latched selection keeps focus on the toggle until Escape (revision 7). Holding Alt selects with the pointer only: arrows pressed while Alt is held do nothing and never reach the browser, whose Alt+Left and Alt+Right go back and forward. Outside that mode, Shift+F10 and the context-menu key stay the page's. Mod+Z undoes and Mod+Shift+Z redoes the last page-context action while Select Elements or a drawing tool is active, or while focus is in devhost outside a text field; everywhere else they stay the app's own undo (`UNDO-7`). Move Chat Panel moves with the arrows and Home returns it, as today. Arrow, Line and Box depend only on their two ends, so none needs a drag: two clicks set the start and the end as a drag does (WCAG 2.5.7), and with a drawing tool active the arrows walk the highlight as in Select Elements, Enter sets the start at the highlighted element and a second Enter the end, Box boxes the highlighted element on one Enter, and Escape cancels the drawing in progress; every drawing is listed in page context by its number and removable from the keyboard.
- **KEY-13 One definition draws each hint, in the platform's notation.** A binding's handler, its hint on the control (`Button`'s `shortcut` prop, outside the accessible name, with `aria-keyshortcuts`), a surface's hint line and the docs come from one definition (`BAR-4`). Hints use the platform's notation: on macOS the glyphs in Apple's order ⌃ ⌥ ⇧ ⌘ (Restart Changed shows ⌃⌥R, Save ⌘S), elsewhere words joined by + (Ctrl+Alt+R, Ctrl+S). A hint names the character the person's keyboard layout puts on the bound physical key, where the browser reports the layout. A key cap carries its key once and is followed only by its outcome ("Esc Finish"), with key and outcome in the accessible text. `guides/devtools.md` gains one Keyboard section listing every binding of this table, and `guides/annotations.md` keeps only the keys of its own tasks. Contract: `CC-29`.
- **KEY-14 Terminal keys are named before they are needed.** In a foreground stack the first Ctrl-C stops the stack gracefully, naming the services still stopping and the time left before devhost forces them, and a second Ctrl-C forces the rest within a second, still releases the stack's routes, port claims and record, and says which services it forced (`FB-10`, `FAIL-5`). `devhost stop` does the same for a detached stack: while it waits, its status line names the services still stopping, the time left and that Ctrl-C forces them, and Ctrl-C, or a second `devhost stop` of the same stack, forces the rest at once, signalling only the verified service process trees (`ACT-11`). Ctrl-C during a detached start's wait cancels the start, stops what it started, and says so as a cancellation, not an error. A serve session's ready line says that Ctrl-C stops it, and on Ctrl-C it names what it removed and what keeps running. Ctrl-C during `devhost caddy download` leaves the previous Caddy binary in place; at the trust step's password prompt it skips trust, leaves Caddy running untrusted and prints `devhost caddy trust` (`REV-8`). A terminal question acts only on y or yes; anything else, an empty answer included, is No (`REV-4`).

| Key | Scope | Action | Owning flow |
| --- | ----- | ------ | ----------- |
| Alt+Ctrl+R (`restartServices`) | Page | Restart Changed's target set (`SEL-2`); when unavailable, a notice says why (`MENU-3`) | services-panel |
| Alt+Ctrl+B (`focusToolbar`), new | Page; Dock; Panel (log panel) | Focus the bar, then the open log panel, then docked chat's composer, then back (`KEY-3`) | toolbar-shell |
| Alt+Ctrl+O (`logs`), new | Page; Panel (log panel) | Open the pinned log panel with focus in it; while open, close it, leaving focus on the page when pressed there (`KEY-12`) | log-minimap |
| Alt+Ctrl+C (`chat`), new | Page | Open docked chat, or focus its composer while docked; Escape there returns focus (`KEY-2`, `KEY-8`) | agent-chat |
| Hold Alt alone (Option on macOS) | Page; Text entry (chat composer) | Select Elements while held, from the first pointer move or after `O34`'s hold; never from a chord or typing (`KEY-2`) | page-context |
| Alt + right-click | Page | The element's source menu, or why it has none (`MENU-3`) | component-source |
| Escape | Mode | Leave Select Elements; cancel the drawing in progress | page-context |
| ArrowUp, ArrowDown, ArrowLeft, ArrowRight, new | Mode (Select Elements latched, a drawing tool) | Highlight the parent, first child, previous or next sibling, announced (`KEY-12`) | page-context |
| Alt+ArrowLeft, Alt+ArrowRight, new | Mode (Alt held) | Nothing; never the browser's Back or Forward (`KEY-12`) | page-context |
| Enter, new | Mode (Select Elements) | Pick the highlighted element | page-context |
| Enter, new | Mode (Arrow, Line, Box) | Set the start at the highlighted element, then the end; Box boxes it on one Enter (`KEY-12`) | page-context |
| Shift+F10, context-menu key, new | Mode (Select Elements) | The highlighted element's source menu | component-source |
| Mod+Z, Mod+Shift+Z, new | Mode; devhost outside text entry | Undo, redo the last page-context action (`UNDO-7`) | page-context |
| ArrowLeft, ArrowRight, Home, End, new | Dock (bar) | Move between bar items, readouts included | toolbar-shell |
| Enter, Space, ArrowUp or ArrowDown toward the panel, new | Dock (bar item) | Open its panel or menu with focus inside | toolbar-shell |
| Tab, Shift+Tab | Dock | Leave the bar; reach the notices in drawn order | toolbar-shell |
| Enter, Space | Every scope, on a focused button | Activate it | toolbar-shell |
| Escape | Panel | Close it; focus returns (`KEY-6`) | toolbar-shell |
| Tab past the last control, Shift+Tab before the first, new | Panel | Close it, cancelling a question open in it; focus returns to its trigger (`KEY-7`, `REV-4`) | toolbar-shell |
| Enter | Panel (in-place question) | The safe choice, which holds focus (`REV-4`) | services-panel |
| Escape | Panel (in-place question) | Cancel; the panel stays open and focus returns to the control that asked (`REV-4`, `KEY-8`) | services-panel |
| Escape | Panel (Disable Reloads popover) | Cancel: nothing is sent (`REV-5`) | reload-control |
| Arrow keys, Home, End, new | Panel (log panel's resize separator) | Resize; Home and End jump to the smallest and largest size (`SURF-13`, revision 7) | log-minimap |
| Escape, new | Panel (log panel) | Close it; focus returns per `KEY-6` (`KEY-12`, revision 7) | log-minimap |
| Escape, new | Page (minimap peek open) | Close the peek; focus stays where it is (revision 7) | log-minimap |
| Enter | Text entry (Disable Reloads request) | Line break; only **Send** sends | reload-control |
| ArrowUp, ArrowDown, new | Text entry (worktree find field) | Move the highlighted worktree | worktree-switch |
| Enter, new | Text entry (worktree find field) | Pick the highlighted worktree; never switch (`REV-2`) | worktree-switch |
| Typing; Enter does nothing more | Text entry (filter fields) | Filter as typed (`SEL-10`) | stack-directory |
| ArrowUp, ArrowDown, Home, End, a letter | Menu | Move, jump, jump by first letter (`KEY-10`) | toolbar-shell |
| Escape, Tab, Shift+Tab | Menu | Close; focus returns to the trigger | toolbar-shell |
| Escape | Modal (crash view) | Minimize to the crash notice; the failure stays (`SURF-3`) | startup-and-recovery |
| Tab, Shift+Tab | Modal (crash view) | Cycle its controls | startup-and-recovery |
| Tab, Shift+Tab | Modal (Configuration window, outside the text) | Cycle its controls | config-editor |
| Escape | Modal (Configuration window, in the text) | Focus the File list; a second Escape closes | config-editor |
| Escape | Modal (Configuration window, elsewhere) | Close with drafts kept; focus returns | config-editor |
| Mod+S | Modal (Configuration window) | Save the open file | config-editor |
| Enter, new | Text entry (configuration text) | Line break; never saves (revision 7) | config-editor |
| Mod+Z, Mod+Shift+Z, new | Text entry (configuration text) | Undo, redo in the text (revision 7) | config-editor |
| Tab, Shift+Tab | Text entry (configuration text) | Indent, outdent | config-editor |
| Ctrl+M (Shift+Alt+M on macOS) | Text entry (configuration text) | Toggle whether Tab moves focus, named in the window's hint | config-editor |
| Ctrl+Space (Option+I on macOS) | Text entry (configuration text) | List completions | config-editor |
| Enter | Text entry (chat composer) | Send; during a turn, join it (`REV-6`) | agent-chat |
| Shift+Enter | Text entry (chat composer, Deny note) | Line break | agent-chat |
| Escape | Text entry (chat composer) | Leave a page-context mode; else focus the page; never stops a turn (`KEY-8`) | agent-chat |
| Alt+Ctrl+. (`stopTurn`), new | Text entry (chat composer); Chat | Stop Turn while no decision card waits (`KEY-9`) | agent-chat |
| Enter, new | Text entry (Deny note) | Deny with the note (`REV-9`) | agent-chat |
| Escape, new | Text entry (Deny note) | Back to the card's question, nothing answered | agent-chat |
| Tab, Shift+Tab; arrow keys, new | Chat (decision card) | Enter the card on its question; move between Details, **Deny**, **Allow Once**, any CLI standing-approval option (`O12`) and **Stop Turn**; Tab leaves (`REV-9`) | agent-chat |
| Arrow keys, Home | Chat (Move Chat Panel) | Move the panel; return it to its side | agent-chat |
| Arrow keys, Enter | Text entry (crop fields) | Adjust and apply the area; never share | page-context |
| Escape | Text entry (crop fields) | Cancel the send; the draft and context stay | page-context |
| Ctrl-C, first | Terminal (foreground stack) | Stop gracefully, naming what still stops and the time left (`KEY-14`) | stack-run-stop |
| Ctrl-C, second | Terminal (foreground stack) | Force the rest within a second | stack-run-stop |
| Ctrl-C | Terminal (detached start's wait) | Cancel the start and stop what it started | stack-run-stop |
| Ctrl-C, new | Terminal (`devhost stop` waiting) | Force the services still stopping; a second `devhost stop` does the same (`KEY-14`) | stack-run-stop |
| Ctrl-C | Terminal (serve session) | Stop the session; Caddy and routed servers keep running | serve-folder |
| Ctrl-C | Terminal (`devhost caddy download`, the trust prompt) | Cancel the step: the old binary stays; trust is skipped | first-run-setup |
| y or yes; anything else | Terminal (a question) | Act; No (`REV-4`, `REV-7`) | stack-run-stop |
| Alt+Shift+D | Library (Vue DevTools) | Show or hide its dock; devhost never binds it | third-party-devtools |
| Ctrl+~ | Library (TanStack Devtools) | Open its panel; devhost never binds it, and launches the library without its source inspector, which opens an editor (`O51`, revision 7) | third-party-devtools |
| Escape | Library | The library's own; devhost adds none | third-party-devtools |

Collisions resolved by this table: Escape in the chat composer (stop the turn, agent-chat; leave the mode, page-context) leaves the mode, else returns to the page, and never stops a turn (`keyboard-and-screen-reader-06`); Stop Turn takes Alt+Ctrl+., because Mod+Shift+Backspace is Clear Browsing Data in Chrome and Firefox on macOS, and Shift+Escape and Ctrl+Shift+Escape open Chrome's and Windows' task managers; Escape in the crash view (blocked today; minimize, startup-and-recovery) minimizes and never dismisses; Escape in the Configuration window (close at once, notices-and-feedback; leave the text first, config-editor) leaves the text first, then closes; Escape on a library's panel stays the library's (`third-party-devtools-02` dropped a devhost-wide Escape); Escape on a decision card answers nothing. Enter in the worktree find field (switch, worktree-switch) picks without switching, and Enter in a question (confirm Restart Stack, services-panel) takes the safe choice (`REV-2`, `REV-4`); Enter in the Deny note denies, because the person chose Deny; Enter in the Disable Reloads request inserts a line break. The focus chord (Alt+Ctrl+D, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-05`; Alt+Shift+D, `toolbar-shell-06`) is Alt+Ctrl+B, because Alt+Shift+D is Vue DevTools' dock toggle and Ctrl+Alt+D shows the desktop on Xfce and MATE; the log chord (Alt+Shift+L, `source-and-minimap-07`) is Alt+Ctrl+O, in devhost's family, because Ctrl+Alt+L locks the screen on KDE Plasma and Xfce. On Windows, Magnifier reserves Ctrl+Alt+D, F, L, M and R, and Enter, H and K for reading (Microsoft's Magnifier keyboard shortcuts), so of devhost's chords only the documented Alt+Ctrl+R collides, and only while Magnifier runs (`KEY-2`, `O17`); Ctrl+Alt is AltGr there, which types { on B in Hungarian, Czech, Slovak and Croatian layouts and ó on O in Polish (Programmers), so a chord event carrying another printable character is typing (`KEY-2`). Mod+Z (page-context's undo; the app's undo) is page context's only in a mode or with focus in devhost. Chat's chord (none today; a direct chord, agent-chat) is Alt+Ctrl+C, which none of the browsers, desktops and libraries named here reserves; it acts only outside text entry, so AltGr+C still types its character in a field. Mod+P for the File list (config-editor) stays the browser's Print; Escape from the text reaches the File list. A single c to copy a source path (component-source) gives way to type-ahead. A chord that starts with Alt, or a character typed with Option, no longer starts selection (`KEY-2`). Tab in the worktree list (never leaves, worktree-switch; leaves the toolbar, toolbar-shell) moves from the find field to the highlighted row's remedies, and past the panel's last control closes the panel and returns to its trigger, and Tab from the bar leaves the toolbar. The readouts (Tab stops, `toolbar-shell-04`; one Tab stop, `toolbar-shell-06`) are arrow stops. The crash view's first focus (its summary; its lead action) is its heading, described by the cause, never a button, and an exit while the person types or uses chat opens the view minimized (`SURF-3`). Focus after a failed restart (stays, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-01`; moves to the error, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-03`) stays while the control exists. The keyboard after a click on the toolbar (kept until the page is clicked, as documented; given back, `toolbar-shell-06`) is given back (`CC-28`). Hint notation (glyphs, config-editor; words, today's hints) follows the platform, and a hint goes in `Button`'s `shortcut` prop, not the label (agent-chat's "Stop (Esc)"). Shift+F10 and the context-menu key are devhost's only in Select Elements. Mod+S is Save only inside the Configuration window.

Rationale: The WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices give one Tab stop and arrow keys to a toolbar, a combobox and a menu, move focus into a dialog and return it to the invoker, trap Tab only in a modal dialog, and prefer `aria-disabled` for a control that must stay focusable; WCAG 2.1.1 asks for a keyboard path for everything except input that depends on the path of a movement, not merely its ends, 2.5.7 for a single-pointer way to do what a drag does, 2.1.4 keeps single-character keys to their component, 2.4.3 asks for a meaningful focus order, and 2.5.3 puts the visible name in the accessible name. VS Code keeps one keybinding registry that draws every hint per platform, returns focus from a widget, closes only the topmost widget on Escape, and toggles Tab between indentation and focus with Ctrl+M, as CodeMirror does; JetBrains returns from a tool window to the editor with Escape; Chrome DevTools picks elements with the keyboard and walks the DOM with the arrows; Claude Code interrupts with Escape and Cursor sends on Enter; Docker Compose names Ctrl-C's force step and forces on the second press; browser devtools run in their own frame so a page never sees their keys, and Vue DevTools and TanStack Devtools make their hotkeys configurable. Today devhost's three page-wide gestures sit on capture listeners (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/components/App.tsx:209-235`, `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/annotationComposer/hooks/usePageSelection.ts:225-258`, `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/componentSourceNavigation/hooks/useComponentSourceNavigation.ts:70-101`); keys stop only at the shadow root, after the page's capture listeners ran (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/shared/components/DevtoolsTopLayer.tsx:63-77`); the bar declares `role="toolbar"` without arrow keys (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/shared/components/DevtoolsToolbar.tsx:58-63`); Escape closes the Configuration window from its text, leaving focus on the body (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/configEditor/components/ConfigEditorPanel.tsx:111-116`); and the composer's Escape and the selection Escape both listen on the document in capture (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/agentChat/components/PageChat.tsx:106-118`). The default chords avoid the reservations named in the collisions paragraph; on macOS Control+Option is VoiceOver's modifier, so every Alt+Ctrl chord goes to VoiceOver while it runs, which is why no action is chord-only. Findings: `keyboard-and-screen-reader-05`, `toolbar-shell-06`, `toolbar-shell-04`, `toolbar-shell-02`, `toolbar-shell-09` (the dock and the bar); `toolbar-shell-08` (configured chords); `keyboard-and-screen-reader-06`, `chat-compose-2-02`, `chat-compose-2-03`, `page-context-08` (Escape and Enter in the composer); `journey-crash-to-fix-01`, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-01`, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-03`, `config-editor-10`, `notices-and-feedback-01` (modal surfaces and focus); `worktree-switch-10`, `chat-checkout-switch-02`, `services-panel-2-03`, `chat-approvals-01`, `chat-approvals-04`, `chat-approvals-07` (Enter and defaults); `source-and-minimap-07`, `source-and-minimap-02`, `page-context-06`, `page-context-04`, `stack-scale-05`, `stack-directory-07` (lists, menus and pointer-only features); `services-panel-2-02` (the restart chord); `interrupted-operations-03`, `stack-run-stop-04`, `serve-folder-02`, `first-run-setup-08` (terminal keys).

Use it for every key devhost binds or must leave alone, in the browser and the terminal, and for focus order, focus movement and containment. Do not use it for what an action does once started (2.5 to 2.8) or for the words of hints and labels (2.12).

States: each scope with its keys active and suspended; the bar with focus on an item, on a readout's details and collapsed; the dock focused by the chord, the cycle through the open log panel and docked chat's composer, and the return; **Chat** pressed while chat is docked; a panel opened by keyboard, by pointer and by a screen reader's click (focus returning to its trigger), closed by Escape, by Tab past its last control and by its action; a menu with type-ahead; an in-place question with focus on its safe choice; the crash view opened with focus on its heading, opened minimized while the person types in the composer, minimized by Escape and reopened; the Configuration window with focus in the text, on the File list and closed; chat opened by its chord; the composer with Escape in a mode and, during a turn, outside one; Stop Turn's key during a turn and while a card waits; the Deny note; Select Elements walked by arrow keys, each move announced, and picked with Enter; a shape drawn by keyboard and by two clicks; page context undone with Mod+Z; a busy control holding focus and a failure as its description; focus after confirming a question, on the busy confirm button and then on the outcome's sentence; a focused control that becomes unavailable keeping focus, in a menu and on the bar; focus after a reload devhost caused, on its control or its outcome; a chord VoiceOver takes; AltGr+B and AltGr+O typed into a page's editing widget; characters typed with Option in the composer, and Alt held with the pointer still and moving; an invalid configured chord reported; the terminal's first and second Ctrl-C, a cancelled detached start, a serve session, a cancelled download and a skipped trust prompt.

Keys: this section is the map; every other section's `Keys:` paragraph points here.

Accessibility: the dock is a landmark named "devhost" (`complementary`); the bar is `role="toolbar"` with roving `tabindex`; pickers are comboboxes with `aria-activedescendant`, their field and list in one shadow tree (`KEY-5`), menus are `role="menu"`, the crash view and the Configuration window are modal dialogs; every control with a binding exposes it in `aria-keyshortcuts`; focus is always visible and never lands on the body; key caps are visually distinct and read as the key name followed by its outcome.

Backend contract: the manifest schema and its validation accept `focusToolbar`, `logs`, `chat` and `stopTurn` under `[devtools.shortcuts]`, accept every key name the client matches, and report a value they cannot use as a warning with its file, line and key (`apps/devhost/src/manifest/validation.rs`); the devhost config file accepts the person's own bindings (`O17`); the stack's shutdown names the services still stopping with their remaining time and forces on a second signal or a second stop request (`KEY-14`); `devhost caddy download` replaces the binary only after a complete download; the document injection puts a small classic script, `/__devhost__/keys.js`, served by the control server like the toolbar's own script, first in `<head>`, before any page script, beside today's module script before `</body>` (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/document_injection.rs`) (`KEY-5`).

Component primitive: `@base-ui/react`'s Toolbar for the bar's roving focus, with `Toolbar.Button` and `focusableWhenDisabled` for bar items, and Combobox for pickers; menus as 2.3 names them, with type-ahead for `KEY-10`; native `<dialog>` with `showModal()` for the two modal surfaces (`SURF-3`, `SURF-4`); `Button`'s `shortcut` prop and the shared `Kbd` for hints; one binding registry under `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/` from which handlers, hints and `aria-keyshortcuts` read; the early script of `KEY-5`, which stops keys typed into devhost's text entry at the window and re-dispatches a clone that stays inside the shadow root, copying its `preventDefault()` onto the original, while that entry stays in the Shadow DOM; CodeMirror's keymap for the configuration text; library work: `Button`'s unavailable state (2.3).

Contract change: `CC-28` (keys after a click on the toolbar, keys typed into devhost), `CC-29` (the Keyboard section, the new chords and their reporting, Escape in the composer); `CC-2` (Escape minimizes the crash view, whose focus starts on its heading, from 2.4), `CC-35` (Ctrl-C forces a waiting `devhost stop`, from 2.10); roster TB-02, TB-04, TB-05, SVC-03, REC-03, LOG-01, CTX-01, CTX-02, CTX-04, SRC-01, CFG-02, CHAT-02, CHAT-03, CHAT-04, CHAT-06, EXT-01, EXT-03, STK-01, STK-02, SRV-01, SET-02, SET-03.

### 2.12 Wording and naming

The words every surface uses: the glossary (`WORD-1`) extends `CONTEXT.md` with the nouns the flows disagree on; then labels, capitalization, sentences, counts, state words, failures, formats, the two terminal outputs, the text devhost writes on the person's behalf, and the worktree vocabulary rule (`WORD-11`). Exact words in other sections' examples follow these rules.

Rules:

- **WORD-1 Glossary.** Every surface, both terminal outputs and the docs use the words in the first column with the meaning given, and never the words in the last column for that meaning. Terms `CONTEXT.md` lacks are added to it (`CC-32`).

| Use | Meaning | Never |
| --- | ------- | ----- |
| stack | One running devhost unit described by a root manifest and its includes, named by its manifest's `name`, plus its worktree label (`SEL-4`) when two stacks share a name; its PID is a detail | project, instance, app, process, stack process |
| serve session | A manifest-free `devhost serve` session, named by its folder or port | stack, project, primary service |
| service | A process or route a stack declares | app (for a service), process (except its PID) |
| external service | A service with `managed = false`, which runs outside devhost and which devhost only checks | unmanaged |
| manifest | A stack's complete configuration, merged from its manifest files | config, settings file, configuration (for the manifest alone) |
| manifest file | One TOML file of a manifest: the root manifest or an include | config file, toml |
| accepted manifest | The manifest the stack runs; it differs from the files only after a change was not applied | previous configuration, current config, active config, last good config |
| devhost config file | The one `config.toml` per machine, holding the shared Caddy settings, the person's own key bindings (`[devtools.shortcuts]`, `O17`), the machine name (`machineName`, `O61`), the default `stopTimeout` (`O30`) and the shared-domain passkey public key and `rpId` (`O36`; `SEL-11`, `CC-39`; editor settings removed by `O51`) | global config, global manifest, settings file |
| shared Caddy settings | The listener settings every stack on the machine shares, set only in the devhost config file | global config, Caddy config, proxy config |
