| configuration | Manifest files and the devhost config file together: what **Config** and the Configuration window edit | settings; never for the manifest alone |
| hot reload; applied, not applied | Applying a saved manifest file or devhost config file without stopping the stack or Caddy; its outcome is applied or not applied | live reload, refresh, restart, configuration reloaded, reload rejected, Saved and applied |
| reloads | What the page's own dev server does when files change (hot module replacement and the page reloads it triggers), which the hot reload notice, **Hold Reloads**, **Reload blocked** and **Disable Reloads** concern | hot reload, hot reloading; HMR, except in the Disable Reloads request, which names the framework's own settings |
| worktree; Worktrees | Every working copy a repository's services run from, the main working copy and any linked Git worktree alike (`WORD-11`); **Worktrees** names the feature and the panel that switch worktrees | checkout (as a noun), folder, directory, ".", Unavailable worktree |
| switch | Moving one repository of the stack to another worktree, which restarts the stack | change worktree, Switch worktree, checkout (as a verb) |
| Restart Stack | Restarting every managed service inside the running stack, with new automatic ports | stack restart, Restart all |
| replace | What `devhost stack restart`, `devhost restart-all` and `devhost upgrade` do: a new devhost process takes over the stack (`O18`, decided 2026-11-25; `devhost stack replace` rename withdrawn); help and output state clearly that they replace the running devhost supervisor process | ambiguous "restarts services" wording (for a process replacement) |
| stop; force-stop | Ending a stack, service, turn or session on purpose; one devhost had to kill after its grace time is force-stopped | kill, crash, exit, fail (for a stop someone asked for) |
| conversation | One chat thread with an agent, named by its first message | session, chat session, thread |
| agent | A coding agent CLI, named by its product name: Claude Code, Codex | harness (in visible text), claude-code and other IDs |
| page context | What a message carries from the page: marks, drawings, a screenshot and the page's details | annotations (as its label), selection |
| edit mode | How the agent handles a message: **This worktree (live)** or **Make tickets** (`O1`, updated 2026-11-18 by q10a6; **New worktree (review before merge)** removed) | workflow, agent workflow, Project, This Project, New worktree |
| notice | An item in the toolbar's notice stack | toast, banner, alert |
| lost connection | The page no longer receives its stack's state (`ACT-5`) | Disconnected (as a title), offline (except for the proxy), down |
| status page | devhost's own page on a stack's hostname: starting, recovery, not found, stopped stack, file listing | error page (for devhost's own), splash page |
| stack directory | The page listing the stacks of one machine, opened by **All Stacks on devbox** | dashboard, stacks page |
| machine name | The name every surface gives the machine that runs the stacks, the same for every reader: `machineName` in the devhost config file, else the host name up to its first dot (`O61`); devbox in examples. Terminal text keeps "this machine" for the machine a command runs on | this machine, the local machine, this computer, host (in the browser, for the stacks' machine) |
| manifest files | The list of a stack's manifest files, on its stack directory card and in the Configuration window's File list (revision 7) | includes, config files |
| Needs you | The group that leads a stack summary with what waits for the person (`ACT-6`; revision 7) | attention, alerts, problems |

- **WORD-2 One label per operation, naming its target.** An operation has one label on every surface, its terminal command names it the same way where one exists (`MENU-6`), and its outcome uses the same verb in the past tense. A label is a verb with its target or count: **Restart** (a row), **Restart api** (crash view, status page), **Restart 2 Changed** or the fallback **Restart docs** (`SEL-2`), **Restart Stack** (its count in `REV-2`'s statement or reach line, `BAR-2`), **Start**, **Start 5 Services** for every not-started service, **Cancel Restart** and **Cancel Start** for a queued restart or start (`SEL-7`), whose accessible name adds the target ("Cancel Restart of worker") and whose outcome reads "Cancelled the queued restart of worker", **Switch and Restart Stack**, **Return to Configured Worktree**, **Stop Stack** (another stack), **Stop Turn** (the composer and the decision card alike), **Close Conversation**, **New Conversation**, **Disable Reloads**, **Hold Reloads**, **Reload Now**, **Reload Page** (on a turn summary after reloads are disabled, `O56`), **Check Again**, **Refresh Worktrees**, **Retry Now**, **Copy Error**, **Edit**, **Minimize**, **Logs**, **Show All Output** (a banner's output in place), **Jump to Latest** (a log's newest line, with the count of new lines), **Move to**, **Reset to Manifest Default**, **All Stacks on devbox** (the machine name, `O61`), and from revision 7 **Expand** (reopens a minimized crash view, paired with **Minimize**), **Retry**, **Show All N Characters** (a folded long line), **Sign In with Passkey**, **Save Anyway**, **Open Conversation** (opens a conversation in docked chat, never "Open Chat"), **Show Changes**, **Answer**, **Refresh Stacks** and **Copy** (copies the text beside it, such as a location `devhost.toml:12:8` or a command; with the config editor off it stands beside **Copy Error**, `KEY-10`); **Apply devhost.toml** names the hot reload's operation, which no control carries ("Applying devhost.toml · 1 of 2 restarted · 4 s"); **Reload Now** is the one label that applies a held or blocked reload, and a bare **Reload** is retired; **Open in Editor** is removed (`O51`). A question's choices are verbs too (`REV-4`): **Answer First**, **Switch Anyway**, **Review Drafts**, **Start Second Agent**, **Select Again**, **Remove**, **Send Anyway**, **Compare**, **Keep Mine**, **Use Disk Version**, **Use Other Version**, **Show Differences**, **Restore Running Version**, **Continue**, **Resume**, **Show Reload Notice**, and **Cancel** only for a question's choice that changes nothing. A filter that hides part of a set adds a line under the label, never changes the verb: **Start 3 Services** with "2 more hidden by the filter, not included" (`BAR-3`). `devhost upgrade` downloads the latest release, verifies checksums, swaps a user-writable binary (clearly reporting read-only paths or externally managed installs without breaking them) and replaces running stacks; shipped `devhost restart-all` and project-scoped `devhost stack restart` keep one verb for dotfiles, package managers and local rebuilds while stating clearly in help, output and docs that they replace the running devhost supervisor process (`O18`, decided 2026-11-25, superseding the `devhost stack replace` rename). `devhost stack info` and its short alias `devhost status` share one command and output contract (`O62`).
- **WORD-3 Capitalization, names and voice.** Action labels (buttons, menu items, toolbar segments, links that act) use title-style capitalization, the UI package's convention: every word capitalized except a, an, and, as, at, by, for, in, of, on, or, the and to after the first word (**Switch and Restart Stack**, **Move to**). Everything else is sentence case: options in a choice (the edit modes keep the owner's words, **This worktree (live)**), headings, notice titles, hints, descriptions, state words, outcome sentences, errors and terminal lines. Names keep their own spelling: devhost always lowercase, at a sentence start too ("devhost config file not applied", revision 7), a third-party library by its product name, never by the old launcher labels "Redux" or "TanStack" (revision 7), Caddy, Claude Code, Codex, a service or stack as declared, a file as named. A sentence makes devhost its subject only when devhost did something a reader could blame on someone else ("devhost stopped api for Restart Stack"); otherwise the target is the subject ("api exited with code 1").
- **WORD-4 One grammar for progress and outcomes.** A busy control reads its verb in progress and its target ("Restarting api…"). An activity line or a terminal status line reads the operation, its progress and the elapsed time joined by " · " ("Restart Stack · 2 of 6 started · 5 s", `FB-1`), and a control it blocks gives the reason "Restart Stack is running · 3 of 6 started" (`HOLD-3`). An outcome is one sentence, verb first in the past tense, the target by name, then details and time after " · " ("Restarted api · 2.6 s"; "Applied devhost.toml: restarted api (changed) and worker (depends on api) · 6 of 6 running · 3.1 s", `FB-3`); a partial result leads with its count and names each failure ("Restart Stack: 5 of 6 running · api exited with code 1 before its health check", `FAIL-1`). An operation from elsewhere ends with its origin as `ACT-1` sees it: "· from another tab", "· from the terminal", "· from an agent", or, for an agent devhost started, "· from Claude Code, conversation Fix the nav", and "· after devhost.toml changed on disk" (`FB-7`). The reconnection reads "Connected again · 6 of 6 running" and a shutdown "Stopping Acme Shop · 3 of 6 stopped" (`ACT-5`, `ACT-3`); a start ends with "Ready in 4.2 s: https://shop.localhost/" before its other URLs (`FB-10`). Chat's decision and stop lines read "You denied: Edit header.tsx · 13:23" and "You stopped this turn after 29 s · 12 tool calls ran" (`REV-9`, `FAIL-5`). A restore reads "Restored devhost.toml to the version Acme Shop runs"; an outcome shows a duration only when the operation waited on something, so a reload that restarted nothing reads "Reloaded devtools settings; nothing restarted" (revision 7). Targets are named, never identified by an ID: services and stacks by name, worktrees by label (`SEL-4`), conversations by title, agents by product name.
- **WORD-5 Counts say what they count, out of what.** Services count against every service of the stack (`ACT-6`), states that need the person first: "3 of 6 running · 1 failed · 2 not started"; a changed service counts as running. Visible text spells "of"; only the Services trigger's fixed-width summary shows "3/6", and its accessible name still spells it (`SURF-10`). Plurals are written out ("1 stack", "2 stacks"), never "(s)" or "process(es)". A set action's count goes in its label ("Restart 2 Changed"), except a stack-wide write's, which keeps its name and states the count in `REV-2`'s statement or reach line ("Restart all 6 services …", `BAR-2`); progress counts what is done of the whole ("3 of 6 started"); the terminal collapses a long list to its count and the command that lists it ("34 services not started · devhost status").
- **WORD-6 One word per state, on every surface and in both outputs.** Each published state (`ACT-2`, `ACT-6`) has one word, followed by its cause or time where the state has one:

  | State | Word | Never |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | running | running | up, ready, healthy, active |
  | starting | starting, with the check and its time ("starting · health check 4 s of 60 s") | down, waiting, pending |
  | restarting, stopping | restarting, stopping | down |
  | failed | failed, with its cause ("failed its health check", "could not start: …") | down, error, crashed |
  | exited | exited, with its code or signal and time ("exited with code 1 at 14:02") | down, crashed, died |
  | blocked | blocked, with its worktree's reason | unavailable, down |
  | not started | not started, with why ("left out of this run", "stopped by you") | stopped (for a left-out service), down |
  | reachable, not reachable | an external service's check, with its time | up, down, available, unavailable |
  | changed | changed, with its files | modified, dirty, files changed |
  | unknown | unknown, with when it was last seen ("last seen 14:02") | down, unavailable, disconnected |
  | stack states | stopped, stopping, proxy offline, failed, starting, changing, running; unknown, for a stack whose devhost process does not answer (revision 7) | down, crashed, partial, idle |
  | conversation and agent states | working, waiting for you, idle, closed; state not reported, for an agent whose CLI records none (revision 7) | waiting for approval, waiting for your response, requires_action |

  A state word is capitalized only where it starts a badge or a title ("Exited"); agent output and `devhost stack info --json` (`devhost status --json`, `O62`) use it as a lowercase identifier with hyphens (`not-started`, `not-reachable`).
- **WORD-7 Failures, stops and refusals are worded as what they are.** A failure leads with the target and what happened in plain words, then the cause, then where to fix it, each once ("devhost.toml line 12, column 8, services.api.port: expected a number. The stack still runs the accepted manifest.", `FAIL-2`); advice fits the cause and never suggests what cannot help, such as a restart for an external service (`MENU-5`); the original text stays behind **Copy Error**. A deliberate end reads as a stop: "Stopped by you", "Stopped for Restart Stack", "Force-stopped after 10 s: it ignored SIGTERM", "Terminated by SIGTERM (143) at 21:53:34, not sent by devhost", "Replaced by PID 85102" (`FAIL-5`); a cancelled question leaves "Not sent", and a closed tab picker leaves nothing. A refusal names what refused it and what to do: "api is already restarting", "Not saved: the file changed on disk", "Sign in with passkey or pair this browser to use devhost here" (`HOLD-7`, `O36`; with `O54` decided, an authenticated remote browser saves, so no refusal names this machine). A stop someone asked for never reads failed or error or takes the failure style, and a denial never takes the success style.
- **WORD-8 One format for paths, times, sizes and exits.** Paths shorten the home folder to `~`; a manifest problem's file is relative to the root manifest's folder, written "devhost.toml line 12" in sentences and "devhost.toml:12:8" in the terminal and in **Copy Error**; a service's folder appears only when it differs from the stack's, relative to it; a path appears once per message, and in the browser a long one is shortened in the middle with the full path in its tooltip and accessible name. Durations use one decimal under 10 s ("2.6 s"), whole units above ("14 s", "2 min 40 s", "1 h 5 min"), and a limit as "4 s of 60 s"; a manifest value set in milliseconds shows in seconds. Clock times are local and 24-hour ("14:02", with seconds where order matters, "21:53:34"), with a relative age where it helps ("started 14:02 · 12 min ago"); ISO times appear only in agent output and copyable details. Exits read "exited with code 1"; an exit by a signal names the signal, then the status a shell reports for it, 128 plus the signal's number ("terminated by SIGTERM (143)"), and a stop devhost made reads as that stop (`WORD-7`). File sizes use binary units ("8 MiB"). A certificate fingerprint is the SHA-256 of the DER certificate, shown whole as colon-separated uppercase pairs, and a certificate's expiry is a date, 2027-11-25 (revision 7). The terminal never breaks a URL, path or command across lines, prints a command to run on its own line without trailing punctuation, and links URLs with OSC 8 where the terminal supports it.
- **WORD-9 Human output and agent output differ by design, and report the same facts.** Both report every outcome and state with the same exit codes (`FB-11`). Human output names stacks, services and worktrees (`SEL-4`), keeps PIDs and absolute paths as details, shortens paths with `~`, collapses long lists (`WORD-5`) and prints the next step as a command. Agent output keeps the whole error on one `devhost: ERR:` line, absolute paths, PIDs and identifiers, and where only a person can act it tells the agent to ask the person ("Ask the person to run devhost caddy trust in a terminal", `REV-8`). Both say before a step that it downloads, with the size (when the server sends no length, the size shows as it arrives and in the outcome, revision 7), or asks for a password. Lines start with a capital letter unless they start with a name that keeps its own case, such as devhost, and only sentences end with a period. Contract: `CC-30`.
- **WORD-10 Text devhost writes for the person shows as devhost's.** The edit modes are labelled as the owner named them, and each sent message shows its mode and the worktree it edited under it ("Mode: This worktree (live) · fix-nav"), with the instructions devhost added under a collapsed "Added by devhost", never inside the person's own message (`O1`, `UNDO-8`, `WORD-11`). The Disable Reloads popover's title names the agent ("Ask Claude Code to turn off reloads?"), and its request starts with what it asks and where ("Turn off automatic reloads in this worktree (~/acme) for local development:"), then the framework's own settings (Vite's `server.hmr`, Bun's `development.hmr`) (`O2`, `REV-5`). Chat says "this worktree", never "this project" or "this checkout", for where an agent edits, and the mode control follows the owner's label with the `SEL-4` label of that worktree (`SEL-8`). Text the agent CLI adds shows as what it is ("Skill loaded: programming"), a turn the agent started by itself reads "The agent continued on its own", and Watch Live Activity labels its entries Reply, Tool call, Result, Denied, Prompt (the person's message) and Stopped (a turn the person stopped; revision 7).
- **WORD-11 Worktree names every working copy, main's included.** From the product owner's comment of 2026-11-18 on owner item q17 (“checkouts? why do we need checkouts? don't we simply serve from different paths where worktrees are?”): every working copy a repository's services run from is a **worktree**, the repository's main working copy and any linked Git worktree alike, and never "checkout" as a noun. Every page, panel, notice, status page, terminal line, doc and design uses "worktree", "this worktree" and "worktree root" (`WORD-1`, `WORD-2`, `WORD-10`, `SEL-4`); **This worktree (live)**, **Return to Configured Worktree** and `"No agents in this worktree"` follow this rule. Exceptions: the Git subcommand `git checkout`, app example URLs or form names (`hotfix-shop.shop.localhost/checkout`, `form Checkout`), existing finding IDs (`chat-checkout-switch-01` to `chat-checkout-switch-04`), and quoted existing product or documentation strings that Phase 8 replaces when their flows land: `stacks/recovery.md` ("with the checkout picker", `CC-4`), `guides/annotations.md` ("working or waiting in this checkout", `CC-14`), `guides/devtools.md` ("running in this checkout", `CC-33`), `reference/chat-api.md` ("send into a checkout the page has switched away from", `CC-38`) and `stacks/worktrees.md` ("If the running checkout was deleted", `O43`). Contract: `CC-31`, `CC-32`.
- **WORD-12 One proxy line and one trust sentence.** Every command that reports the shared proxy prints one line: "Proxy: HTTPS on port 443, every interface, reachable from your network · ~/.config/devhost/config.toml"; on a loopback listener "Proxy: HTTPS on 127.0.0.1:4443 and [::1]:4443, direct connections from this machine only; relay previews and SSH forwards still reach it · ~/.config/devhost/config.toml"; with plain HTTP on, " and HTTP on …" follows the HTTPS part; the macOS warning of `O39` follows on the next line when it applies. Browser surfaces say "devbox's network" (`O61`) for "your network". Where the machine does not trust devhost's certificate authority, every surface uses one sentence: "devbox does not trust devhost's local certificate authority, so browsers warn on each devhost hostname and block a page's requests to another one. On devbox, run devhost caddy trust in a terminal, then reload; on another Mac, run devhost caddy trust-remote with devbox's SSH name there."; a full page puts its heading first and this line under it (`FB-9`). Contract: `CC-43`, `CC-48` (revision 7).

Rationale: The glossary in `CONTEXT.md` is a binding contract, so `WORD-1` keeps its words and adds the nouns it lacks; the language audit found the same thing called stack, project and process, "checkout" and "worktree", conversation and session, and the same state called up, running and ready on different surfaces, and on 2026-11-18 the product owner unified every working copy under "worktree" (`WORD-11`). The UI package already capitalizes action labels (`packages/devhost-ui/AGENTS.md`), and Apple's Human Interface Guidelines use title style for buttons and sentence case elsewhere; the owner's own labels (**Disable Reloads**, **Hold Reloads**, the edit modes) set the rest. Docker Desktop and `docker compose` name a unit by its project name and use Running, Exited (code) and Restarting with one verb for one target in CLI and UI; Vite prints "ready in"; rustc and TypeScript print file:line:column; VS Code shows workspace-relative paths with the full path on hover; Claude Code marks an interrupt neutrally; the GitHub CLI and Docker keep machine output beside human tables. Today the CLI reports a reload as "configuration reloaded:" while the toolbar says "Manifest change not applied" (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:646`, `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/configEditor/components/ConfigRejectionNotice.tsx:13`); the stack directory tells people to start "a project" (`apps/devhost/src/statuspage.rs:147-157`); conversations are listed by a 32-character ID (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/agentChat/formatChatSessionLabel.ts:4`); a stopped turn reads "Native Claude turn failed" (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/chat/session.rs:444-456`); and `reference/output.md` says the two outputs differ only in layout while agent output already keeps the whole error (`apps/devhost/src/cliout.rs:234-261`). Findings: `stack-run-stop-06`, `worktree-switch-07`, `stack-directory-03`, `serve-folder-02`, `serve-folder-03` (stack and session nouns); `config-editor-01`, `proxy-settings-04`, `manifest-hot-reload-02`, `journey-crash-to-fix-03` (configuration nouns); `worktree-switch-03`, `worktree-switch-04`, `reload-and-agents-05` (worktrees); `chat-conversations-01`, `chat-conversations-05`, `chat-approvals-02`, `chat-approvals-04`, `journey-agent-fix-05`, `chat-conversations-06`, `chat-approvals-06`, `reload-and-agents-06` (chat nouns); `agent-cli-journey-03`, `services-panel-2-02`, `stack-run-stop-02`, `chat-approvals-01` (one name per operation); `manifest-hot-reload-01`, `manifest-hot-reload-04`, `config-editor-05`, `config-editor-06`, `config-editor-12`, `stack-directory-05`, `notices-and-feedback-03` (one event, one name); `config-editor-03`, `worktree-switch-02`, `stack-run-stop-09`, `journey-new-project-04`, `error-pages-07`, `page-context-09`, `journey-new-project-02`, `first-run-setup-06`, `keyboard-and-screen-reader-03` (raw text); `manifest-hot-reload-05`, `stack-run-stop-01`, `journey-new-project-03`, `journey-crash-to-fix-04`, `manifest-hot-reload-2-04`, `error-pages-03`, `error-pages-04`, `worktree-switch-05`, `chat-conversations-04` (error content); `stack-scale-07`, `first-run-setup-07`, `stack-run-stop-03`, `chat-compose-2-03`, `chat-approvals-05` (outcomes); `interrupted-operations-03`, `interrupted-operations-04`, `stack-run-stop-04`, `journey-crash-to-fix-06` (deliberate stops); `stack-directory-02`, `stack-directory-08`, `services-panel-2-05`, `stack-scale-04`, `notices-and-feedback-02`, `agent-cli-journey-01`, `chat-approvals-03` (states and counts); `page-context-08`, `third-party-devtools-06` (capitalization and names); `stack-run-stop-10`, `first-run-setup-04` (formats); `agent-cli-journey-05`, `first-run-setup-03`, `agent-cli-journey-06`, `agent-cli-journey-02`, `stack-run-stop-12` (agent output); `journey-agent-fix-01`, `reload-and-agents-01`, `page-context-07`, `chat-compose-01` (text devhost writes); `chat-compose-2-05` (a send error that names a Reconnect control chat does not have).

Use it for every visible word devhost produces, in the browser, the terminal, agent output and the docs. Do not use it for which states exist (2.10), what a failure must contain (2.8) or what an outcome must state (2.7); those sections own the content and this one its words.

States: each glossary noun in a label, a sentence and both outputs; each operation's label, busy label, activity line and outcome; a partial result; an outcome from another origin; each state word in a badge, a sentence and `--json`; a failure, a deliberate stop, a cancelled question and a refusal; a long path in the browser and the terminal; a sent message with its mode line and devhost's added text; the Disable Reloads request for each detected framework.

Keys: hints and key names follow `KEY-13`.

Accessibility: an action's accessible name starts with its visible label (WCAG 2.5.3) and adds its target when the label leaves it out ("Restart api"); counts in accessible names spell "of"; state words and causes are text, never only an icon or color (`ACT-13`); a shortened path exposes the full path in its accessible name; key caps read as the key's name, then its outcome.

Backend contract: the published state carries each state's cause and time as fields that surfaces word (`ACT-2`); one structured error with file, line, column and key (`MENU-4`) instead of preformatted strings; outcome records carry each target's name, step and time so every surface builds the same sentence (`ACT-1`); the session's journal records the text devhost added and the mode as record frames apart from the person's message (`CC-40`), and skill loads and turns the agent started are read from the agent's own events; `apps/devhost/src/cliout.rs` builds human and agent lines from the same facts; `devhost stack info --json` (`devhost status --json`, `O62`) uses the state identifiers of `WORD-6`.

Component primitive: one shared module of state words, labels and sentence builders under `packages/devhost-ui/src/`, used by the toolbar and the status pages (`SURF-14`), and its Rust counterpart in `apps/devhost/src/cliout.rs` for terminal lines; library work: none.

Contract change: `CC-30` (the two outputs), `CC-31` (labels and words the docs change), `CC-32` (glossary terms added to `CONTEXT.md`); `CC-39` (the devhost config file's definition, from 2.1), `CC-40` (each message's mode in the session's journal, from 2.9); roster TB-03, SVC-01, SVC-02, SVC-03, SVC-04, SVC-05, WT-01, WT-02, WT-06, CFG-02, CFG-04, CFG-06, RLD-01, RLD-02, CHAT-01, CHAT-03, CHAT-04, CHAT-05, CHAT-08, AGT-01, AGT-02, REC-01, REC-02, REC-07, STK-01, STK-04, STK-07, STK-09, MAN-03, MAN-04, SET-02, SRV-01.

### 2.13 Empty, loading and error states

What a surface shows before its content arrives, when there is none, and when it cannot get it. Service and stack states are 2.10's; these rules cover the views that show them.

Rules:

- **STATE-1 A wait names what it waits for, and for how long.** Every loading or waiting state names its step or target, the elapsed time, and the limit when one exists: a starting service shows its health check's target and "4 s of 60 s" (`ACT-4`); a switch names its step and the service it waits on (`FB-1`); a terminal wait keeps one status line (`FB-10`). A surface that loads content shows its frame at once with "Loading <what>…" where the content goes, never a blank area or "Working…" before anything started. Chat shows a message that has not left as "Not sent yet", and a message sent during a turn, once the agent's CLI has it, as "Sent into the running turn · the agent reads it at its next step" (`REV-6`).
- **STATE-2 An empty state says why it is empty, what to do, and what just ended.** An empty list or view gives one sentence of why and its next step as an action or a command: the stack directory with nothing running lists the stacks that stopped recently, with when, why and the command that starts each (`ACT-10`, `O15`; revisiting a stopped stack's HTTPS hostname renders its stopped-stack recovery page over HTTPS for `O15`'s window while arbitrary unknown HTTPS hostnames keep strict configured-hosts-only TLS and close-hostname suggestions appear on plain HTTP when `httpEnabled = true` and in the stack directory, `O63`), else "No stacks are running on devbox. Start one with devhost start in its folder." (the machine name, `O61`); while it lists only stopped stacks, its heading reads "No stacks running · 2 stopped in the last 24 hours" (revision 7); the running agents control stays on the bar with "No agents in this worktree", and while chat is off, which turns the agent scan off as documented, with "Running agents are shown while chat is on", never claiming none, while a switch's reach line says that agents were not checked (`REV-3`); a service's log with no lines reads "No output from api since it started at 14:02"; Watch Live Activity reads "No activity yet. Agents in this worktree appear here when they start."; a filter with no match names the query with **Clear Filter** (`SEL-10`); a picker with nothing chosen names what it picks ("Store: none chosen"). Contract: `CC-33`.
- **STATE-3 Unknown, starting and stopped are states, not empties.** A view whose source stopped answering keeps its last content drawn as unknown with its age (`ACT-5`, `ACT-12`), never an empty list or an endless spinner; a starting service shows starting (`ACT-4`), never "no logs" or failed; a stopped stack shows its end with its reason and the command that starts it again (`ACT-10`). An empty state appears only when the source answered with nothing.
- **STATE-4 A view that cannot load says so in place and keeps what it had.** When a panel, page or file cannot load (the control server did not answer, a file is unreadable, a list request failed), the place of its content shows one sentence naming what failed and why (`FAIL-2`, `WORD-7`), the next step (**Retry**, a command or **Copy Error**) and whatever it showed before, marked with its age; never markup, a raw error chain or a blank panel (`SURF-9`). A failed retry updates the same message with its count (`FAIL-4`).

Rationale: Docker Desktop's empty Containers view explains how to run one and VS Code's empty views name the reason and offer the action; Vite prints the step it waits on and Kubernetes keeps a pod Pending with a reason; VS Code Remote keeps the last known state while reconnecting. Today the stack directory's empty state tells people to start "a project" and refresh (`apps/devhost/src/statuspage.rs:147-157`), the startup page waits without naming the check or the time (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/live.rs:226-291`), chat shows "Working…" while it still waits for the person's choice (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/agentChat/components/CompactChat.tsx:55`), and the running agents control disappears when no agent runs. Findings: `stack-directory-07`, `stack-run-stop-11` (empty states that remember); `startup-recovery-02`, `worktree-switch-09`, `stack-run-stop-04` (waits that say what they wait for); `chat-approvals-04`, `chat-compose-2-02` (chat before and after Send); `reload-and-agents-05` (the running agents control); `third-party-devtools-04` (a picker with nothing chosen); `services-panel-2-04`, `notices-and-feedback-02` (unknown is not empty).

Use it for every view's loading, waiting, empty and cannot-load state, in the browser and the terminal. Do not use it for a service's or stack's own state (2.10) or an operation's failure (2.8).

States: each surface loading, empty with and without something that just ended, unknown while disconnected, and unable to load with and without earlier content; a filter with no match; a starting service's page; a stopped stack's page and the directory with recently stopped stacks; chat before Send, with a message sent into a running turn, and loading a conversation.

Keys: **Retry**, **Clear Filter** and every next step are ordinary buttons; see 2.11.

Accessibility: a loading region is `aria-busy` and announces only when it ends in an empty state or a failure (`FB-9`); waits are never announced step by step; an empty or error state's sentence comes before its action in reading order; when content replaces a loading state that held focus, focus moves to the content's first item, else its heading (`KEY-6`).

Backend contract: the stopped-stack record of `ACT-10`; the health check's target, elapsed time and timeout in the published state (`ACT-4`); list routes that answer an empty list and a failure differently, so a page never shows a failure as empty.

Component primitive: one empty-state and one cannot-load block with a sentence and an action, shared by the toolbar and the status pages (`SURF-14`); library work: none.

Contract change: `CC-33` (the running agents control stays on the bar); `CC-27` (recently stopped stacks, from 2.10); roster AGT-01, AGT-02, REC-01, REC-07, LOG-01, LOG-02, CHAT-03, CHAT-05, EXT-02.

### 2.14 Contract changes collected

Every project-instruction and feature-roster change this reference requires. Entries `CC-41` to `CC-51` (revision 7) collect the texts the reconciled flows change that no entry covered; each names the documents and quotes the opening of each changed sentence, and the owning proposal's "Backend and contract changes" quotes the current and the new text in full. `reference/control-api.md` changes in `CC-4`, `CC-5`, `CC-19`, `CC-25`, `CC-26`, `CC-28`, `CC-37` and `CC-38`; `reference/chat-api.md` in `CC-5` (panel position), `CC-10` (workflow instructions, `send`'s mode), `CC-19` (browser state), `CC-38` (sessions across the stack's worktrees, ended sessions, chat off) and `CC-40` (devhost's record frames). Each lands in the same change as the code that implements it, with roster updates. Project text is quoted, not cited by line, because line numbers drift. Revision 5 withdrew three clauses of `CC-10` (the New worktree mode and `worktree` key under `O1`, Make tickets drafts under `O21`, and live-mode agent restarts under `O58`) and the editor parts of `CC-39` (`[devtools.editor]`, `DEVHOST_EDITOR` and roster `SRC-03` under `O51`), without renumbering. Revision 6 withdraws the `devhost stack replace` rename in `CC-12`, `CC-24`, `CC-31`, `CC-35` and `CC-36` under `O18` (adding `devhost upgrade` while keeping shipped `devhost restart-all` and project-scoped `devhost stack restart`), extends shipped `devhost stack info` with the `devhost status` alias in `CC-23` (`O62`), clarifies configured-hosts-only TLS vs 24 h stopped-stack HTTPS routes in `CC-27` (`O63`), records the settled `O30` parallel-shutdown contract and configurable `stopTimeout` in `CC-35`, and adds the shared-domain passkey public key (`O36`) and `stopTimeout` (`O30`) to `config.toml` in `CC-39`, without renumbering.

1. **CC-1** (`SURF-1`): `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`, "Chat and the annotation controls share `AnnotationTopLayer`, a separate manual popover above native host and devhost popups." becomes "Chat and the annotation controls share `AnnotationTopLayer`, a separate manual popover above native host popups and devhost popups, except an open toolbar panel, which paints above docked chat; no devhost surface covers the toolbar's notice stack." Roster TB-06.
2. **CC-2** (`SURF-3`, `KEY-6`, `KEY-8`): `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`, "Keep the dialog open during recovery and failed attempts; remove it after healthy recovery." becomes "Keep the dialog open during recovery and failed attempts unless the person minimizes it, with Escape or **Minimize**, to a crash notice in the notice stack that reopens it; reopen it on the next exit; open it minimized, as that notice, while the person types or chat holds a draft, a decision card or a working agent, and otherwise focus its heading, never a button; keep chat and the notices usable over it; remove it after healthy recovery. Never open it over devhost's own status page for the same failure: both render the shared recovery view." Roster REC-03.
3. **CC-3** (`SURF-11`): `guides/devtools.md`, "It stays above your page's popovers and modal dialogs, and chat keeps working while a modal is open." becomes "It stays above your page's popovers and modal dialogs. While your page shows a modal dialog, chat and the toolbar's notices keep working inside it, and the rest of the toolbar shows that it is paused until the dialog closes." `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`, "A host modal makes ordinary controls outside it inert; promotion changes paint order only." is kept and extended with "the notice stack and the announcer move into the active modal as the annotation mount does, a scripted one included (`role="dialog"` or `"alertdialog"` with `aria-modal="true"`, or the devtools host made `inert` or `aria-hidden` by the page), and the rest of the toolbar renders its paused state." Roster TB-06.
4. **CC-4** (`SURF-16`, `MENU-5`; `O5`): `stacks/recovery.md`, "**HTTP 404**: devhost shows **Page not found**, with the checkout picker when [worktrees](../worktrees/) are on." and "**HTTP 5xx**, including an empty response from a failed compile: devhost shows **<service> could not load** with recent logs." become "**HTTP 404** or **5xx** from your app: the page shows your app's own response with a devhost notice naming the service and worktree that answered, **Retry**, and, when worktrees are on, the worktree list under "Is this route in another worktree?". devhost's own page answers only when the service is not running or has no route." `reference/control-api.md`'s Request routing row "Everything else | The app, directly" is kept and extended with "When the app does not answer, devhost answers `503` with `Retry-After` and a body naming the service and its phase, instead of an empty `502`.", and "Assets, HMR, fetches, server-sent events, and WebSockets therefore never pass through devhost." becomes "Assets, HMR, fetches, server-sent events, and WebSockets therefore reach devhost only when the app cannot answer them." Roster REC-04, REC-05.
5. **CC-5** (`SURF-7`): `guides/devtools.md`, "The toolbar sits at the bottom-right corner of the page; `[devtools.status].position = "top-right"` moves it up." becomes "The toolbar starts in the corner `[devtools.status].position` names, bottom-right by default. Drag it, or use **Move to** in the stack name menu, to place it for yourself; devhost remembers your placement and collapsed state for this stack on every hostname, and **Reset to Manifest Default** returns to the manifest's corner." `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`, "Its position is saved on the server per manifest, never in `localStorage`, which is separate per hostname; it is page layout, so it does not follow the chat target." is kept and extended to the toolbar's corner and collapsed state, under a layout route that does not depend on chat. `reference/control-api.md`'s HTTP endpoints table replaces the `/__devhost__/chat/panel` row with `/__devhost__/layout` (`GET`, `PUT`: "The toolbar's placement and collapsed state and the chat panel's position, saved per manifest, or per served folder for `devhost serve`"), and `reference/chat-api.md`'s Panel position section, which documents `GET` and `PUT` on `/__devhost__/chat/panel` and says "A session without a manifest answers `404` and keeps no position.", becomes "The panel's position is part of the toolbar's layout, `/__devhost__/layout` in the Control API, saved per manifest, or per served folder for `devhost serve`, because browser storage is separate for each hostname." Roster TB-02.
6. **CC-6** (`SEL-2`, `SEL-3`; `O4`): `stacks/recovery.md`, "Every service marked **changed** by its `watch` paths (the primary service when none is)" becomes "Every service marked **changed** by its `watch` paths (when none is, the page's own service, else the primary service; the button names it)"; `guides/devtools.md`, "**Restart Changed** restarts the changed services, or the primary service when none changed." becomes "**Restart Changed** restarts the changed services, or, when none changed, the page's own service (else the primary service), which its label names." Roster SVC-03.
7. **CC-7** (`SEL-7`, `ACT-6`; `O6`): `guides/stack-lifecycle.md`, "To change the selection, stop the stack and start it again." becomes "To change the selection while the stack runs, use **Start** in the Services panel, `devhost service start` or `devhost service stop`, or run `devhost start` with the new names."; `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`, "Keep them out of the service counts, dots, and rows: the Services panel reaches them through its **Stopped services** button and view, which closes by itself when the last one has started." becomes "Count them in the stack's totals and list them in their group as not-started rows, each with **Start**." 8. **CC-8** (`SEL-7`, `MENU-3`, `HOLD-3`, `WORD-2`): `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`, "Disable **Start** while a service restarts, a repository switches, or the stack restarts, because devhost runs those operations one at a time." becomes "devhost runs those operations one at a time: it queues a **Start** or **Restart** of another service while one runs, every page shows the row as queued with **Cancel Restart** or **Cancel Start**, and the queue survives page reloads; while a repository switches or the stack restarts, **Start** and **Restart** are disabled with the reason shown." Roster SVC-02, SVC-05.

10. **CC-10** (`REV-6`; `O1`, `O19`, `O21`, `O35`, `O58`): `guides/annotations.md`, "The workflow prompt", "devhost adds instructions to each message: work in a dedicated Git worktree, keep unrelated changes, run the project's checks, commit and merge into the local `main`, then remove the worktree and branch." becomes "devhost adds instructions to each message for the edit mode you chose: **This worktree (live)** edits the active worktree in place, main's included, leaves every change uncommitted, and creates no additional worktree, commit or merge; **Make tickets** files tickets for the problem instead of editing, or changes no files and says it cannot make tickets when it lacks that capability. Your manifest's own guidance comes first, and the mode's instructions decide where the agent edits and whether it commits or merges. Each sent message shows its mode." The two-mode contract follows `O1` (updated 2026-11-18 by q10a6), the no-drafts fallback follows `O21` (reversed 2026-11-18), and the order follows `O35`. `apps/devhost/devhost.example.toml`'s description of the default prompt changes with it, and its "`prompt` replaces those instructions; an empty string disables them." becomes "`prompt` adds the team's guidance before the instructions of every mode, which decide where the agent edits; `[devtools.chat.modes.<mode>].prompt` (`live` or `tickets`) replaces one mode's instructions, and an empty string there sends none for that mode." (`O35`). `reference/chat-api.md`, Workflow instructions, "devhost appends `[devtools.chat].prompt` to the text of each non-empty `send`, as a labeled section of the user message." and "Without `prompt`, devhost appends its default worktree workflow; an empty string appends nothing." become "devhost appends `[devtools.chat].prompt`, then the instructions of the message's edit mode, to the text of each non-empty `send`, as a labeled section of the user message.", and its Commands table's `send` gains `mode` (`live` or `tickets`). Withdrawn in revision 5 (without renumbering): the New worktree mode and its `worktree` key (`O1`, q10a6), Make tickets drafts (`O21`, reversed 2026-11-18), and live-mode agent restarts (`O58`, reversed 2026-11-18; the person restarts changed services from the turn's summary). Roster CHAT-08.

"`devhost caddy stop` | Stops the shared Caddy." becomes "Stops the shared Caddy. Names the running stacks and the hostnames it takes offline first." The flags table gains `--yes` ("Act even when an agent's turn would end; without it, these commands ask in a terminal and refuse elsewhere") for `stop --worktrees`, `restart-all` and `upgrade`, and `--dry-run` ("Print the stacks the command would act on, then exit without acting") for those and `caddy stop`. `guides/stack-lifecycle.md`, "After installing a new devhost executable, replace the running supervisors:" names `devhost upgrade` for self-managed user-writable installs and `devhost restart-all` (with `--yes` for scripts) for dotfiles, package managers and local rebuilds, plus `devhost stack restart` for one project. (the text on `main`; revision 7 corrects the quote) is kept and extended to state the rest of `O63`: strict configured-hosts-only TLS stays in place for arbitrary unknown HTTPS hostnames (zero on-demand certificates for typos); a stack that stopped recently keeps its configured Caddy route and HTTPS certificate active for `O15`'s 24 h window so revisiting a stopped HTTPS tab renders the stopped-stack recovery page over HTTPS naming the stack, when and why it stopped, and its start command; close-hostname typo suggestions (`O46`) appear on the unmatched-hostname 404 served on plain HTTP (when `httpEnabled = true`) and in the stack directory. `guides/stack-lifecycle.md`, "After the last stack stops cleanly, the page shows an empty list until Caddy stops." becomes "Stacks that stopped recently stay listed as stopped, with when and why and the command that starts them again; with none running or recently stopped, the page shows an empty list until Caddy stops."; "Stacks appear in a single column of compact cards showing their PID, directory, and services." is kept and extended with "Each card counts its running services of the stack's total and names its failures, and statuses update while the page is open." 28. **CC-28** (`KEY-5`): `guides/devtools.md`, "Keys you type into it, such as in the chat composer, never trigger your page's shortcuts. Click the page to give it the keyboard back." becomes "Keys you type into it, such as in the chat composer, never reach your page, not even shortcuts your page listens for before devhost does. After you click a toolbar button or close one of its panels, your page's keys keep working: a toolbar button keeps only Enter, Space, the arrow keys, Home, End and Escape." `packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/AGENTS.md`, "`DevtoolsTopLayer` ends the propagation of every `keydown`, `keypress`, and `keyup` from inside devtools at the shadow root." becomes "A classic script injected first in `<head>` stops every `keydown`, `keypress`, `keyup` and `beforeinput` typed into devtools text entry at the window in the capture phase, before any page listener, and dispatches at the field a clone that stays inside the shadow root, so devtools' own handlers run and the page's never do; the clone's `preventDefault()` is copied onto the original. Devtools text entry stays in the Shadow DOM. `DevtoolsTopLayer` ends, at the shadow root, the propagation of the keys a focused devtools control uses (Enter, Space, the arrow keys, Home, End and Escape, and every key inside an open panel, menu or dialog) and lets every other key reach the page." `reference/control-api.md`'s HTTP endpoints table gains `/__devhost__/keys.js` (`GET`, "The script page loads include first in `<head>` so keys typed into the toolbar never reach the page"). ### 2.15 Wireframe conventions

This group follows 2.14 because existing section numbers stay. Revision 7 adds it from the conventions the 19 reconciled flows needed beyond the design language, so every wireframe draws the same thing the same way.

Rules:

- **WF-1 One fixture set.** Every wireframe uses section 1's examples: the machine devbox, the Acme stacks and their labels under `.acme.test`, and relay hostnames under `--devbox--preview.acme.test`; a proposal writes the same labels under `.localhost`. Loopback addresses (`127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`, `localhost` with a port) may appear where the product's own message names the bind host or a loopback target, such as a port serve session's name; machine host names and private domains never do.
- **WF-2 One glyph per state, in every legend.** ● running, ○ not started, ◐ starting, restarting or stopping, ✕ exited, ▲ failed, ◆ a condition, ? unknown, ⏸ paused; each always beside its state word (`ACT-13`). The legend on every flow page carries them with the notice kinds of `WF-3`.
- **WF-3 Notice kinds by style.** A condition notice is amber (`banner`, ◆), a failure red (`banner bad`, ▲ or ✕), a success green (`banner ok`), information blue (`banner info`). A rejected configuration is a condition (`FB-8`).
- **WF-4 Frames say what they are.** A window frame that holds no screen (a sequence table, a terminal transcript) says "Diagram, not a screen" in its title; terminal transcripts mark warnings and errors with the notice kinds' colors; a phone layout (`O8`) is drawn in the phone frame at 320 CSS pixels; devhost's full pages are drawn in a page frame with their address.
- **WF-5 Two banner kinds for open questions.** "Open conflict" when two flows disagree; "Proposed exception" when one flow departs from a rule. Each names the rule and the other flow, the wireframe index lists every open one, and both are removed once this reference settles them.
- **WF-6 Shared parts live in the stylesheet.** Control boundaries at 3:1 (buttons, fields, chips), the pressed and the unavailable bar item (unavailable stays in place, dimmed, with its reason, `BAR-5`), the library panel, the tooltip card, the phone frame and docked chat are classes of `wireframe.css`, never page-local copies; a tooltip is drawn as a card beside its control; callouts are numbered in reading order.

## 3. Decision log

One entry per audit decision that had a conflict or silent flows. Format: the options (with the flows that held them), the decision (with the rule it creates), the reason, and the flows that must change. "Silent, must adopt" lists flows that did not state the behavior and must now state it. Every decision the seven audits raised had a conflict or flows that never stated it, so each has an entry below; no decision was agreed and fully stated, so none is folded into section 2 without one. Entries that point to another entry ("see `selection-12`") keep their own Must change and Silent lists. An entry whose decision revision 2 changed says "Decision (revision 2)" and keeps the earlier reasoning in its Reason; an entry changed by the product owner's 2026-11-18 answers says "Decision (revision 5, product owner)" and keeps the superseded reasoning; an entry changed by the product owner's 2026-11-25 answers says "Decision (revision 6, product owner)" and keeps the superseded reasoning.

### 3.1 selection

- **selection-01 · Target of Restart Changed, its badge icon and Alt+Ctrl+R.** Options: (a) the control names its target, the changed services by count with their names in its tooltip, or its fallback service by name, and the key names what it restarted or why it did nothing [services-panel]; (b) with nothing changed, the control is disabled with "No changes" [services-panel]; (c) the no-change fallback becomes the page's own service instead of the stack's primary service [services-panel]; changed and failed are independent and the changed set leaves out edits already applied in place [services-panel, reload-control]; (d) as today, a hot reload decides its restart set without naming it [manifest-hot-reload]. Decision (revision 2): (a) with (c), except that an edit applied in place stays in the changed set, `SEL-1`, `SEL-2`, `ACT-8`; `O4` (decided 2026-11-18) keeps the fallback. Reason: the docs promise a fallback (`stacks/recovery.md`), so naming it changes the least; the page's service already scopes the crash view and **Reload blocked** (`SEL-3`); VS Code, JetBrains and Tilt name a rerun's target; one selector feeds the button, the badge icon and the key today (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/shared/selectRestartServiceNames.ts:4`). Revision 1 also left edits applied in place out of the set, as the page saw them; the server alone now computes changed, so every tab, the terminal and `devhost status` read one mark, and the page's "applied in place" note sits beside it (`ACT-8`, `CC-25`). Must change: services-panel (the label names the set or the fallback; the reload icon stays while an error shows; the key reports why it did nothing), manifest-hot-reload (each reload names its restart set and why, `SEL-11`), reload-control (**Reload blocked** names the changed files, `SEL-3`). Silent, must adopt: startup-and-recovery, worktree-switch, notices-and-feedback, toolbar-shell (each states what Restart Changed and Alt+Ctrl+R target on its surfaces, per `SEL-2`). Contract: `CC-6`; SVC-03.
- **selection-02 · Real scope of Restart Stack and of a stack-wide restart from every entry point.** Options: (a) the real scope never exceeds the label: the control states the count and the port change, sits apart from Restart Changed, healthy services are restored when one fails and the failure stays visible in place [services-panel, manifest-hot-reload, startup-and-recovery, notices-and-feedback]. Decision: (a), `SEL-1`, `BAR-2`, `SURF-3`; the confirmation is 2.5's and the partial failure 2.8's. Reason: `error-pages-01`, reproduced by its reviewer and its verifier, shows a failed rollback taking the whole repository down against `stacks/recovery.md`; Docker Desktop and JetBrains act on a visibly listed group. Must change: services-panel (count and port change stated; moved apart from Restart Changed), startup-and-recovery (the crash view keeps the outcome in place, `SURF-3`). Silent, must adopt: worktree-switch (a switch is a stack-wide restart and names its reach, `SEL-5`), config-editor (a save whose reload restarts services names them, `SEL-11`), agent-chat (states what a stack-wide restart does to a working agent; `stacks/recovery.md` keeps chat sessions connected). Contract: none; SVC-04.
- **selection-03 · The page's own service as the implicit target.** Options: (a) page-scoped surfaces act on the page's service: the crash view, **Reload blocked** naming the files under the page's service's watch paths, and one recovery screen per failure on devhost's own status page [startup-and-recovery, reload-control]; (b) Restart Changed falls back to the stack's primary service, as documented [services-panel proposes moving it to the page's service]; (c) logs narrow by a legend while the default stays every service [log-minimap]. Decision: (a) and (c), `SEL-3`, `SEL-2`, `SURF-3`, `SURF-13`. Reason: keep the convention most surfaces already share; (b) is the one outlier; Vite and Next.js overlays always concern the page's own server; logs are a stack-wide view, so narrowing them is a filter. Must change: services-panel (the fallback names the page's service), startup-and-recovery (no crash view over devhost's own page for the same failure), log-minimap (every service by default, a service filter), reload-control (names the changed files). Silent, must adopt: agent-chat and page-context (page context tells the agent the page's URL, service and worktree), component-source (looks up source in the page's worktree), toolbar-shell (says which of its segments are stack-wide), third-party-devtools (states that launchers act on the page itself). Contract: `CC-6`; REC-03, RLD-02.
- **selection-04 · Identifying and picking a worktree on every surface.** Options: (a) branch or "Detached" and a short commit ID, plus the `~`-shortened path, in one shared list on status pages and in the toolbar, found by commit prefix as well as fuzzily, never preselecting a deleted worktree [worktree-switch, startup-and-recovery, stack-run-stop]; (b) the branch, or "detached at" an ID, plus the worktree folder, main worktree first [stack-directory]; (c) "worktree root" instead of "." [running-agents]; (d) chat follows the selected worktree without losing conversations of the one it left [agent-chat]. Decision: (a) with (b)'s ordering, (c) and (d), `SEL-4`, `SEL-8`, `SURF-14`, `WORD-11`. Reason: (a) is the toolbar's existing label (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/serviceStatusPanel/components/WorktreePicker.tsx:43`) and the most-held position; a folder name repeats across repositories, a shortened path does not; VS Code, JetBrains and GitHub Desktop. Must change: stack-directory (cards add the `SEL-4` label after the stack name), startup-and-recovery and worktree-switch (status pages render the shared list), stack-run-stop (terminal lines name worktrees by label, not folder), running-agents ("worktree root"), agent-chat (lists the conversations of every worktree). Silent, must adopt: component-source (names the worktree of the file it resolves), config-editor (names the worktree whose manifest it edits), page-context (sends the worktree label), reload-control (the Disable Reloads popover names the worktree it is scoped to). Contract: none; WT-01, WT-05, WT-06, REC-07.
- **selection-05 · What a worktree switch acts on.** Options: (a) one line under "Switch and Restart Stack" reading "Stops every service in this repository and starts them from the selected worktree" [worktree-switch]; (b) a switch that would leave a working or waiting agent behind names each one first, and conversations stay bound to their worktree yet listed and answerable [worktree-switch, agent-chat, running-agents]; (c) keep the external-service block but state it where it is caused, and leave skipping it to the owner [manifest-hot-reload, worktree-switch]; (d) the run's service names last for the whole run, with no exemption for a switch [stack-run-stop]. Decision (revision 6, product owner): `SEL-5`, with (a)'s line corrected to the real reach (every managed service of the stack), (b), (d), and (c) reversed under `O7` (decided 2026-11-25, owner item q17a6): external services (`managed = false`) never block worktree switches and need no warning when managed services switch — managed services restart from the target worktree's folder while external services stay untouched (still marked external in the Services panel). Reason: the code and `stacks/worktrees.md` stop every managed service of the stack, so (a)'s sentence understates it; dropping the selection on a switch is a defect already routed for filing; JetBrains and VS Code warn about running sessions before a branch switch; revision 5 had kept option (c)'s external-service block while awaiting the owner, who reversed it on 2026-11-25 so external services never block switches and trigger no extra warning. Must change: worktree-switch (the line states the stack-wide reach and names agents, drafts, page context and a held reload; remove the external-service switch block), agent-chat and running-agents (the old worktree's conversations and agents stay listed). Silent, must adopt: services-panel (shows the switch's restarts per row, 2.7, keeping external rows untouched), startup-and-recovery (the status-page switch uses the same control and line), page-context (states what the switch's page reload does to page context), reload-control (a held reload is named and discarded), config-editor (an unsaved draft is named before the switch). Contract: none; WT-02.
- **selection-06 · How CLI commands pick the stacks they act on.** Options: (a) multi-stack commands print their plan first and offer `--dry-run`; `stack find` treats folders as folders; `caddy stop` names the stacks and hostnames it takes offline; `devhost stop` names the stack, not processes; the in use popover's **Stop Stack** names the owner by worktree [stack-run-stop, first-run-setup, worktree-switch]. Decision: (a), `SEL-6`, `BAR-2`; whether and how they ask is 2.5's. Reason: `docker system prune`, `git clean` and `kubectl` name their target set and offer a dry run; Docker Compose and Overmind name the project they act on. Must change: stack-run-stop, first-run-setup, worktree-switch. Silent, must adopt: proxy-settings (a devhost config file change names the stacks whose URLs move), serve-folder (serve sessions appear in `devhost restart-all`'s and `devhost upgrade`'s printed sets under 2.12's name), stack-directory (lists stacks with the same name and worktree label). Contract: none; STK-04, STK-05, STK-06, STK-07, SET-04.
- **selection-07 · Choosing which services of a stack run.** Options: (a) count the whole stack, list not-started services as rows in their group with **Start**, start them all at once, queue starts [services-panel]; (b) starting a service never draws running services as down [services-panel, notices-and-feedback]; (c) a start that names services the running stack left out names them and the remedies, and the owner decides between starting them into the running stack and exiting non-zero [stack-run-stop]; (d) `devhost service start|stop|restart` reaches the running stack [stack-run-stop, services-panel]; the docs say the selection changes only by stopping and starting again. Decision: (a), (b) and (d), `SEL-7`, `BAR-2`; (c) settled by `O6` (decided 2026-11-18: starts them into the running stack). Reason: **Start** in Stopped services already adds to the running selection, so the documented "stop the stack and start it again" no longer describes the product; `docker compose up <service>` and Overmind add to a running project. Must change: services-panel (rows, counts, queue), stack-run-stop (service commands; start into the running stack), notices-and-feedback (starting never draws running services as down). Silent, must adopt: worktree-switch (keeps the selection, `SEL-5`), stack-directory (cards count not-started services like the toolbar), manifest-hot-reload (a reload keeps the selection, as documented), startup-and-recovery (a not-started service's hostname offers **Start**, `MENU-5`, `O10`). Contract: `CC-7`, `CC-8`, `CC-11`; SVC-05, STK-03.
- **selection-08 · Which conversation and agent receive a message.** Options: (a) the composer names the harness and model before the first Send, the harness is fixed for an open conversation, a Send that starts a new conversation says so, and conversations are listed by first message with harness, model, worktree, time and state [agent-chat]; (b) harness, model and draft follow the stack, not the worktree [agent-chat, worktree-switch]; (c) owner decisions q4 and q5 name the mode and the agent before a send [agent-chat, page-context, reload-control]; (d) the running agents list marks this chat's agent [running-agents]. Decision: all, `SEL-8`, `SEL-11`, `O1`, `O2`; the default without page context is `O3` (decided 2026-11-18). Reason: Cursor and Claude Code's IDE chat show the model and mode before Send and list conversations by title. Must change: agent-chat, reload-control, running-agents. Silent, must adopt: page-context (page context goes to the conversation the composer shows, also right after a new conversation), notices-and-feedback (a send's outcome names the conversation it went to). Contract: `CC-9`, `CC-10`, `CC-38`; CHAT-01, CHAT-05, CHAT-08.
- **selection-09 · Picking page elements and keeping their identity.** Options: (a) stable numbers for the life of the draft, drawings anchored and numbered on the page, a per-pick preview, Undo of the last action, picks reaching the page around the bar, and a pick that reports what it resolved [page-context, toolbar-shell, component-source, agent-chat]. Decision: (a), `SEL-9`, `SURF-11`, `SURF-12`. Reason: Chrome DevTools and React DevTools highlight the element under the pointer before the click; Figma and Linear keep pins numbered and anchored. Must change: page-context, toolbar-shell, component-source. Silent, must adopt: third-party-devtools (states whether picks reach a docked library panel), reload-control (states that a hot update keeps marks anchored, `SURF-12`), worktree-switch (states that a switch's page reload discards picks, `SEL-5`). Contract: none; CTX-01, CTX-02, CTX-03, SRC-01.
- **selection-10 · Entry points that lead to a manifest or settings problem, and what they open.** Options: (a) every entry point opens the file, line and key of the problem, built from one structured error, with Open in Editor and Copy when the config editor is off [config-editor, manifest-hot-reload, startup-and-recovery, services-panel, worktree-switch, proxy-settings, stack-run-stop]. Decision (revision 5, product owner): (a) without Open in Editor (`O51`, 2026-11-18, dropped editor support completely), `MENU-4`, `SURF-4`: every entry point opens the file, line and key in the Configuration window when on, or shows the file and line with **Copy** when off. Reason: Vite and Next.js overlays link errors to file and line; VS Code's Problems panel jumps to the line; dedupe found one missing prerequisite behind six findings, the structured error; revision 4 had included **Open in Editor**, which `O51` removed. Must change: config-editor, manifest-hot-reload, startup-and-recovery, services-panel, worktree-switch, proxy-settings, stack-run-stop. Silent, must adopt: stack-directory (its read-only configuration files show the file and line with **Copy**), first-run-setup (a devhost config file error at `caddy start` names file and line), serve-folder (a `serve` service's manifest error names file and line). Contract: none; CFG-06, MAN-02, MAN-04.
- **selection-11 · Disabled, hidden and dead actions, and where their reason shows.** Options: (a) disable with a stated reason, as for every write while the status stream is lost and Restart Changed with nothing to restart [notices-and-feedback, services-panel]; (b) remove the action, as **Restart** on an external service's page [startup-and-recovery, serve-folder]; (c) show an explicit paused state under the page's modal dialog [toolbar-shell, reload-control]; (d) queue starts instead of disabling every other **Start** [services-panel]; (e) keep a blocked repository's worktrees readable with a reason per row [worktree-switch, manifest-hot-reload]; (f) Close Conversation disabled while in flight, and a failed close says why [agent-chat]; (g) Alt + right-click with no source says so [component-source]. Decision: all, reconciled by `MENU-3`: an inapplicable action is absent with its reason stated once (b), an unavailable one is disabled with a visible reason (a, e, f), nothing dead looks live (c, g), and different services queue (d, `SEL-7`). Reason: the positions differ because their situations differ, an action that can never apply against one that cannot run now; GitHub's merge box lists unmet requirements beside the disabled button, Docker Desktop hides actions that cannot apply. Must change: services-panel, notices-and-feedback, startup-and-recovery, serve-folder, toolbar-shell, reload-control, worktree-switch, manifest-hot-reload, agent-chat, component-source. Silent, must adopt: config-editor (Save unavailable with its reason while a save applies), stack-run-stop (a terminal refusal names why and the remedy, 2.8), third-party-devtools (a launcher that cannot open now is disabled with its reason), page-context (Select Elements unavailable with its reason), running-agents (the list unavailable with its reason when the scan fails). Contract: `CC-8`.
- **selection-12 · Which actions a recovery surface offers for a given cause.** Options: (a) an HTML 5xx shows the app's own error document under devhost's notice, with Restart secondary [startup-and-recovery]; (b) an app 404 stays a devhost page naming who answered, with the app's body and the worktree picker collapsed [startup-and-recovery, worktree-switch]; (c) devhost's file server is exempt and renders its own 404 [serve-folder, startup-and-recovery]; (d) a deleted worktree leads with returning to the configured one [worktree-switch, startup-and-recovery]; (e) a crash that needs a manifest fix offers Edit, which leads after an identical failed retry [startup-and-recovery, config-editor]; (f) an external service offers "Check Again" [startup-and-recovery, serve-folder]; (g) a fresh worktree offers "Retry setup" [worktree-switch]. Decision: `MENU-5` for every cause, with (a) applied to 404s and 5xxs alike over (b) (`SURF-16`), settled by `O5` (decided 2026-11-18). Reason: one rule for an answer the app gave keeps the app's own content and its file and line in view, as Vite, Next.js, Laravel Herd and Valet do; a restart cannot fix the app's own error; dedupe asked for one replacement rule. Must change: startup-and-recovery, worktree-switch, serve-folder, config-editor. Silent, must adopt: services-panel (a failed row offers `MENU-5`'s actions for an exited service), manifest-hot-reload (a rejection notice leads with Edit, `MENU-4`), stack-directory (the Caddy 404 leads with the close hostname), notices-and-feedback (the crash notice carries the lead action). Contract: `CC-4`; REC-02, REC-04, REC-05.
- **selection-13 · Finding and filtering targets in long lists.** Options: (a) every list filters as you type on the page, keeps group headers, highlights matches and explains an empty result, with match keys per list (commit prefixes for worktrees, folders for `stack find`, a service legend for logs), and the Caddy 404 names the host asked for and a close match [stack-directory, services-panel, toolbar-shell, worktree-switch, stack-run-stop, log-minimap]. Decision (revision 6, product owner): (a), `SEL-10`, `SEL-6`, `SURF-10`, `BAR-3`, bounded by `O63` (decided 2026-11-25): close-hostname typo suggestions (`O46`) show on plain HTTP (when `httpEnabled = true`) and in the stack directory while arbitrary unknown HTTPS hostnames keep strict configured-hosts-only TLS (zero on-demand certs for typos) and recently stopped stacks (`O15`) keep active HTTPS routes for 24 h. Reason: VS Code quick pick, JetBrains' Services window, Docker Desktop and k9s filter live and keep group headers; `O63` keeps configured-hosts-only TLS intact on HTTPS. Must change: stack-directory, services-panel, toolbar-shell, worktree-switch, stack-run-stop, log-minimap. Silent, must adopt: agent-chat (the conversation list gains a filter once it overflows, `SURF-10`), running-agents (the same), config-editor (the File list the same), third-party-devtools (launchers follow `SURF-10`'s overflow form). Contract: none; REC-06, REC-07.
- **selection-14 · Switches and settings whose reach exceeds their label, and whose setting it is.** Options: (a) the toolbar renders while any segment has something to show, with one master switch, and turning a segment off never turns off **Hold Reloads** [toolbar-shell, reload-control]; (b) one switch per third-party integration [third-party-devtools]; (c) Markdown source per file, or a visible mode that says its reach [serve-folder]; (d) every reload names the services it restarted and why, and the repository-wide default restart scope is the owner's call [manifest-hot-reload]; (e) personal preferences in three proposed homes: the browser settings store per project and origin [toolbar-shell], `DEVHOST_EDITOR`, then the devhost config file, then the manifest [component-source], and per stack and origin [agent-chat]. Decision (revision 5, product owner): (a) to (d), `SEL-11`, with (d) settled by `O9` (decided 2026-11-18); (e) settled per kind: layout on the control server per manifest (`SURF-7`), key bindings (`O17`, decided 2026-11-18), machine name (`O61`, settled 2026-11-18), default `stopTimeout` (`O30`) and shared-domain passkey public key (`O36`) per machine in the devhost config file, and chat's choices per stack and origin; editor settings (`[devtools.editor]`, `DEVHOST_EDITOR`) are removed by `O51` (2026-11-18). Reason: layout must agree across a stack's hostnames, which browser storage cannot do, the reason the contract already keeps chat's position on the server; personal bindings, machine name, default stop timeout and passkey public key are machine-wide choices; chat drafts are view state the settings-store contract keeps per origin, and they must survive a switch; VS Code separates user from workspace settings; revision 4 kept the editor per machine, which `O51` dropped completely. Must change: toolbar-shell, third-party-devtools, serve-folder, manifest-hot-reload, component-source (remove editor preferences), agent-chat. Silent, must adopt: config-editor (a setting saved in the window names its reach), log-minimap (turning the minimap off says what it removes), running-agents (turning chat off says what happens to running agents), page-context (turning annotations off says that page context goes). Contract: `CC-5`, `CC-9`, `CC-39` (editor parts withdrawn); TB-01, TB-02, SRV-03, EXT-01, EXT-02.
- **selection-15 · Which surface offers which lifecycle action, and same-named actions that differ.** Options: (a) `devhost service restart|start|stop` reaches the running stack, and the crash line, help and the skill name it [stack-run-stop, services-panel]; (b) `devhost restart-all` and project-scoped `devhost stack restart` keep replacing the supervisor process, joined by `devhost upgrade`, and a replaced foreground terminal prints a handoff [stack-run-stop]; (c) every surface leads to the stack directory [stack-run-stop, stack-directory]; (d) restarting one service has one owner, to which the crash view and recovery pages hand off [services-panel, startup-and-recovery]; (e) the restart routes answer a script's mistakes with documented codes [services-panel]. Decision (revision 6, product owner): all, `MENU-6`, `MENU-1`, `SEL-7`, `SURF-15`; browser lifecycle is settled by `O10` (decided 2026-11-18); under `O18` (decided 2026-11-25, owner item q79a2), devhost adds `devhost upgrade` and keeps shipped `devhost restart-all` and project-scoped `devhost stack restart` (withdrawing revision 5's `devhost stack replace` rename so both keep one verb while help and output state clearly that they replace the running devhost supervisor process). Reason: Docker Compose, Overmind and Tilt offer the same per-service verbs in the terminal and the UI; keeping one verb across `restart-all` and `stack restart` alongside built-in `devhost upgrade` avoids breaking scripts and dotfiles workflows while making self-managed upgrades one step. Must change: stack-run-stop, services-panel, stack-directory, startup-and-recovery. Silent, must adopt: worktree-switch (states the terminal counterpart of a switch, or that it has none), agent-chat (states that an agent restarts services through `devhost service`, not browser routes), notices-and-feedback (an outcome uses the name of the control that started it), first-run-setup (`caddy start` and `caddy stop` keep one name in help, docs and output). Contract: `CC-11`, `CC-31`; SVC-02, STK-07.

### 3.2 review

- **review-01 · Confirming Restart Stack.** Options: (a) confirm in place with the count, the port change and the last duration, apart from Restart Changed [services-panel]; (b) check first and roll back per service [services-panel, manifest-hot-reload]. Decision (revision 2): both, `REV-2`, `REV-11`, `UNDO-1`, with Enter on Cancel (`REV-4`); the question stays in the Services panel, beside Restart Changed, while the crash view's and recovery pages' Restart Stack and a failed switch's Retry run on one press with their reach named. Reason: one misclick beside Restart Changed costs 14 s of downtime, and JetBrains asks before replacing a run; where the page's service is already down and Restart Stack is the remedy on offer, a question only slows the crash-to-fix loop; `error-pages-01`, reproduced by its reviewer and its verifier, takes the stack down against `stacks/recovery.md`. Must change: services-panel, manifest-hot-reload. Silent, must adopt: startup-and-recovery (the crash view's Restart Stack runs on one press with its reach named, `MENU-6`), worktree-switch (Return to Configured Worktree asks in place; a failed switch's Retry runs on one press), stack-run-stop (`devhost stack restart` of one stack does not ask, `REV-7`, `O18`). Contract: `CC-17`; SVC-04.
- **review-02 · Review before a worktree switch.** Options: (a) ask only when an agent of this worktree works or waits, Answer First by default [worktree-switch]; (b) state the reach instead of asking [worktree-switch]; (c) mark worktrees that need setup and keep the previous one when setup fails [worktree-switch]; (d) conversations left behind stay listed and answerable [agent-chat]. Decision: all, `REV-2`, `REV-3`, `SEL-5`, `UNDO-1`: picking and pressing are the two steps, and the question also covers drafts, a held reload and page context, saying that agents keep running in the old worktree. Reason: VS Code and GitHub Desktop switch a clean worktree without asking. Must change: worktree-switch. Silent, must adopt: startup-and-recovery (the same steps and question on status pages), running-agents (supplies the terminal agents the question names). Contract: none; WT-02, WT-06.
- **review-03 · What counts as disruptive, and which actions ask first.** Options: (a) Restart Stack always asks [services-panel]; (b) a switch asks only for agents [worktree-switch]; (c) writes to other stacks ask [worktree-switch, stack-run-stop, first-run-setup]; (d) one-target, recoverable actions never ask [services-panel, agent-chat]; (e) saves never ask but one [config-editor, manifest-hot-reload]; (f) a Send never asks, or once per project [agent-chat, page-context]; (g) Disable Reloads asks [reload-control]; (h) a second agent asks [agent-chat]; (i) trust changes ask and refuse agents [first-run-setup]. Decision (revision 2): one table, `REV-1`, on section 1's Disruptive: (a) in the Services panel only, beside Restart Changed, and (b), through `REV-2` and `REV-3`, (c) `REV-7` only while a turn would end (`O33`), (d), (e) `REV-10`, (f) without the one-time question (`REV-6`, `O1`), (g) `REV-5`, (h) `REV-3` in **This worktree (live)** only, (i) `REV-8`. Reason: ask when an action reaches beyond what the person looks at or ends work they cannot get back (Docker Desktop, Git clients, Terraform); revision 1 also asked for Restart Stack where it is the remedy for a page that is already down, for every multi-stack command in a terminal, and for second agents that edit no file of this worktree. Must change: services-panel, worktree-switch, stack-run-stop, first-run-setup, config-editor, agent-chat, reload-control. Silent, must adopt: startup-and-recovery (its actions follow the table), proxy-settings (a devhost config file save names the stacks whose URLs move, `REV-10`). Contract: `CC-12`, `CC-17`.
- **review-04 · Actions that stop or restart other stacks.** Options: (a) Stop Stack asks in place and shows progress in the open popover, and the stopped stack's terminal names who stopped it [worktree-switch]; (b) `stop --worktrees`, `restart-all` and `upgrade` print their set, ask in a human terminal unless `--yes`, act without one, take `--dry-run` [stack-run-stop]; (c) `caddy stop` names its stacks and asks, and without a terminal acts or refuses unless `--force` [first-run-setup, stack-run-stop]; (d) ended conversations are named and resumable [agent-chat, stack-run-stop]. Decision (revision 2): (a); (b) and (c) print their set and act, asking in a terminal, or refusing elsewhere, without `--yes` only when the set would end an agent's turn (`O33`, `O18`); `--yes` the only skip flag; and (d); `REV-7`, `REV-4`, `SEL-6`, `UNDO-4`. Reason: revision 1 asked whenever a terminal was attached, which asked a person on every run and let an agent end other agents' turns unasked; a turn in progress is the work the question protects (`REV-3`), and acting otherwise keeps scripts and the post-upgrade step working; Terraform and `docker system prune` print before asking. Must change: worktree-switch, stack-run-stop, first-run-setup, agent-chat. Silent, must adopt: startup-and-recovery (the owner card's Stop Stack asks the same way), serve-folder (serve sessions appear in `devhost restart-all`'s and `devhost upgrade`'s printed sets), running-agents (the set names the conversations each stack ends).
