  - Backend work: the activity page and socket served on the stack directory's hostname, one path per stack routed to that stack's control server, under `O36`'s check; the relay forwards that hostname; contract changes to the Watch Live Activity paragraph of `guides/devtools.md` and the Security model of `reference/control-api.md`.
  - Applied by: running-agents (its proposed `HOLD-7` extension and `ACT-10` exception: the page is available to authenticated remote browsers instead of unavailable on a page opened from another machine); `SURF-15` points here.
- **O56 Whether Disable Reloads or the chat rule decides how a later turn's change reaches the page. Decided (product owner, 2026-11-18, owner item q66a2, reversing the recommendation): **Disable Reloads** wins — once a Disable Reloads request takes effect, devhost never auto-reloads after a later chat turn; each turn that changed files ends with `"Reload to see the change"` (from owner comment verbatim: “just reload to see the change”) and **Reload Page** (`FB-12`, `WORD-2`). Resolves `O2`'s clarification.** Supersedes the recommendation (both stand, with devhost auto-reloading the page once after each turn that changed files unless focus is in a text field). From `FB-12`'s check of `O2` (owner item q66). For (superseded recommendation's case): once the request takes effect, the dev server sends neither hot updates nor reloads (`guides/devtools.md`: Disable Reloads stops hot module replacement too) and Hold Reloads, on by default, holds any script reload, so auto-reloading once at turn end would have shown each change without a click. Against (why the owner chose q66a2): a reload the person did not press after each turn undermines Disable Reloads; naming the remaining step (`"Reload to see the change"`) with **Reload Page** satisfies `FB-12` while ensuring nothing reloads that the person did not trigger.
  - Consequence: after Claude Code turned off Vite's `server.hmr`, a later live turn that edited the header ends without reloading the page and shows `"Reload to see the change"` with **Reload Page** in the turn's receipt (exact words per 2.12).
  - Backend work: none in Rust; the page knows the turn ended, which files it changed (`O22`), and that reloads were disabled for this service (`O2`), so it skips `O57`'s auto-reload and renders `"Reload to see the change"` with **Reload Page**.
  - Applied by: agent-chat (the turn's end and its receipt), reload-control (the page after a Disable Reloads request); resolves `O2`'s clarification.
- **O57 What a chat turn's end does with the reloads its edits caused while Hold Reloads is on. Decided (product owner, 2026-11-18, owner item q67a1): when a turn that changed files ends while the page it was sent from holds a reload (and reloads have not been disabled under `O56`), devhost applies the held reloads once, as its own reload, unless focus is in a text field there (the page's or chat's composer, since the next message is often typed as a turn ends), where the turn's receipt leads with **Reload Now** and "The page reload this change needs waits for you"; nothing reloads while the turn runs.** Approved as recommended (bounded by `O56`, under which a Disable Reloads page does not auto-reload). From `FB-12` (owner item q67). For: `FB-12` (ask, wait, see the change); Hold Reloads, on by default (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/shared/createDevtoolsSettingsStore.ts:22`), still keeps reloads from landing mid-turn; devhost's own reloads, after a worktree switch, already pass the hold; chat's draft, images and marks survive a reload devhost causes (`UNDO-2`). Against: a half-filled form of the app whose field lost focus is reloaded away (`UNDO-3`'s concern); another tab of the same page keeps its held reload; page-context's own trigger (`journey-agent-fix-04`: an agent's edit reloads the page while the person prepares the next message) is why the composer counts as typing; the alternative never reloads by itself, and the receipt leads with "The page reload this change needs is held" and **Reload Now**.
  - Consequence: a live turn that edited a module outside hot replacement ends with the page reloaded and the receipt "Changed 2 files · page reloaded to show them" (exact words per 2.12).
  - Backend work: none in Rust; the page knows the turn ended (chat socket) and holds the reload (Navigation API). On a page whose dev server has reloads disabled (`O56`), this auto-reload does not fire; the receipt instead shows `"Reload to see the change"` with **Reload Page**. The Hold Reloads description in `guides/devtools.md` says what a turn's end applies (a `CC-n` to add).
  - Applied by: agent-chat (the turn's end and its receipt), reload-control (**Reload blocked** at a turn's end), page-context (the composer counts as typing, and marks survive the reload, `UNDO-2`), notices-and-feedback (the reloaded page's outcome).
- **O58 Who restarts a service whose running code a chat turn changed. Decided (product owner, 2026-11-18, owner item q68a2, reversing the recommendation): the person, from the turn's summary — the receipt names each service the turn's edits marked changed (`"api runs the old code until it restarts"`) with **Restart Changed**; the agent does not restart services as the turn's last step, and services without `watch` paths go unnamed.** Supersedes the recommendation (having the live mode's instructions tell the agent to restart changed services with `devhost service restart <name>` as the turn's last step). From `FB-12` (owner item q68). For (superseded recommendation's case): the agent knows which code it changed and reads the manifest; devhost still restarts nothing by itself (`FB-5`), and the terminal redesign adds `devhost service restart <name>` (`CC-11`). Against (why the owner chose q68a2): an agent-run restart under a CLI that never asks restarts services without the person pressing anything; letting the receipt name each changed service with **Restart Changed** keeps restarts under the person's click while naming the remaining step (`FB-12`).
  - Consequence: a live turn that edited the api service's handler ends with `"api runs the old code until it restarts"` and **Restart Changed** in the receipt; one press restarts api and shows the change.
  - Backend work: the live-mode agent-restart clause proposed for `CC-10` is withdrawn; `devhost service restart` stays in stack-run-stop as its own terminal command (`CC-11`), and the turn receipt surfaces changed services from `watch` paths with **Restart Changed**.
  - Applied by: agent-chat (the turn receipt; withdraws the live-mode restart instruction), services-panel (**Restart Changed** offered from the receipt); stack-run-stop keeps `devhost service restart` (`CC-11`) for terminal use.
- **O59 How long an authenticated remote browser session stays active. Decided (product owner, 2026-11-18, owner item q69a1, settled by default, calibrated; covers both passkey and paired sessions under O36): until the person revokes it, ending after 30 days in which the browser made no request with its session cookie, with no fixed lifetime; `devhost pair` (and `devhost passkey`) lists every active remote browser session (browser and system from its user agent at sign-in, whether signed in via passkey or pairing link, when it signed in, when and from which network it was last used, and whether through the relay) and revokes one or all, and an authenticated remote page offers **Sign Out This Browser** (or **Unpair This Browser** for paired fallback sessions).** Blank without a comment on 2026-11-18, so the calibrated default stays applied and is treated as settled for reconciliation (its former editor host column from `O60` is removed because `O60` is retired). From `O36`, which left the session lifetime and idle expiry uncalibrated (owner item q69). For: the person's use of devhost's pages comes in bursts of days separated by weeks (below), so a limit counted from the last use ends a session only once the browser is really out of use, while a fixed lifetime expires inside a burst; an authenticated remote browser can use every control this machine's browser can, agents included (`O36`), so a forgotten device should not keep that power for ever. Peers that sign a browser in to a development server keep it for days to weeks: Jupyter Server prints a token at start and exchanges it for a sign-in cookie that lasts 30 days (its `set_login_cookie` passes no lifetime, so tornado's `set_signed_cookie` default `expires_days=30` applies; jupyter-server.readthedocs.io/en/latest/operators/security.html, tornadoweb.org/en/stable/web.html); JupyterHub's login cookie lasts 14 days (`cookie_max_age_days`); VS Code's `serve-web` keeps its connection-token cookie for a week and renews it on every workbench load (`src/vs/server/node/webClientServer.ts`, `maxAge: 60 * 60 * 24 * 7`); code-server's session cookie carries no lifetime, so it ends with the browser session (`src/node/http.ts`, `getCookieOptions`); Home Assistant signs out a device whose refresh token went unused for 90 days (home-assistant.io/docs/authentication); Tailscale re-authenticates devices every 180 days by default, 1 to 180 days, with expiry turned off for trusted servers (tailscale.com/kb/1028/key-expiry); Signal unlinks a linked device after 45 days without use (its support page as quoted in search results, since the page refused a direct fetch; a 2021 issue cites 30 days); Android revokes an `adb` authorization after 7 days without a connection (Android 11, as reported by XDA; the developer-options page no longer describes it). Against: a lost device keeps every control for up to 30 days after its last use unless the person revokes it, and a stolen cookie that keeps being used never expires; through a relay the gateway's own sign-in still stands in front (Cloudflare Access, the documented gateway: 24 hours by default, from immediate to a month; developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/identity/users/session-management), but on the direct network path the session cookie is the only gate.
  - Measured on one development machine's real devhost use, from Caddy's access logs and in aggregate. A person's browser is a Chrome user agent that is not headless and sends fetch metadata; agents' headless browsers, `curl` and Bun clients are left out. 10,810 such requests fell on 8 days over eight weeks. The idle periods of 4 hours or more were 7.4, 13.6 and 19.0 hours overnight, 26.8 hours across a weekend, and 604.4 and 625.9 hours (25.2 and 26.1 days); none fell between 26.8 hours and 25.2 days, and daytime breaks stayed under 4 hours (`O15`'s ladder found the same: 45 breaks of 5 minutes to 4 hours, overnight returns after 12.1 to 15.0 hours). The relay, in use for 20.6 hours at the time (1,604 relayed requests: one person, one browser, one stack, two networks), showed a 1.0-hour break and a 13.4-hour overnight one. No request ever reached Caddy directly from another device. Replaying the timeline as one browser: a 30-day idle limit needed no renewal; a 7-, 14- or 21-day idle limit needed 2, one after each long absence; a fixed 30 days needed 1; a fixed 24 hours needed 5, 2 of them less than 4 hours after the previous request, that is, mid-work. 30 days keeps the longest measured absence signed in with 3.9 days of margin, and ends a browser that never comes back 30 days after its last use.
  - Nearest alternatives: below, every idle limit from about 3 days (a full weekend, Friday evening to Monday morning, about 64 hours, which the data does not contain) to 25 days costs the same 2 renewals, so the strongest of them is 7 days, VS Code's sliding week, which ends a forgotten browser's access 23 days sooner at the cost of signing in again after every absence of a week or more; above, 45 days (Signal) or 90 days (Home Assistant) covers no measured absence that 30 days misses, since none falls between 26.1 and 45 days, while a lost device keeps every control 15 to 60 days longer. A fixed lifetime is the wrong shape: 24 hours, the relay gateway's default, asks for a new sign-in every day of use and twice mid-work in the data, and a fixed 30 days ends a session whatever its use; against a device the person knows is lost, revocation acts at once, and neither fixed lifetime adds to it.
  - Pitfalls: the timeline is the person's browser on the machine running the stacks, because direct remote use never occurred and relayed use had begun less than a day before the measurement; how often a person comes back to devhost's pages does not depend on where the browser runs, but a week of remote use that includes a weekend would measure it directly from the relay's requests, which carry the connector's `X-Forwarded-Host` (`apps/devhost/src/remote/proxy.rs:108`). In remote mode this machine's own browser comes through the relay and authenticates too (`O36`), so the limit also sets how often the person re-authenticates their own browser — with the shared-domain passkey (`O36`, q46a6), renewing on a phone or laptop takes one biometric tap without needing a terminal on the stack's machine. A cookie that keeps being used never reaches the idle limit; rotating the cookie's value on use, with a reused old value revoking the session, would bound a stolen cookie without a fixed lifetime (a backend option to weigh).
  - Consequence: a person signs in on a laptop or phone with their passkey (or pairs as fallback) once and stays signed in through nights, weekends, holidays and breaks of up to 30 days; a browser unused for longer shows "Sign in with passkey or pair this browser to use devhost here" again; `devhost pair` lists every active remote browser session and when it was last used, and revoking one ends its access at its next request.
  - Backend work: `O36`'s session store records, per session, the auth method (passkey or pair), the user agent at sign-in, the time created, and the time and network of the last use (written at most once a minute); the session check refuses and deletes a session whose last use is older than 30 days; `devhost pair` lists and revokes one session or all; the toolbar's authenticated remote state offers **Sign Out This Browser** / **Unpair This Browser**. Ships with `O36` (the agent-chat redesign, a2a2).
  - Applied by: agent-chat (owns `O36`'s check, passkey sign-in and `devhost pair`); every flow that shows the unauthenticated state (`HOLD-7`).
- **O60 Where the machine name in a remote editor link comes from (`{host}`). Retired (product owner, 2026-11-18, owner item q70 — retired by `O51` / q61a4, no editor support): editor support is removed completely (`O50`, `O51`), so devhost builds no remote editor links and collects no SSH host name per pairing.** Supersedes the recommendation (asking each paired browser once for its SSH host name and keeping it in `O36`'s session store). From `O51`, which had left `{host}`'s value open (owner item q70). For (superseded recommendation's case): every remote-editor link names the SSH destination as the editor's computer knows it (VS Code `vscode://vscode-remote/ssh-remote+<host>/<path>`, Cursor `cursor://`, JetBrains Toolbox/Gateway), whose SSH configuration lives on the editor's computer; in remote mode this machine's own browser also arrives through the relay, so only an answer per paired browser would have distinguished local from remote editors. Against: one more question per paired browser, moot once editors are dropped completely (`O51`).
  - Compared (superseded): the machine's host name alone, a per-machine `[devtools.editor].host` setting, `main`'s relay suffix, and asking at pairing were weighed before `O51` removed editor support.
  - Pitfalls (superseded): JetBrains' link opens a project (the worktree) rather than a line; today's link builder knew only four local forms (`packages/devhost-ui/src/devtools/features/componentSourceNavigation/componentSourceUtils.ts:12-36`, editors from `apps/devhost/src/manifest/validation.rs:85`).
  - Consequence: no SSH-host question is ever asked, `O36`'s session store keeps no editor host field, and `devhost pair` lists no SSH host column.
  - Backend work: none; the host field per session in `O36`'s session store, the `POST` from the source menu, `devhost pair`'s host column, and the proposed remote-editor contract change beside `CC-39` are withdrawn.
  - Applied by: none (retired; component-source drops remote editor links and agent-chat omits the host column from `devhost pair`).
- **O61 How devhost's pages name the machine that runs the stacks. Decided (product owner, 2026-11-18, owner item q77a1, settled by default; editor sentences removed per `O50` and `O51`): by one machine name, the same for every reader: `machineName` in the devhost config file, else the machine's host name up to its first dot; every browser surface that means the stacks' machine says that name where the designs say "this machine" (**All Stacks on devbox**, "Stacks on devbox", "No stacks are running on devbox. Start one with devhost start in its folder.", "Hostnames on devbox", the devhost config file's group and reach lines, the CPU, RAM and Disk readouts' scope, the certificate line, chat's agent-CLI line); terminal text keeps "this machine" where it means the machine a command runs on, and a terminal line that points to a page uses that page's words ("All stacks on devbox: https://devhost.localhost/").** Blank without a comment on 2026-11-18, so the default stays applied and is treated as settled for reconciliation; remote-editor mentions from `O50` and `O60` are removed because editor support is dropped (`O50`, `O51`). From `HOLD-8` (owner item q77): read in another environment, "this machine" names the reader's own computer, and no entry gave the stacks' machine a name the pages could show instead. For: one name means the same machine wherever it is read, while "this machine" is right only on that machine; the designs say "this machine" for the stacks' machine in 9 places and 1 more means it implicitly (chat's "No coding agent found: devhost looks for codex and claude on your PATH.", which means that machine's PATH), while today's build says it in a browser only in 3 refusals, each shown only to a client that is not on the machine, the one reader for whom "this machine" is the wrong computer (`apps/devhost/src/devtools/directory.rs:34-39` with Caddy's copy at `apps/devhost/src/caddy/files.rs:313-365`, `apps/devhost/src/devtools/config_files.rs:269`, and the relay connector's `apps/devhost/src/remote/proxy.rs:21-71`), so the words are new with the redesign and can be chosen now. The default needs no setup where the host name is the person's own name for the machine: on the measured development machine it was set by hand (macOS `HostName` is set), equals the local name the Mac announces on its network (`LocalHostName`), and is the label in its relay suffix. The setting covers machines whose host name means nothing or reveals too much: a container's host name "Defaults to the container's ID if not specified." (docs.docker.com/engine/network/); an AWS instance's default "IP name" includes "the private IPv4 address of the instance", as in `ip-10-24-34-0.ec2.internal` (docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/hostname-types.html); a Google Cloud VM's DHCP host name is `INSTANCE_NAME.ZONE.c.PROJECT_ID.internal` (docs.cloud.google.com/compute/docs/internal-dns), whose first label leaves the project ID out; and recordings and screenshots show whatever name the machine has. The devhost config file is already one per machine ("The one `config.toml` per machine", `WORD-1`), and `DEVHOST_CONFIG_DIR` can give each installation its own, so two devhost installations on one machine can carry two names. Peers name a remote machine by a name the person gave or can change, never "this machine": Tailscale "automatically generate[s] its machine name from its OS hostname", the person can edit it, and "editing the machine name also edits the MagicDNS domain name" (tailscale.com/kb/1098/machine-names); Docker contexts take the name given to `docker context create` ("creates a new context called `docker-test`", docs.docker.com/engine/manage-resources/contexts/); JetBrains Toolbox lists remote projects under "the name of the server", the address the person entered (jetbrains.com/help/toolbox-app/gettings-started-with-ssh.html); VS Code says "You can always refer to the Status bar to see which host you are connected to." (code.visualstudio.com/docs/remote/ssh), and its integrated browser warns that "A `file://` URL entered in a remotely connected tab also refers to the remote machine, not your local disk." (code.visualstudio.com/docs/debugtest/integrated-browser). Against: one more setting to document and validate; until the person sets one, the default shows the host name to readers who need no sign-in (the Caddy 404 to any device that reaches Caddy on plain HTTP, the stack directory under `stackDirectoryAllowRemote`, a relay preview's status pages to anyone the gateway admits) and in every screenshot and recording, although on a local network a Mac already announces its local name ("Your computer's name and local hostname are used to identify your computer on your local network.", support.apple.com/guide/mac-help/mchlp2322) and relay hostnames already carry the label the person chose, so the new exposure is mostly recordings, screenshots and cloud machines; a person who only ever reads the pages on that machine sees "devbox" where the designs said "this machine".
  - Compared: the host name alone (q77a2) is this default without the setting: right where the person named the machine, and wrong or revealing with nothing to change on a container, a cloud machine or in a recording, where the only remedy is renaming the machine, which also renames it for Bonjour, Tailscale and SSH. Reader-relative wording (q77a3: "this machine" for a client `O36`'s classifier counts as this machine's, the name for every other) still needs a name, gives every label two forms against `WORD-2`'s one label per operation, and fails twice: the Caddy 404 is one static file that devhost writes when routes change and Caddy serves to every client (`apps/devhost/src/caddy/files.rs:112`, `sync_not_found_site`, `route-not-found/index.html`), so it cannot change with the reader; and the classifier counts a browser that reaches Caddy through an SSH port forward, or a tunnel the person runs, as this machine, so exactly that remote reader would read "this machine" as their own computer (in remote mode this machine's own browser arrives through the relay and would see the name, which is harmless). No name (q77a4: **All Stacks**, "No stacks are running. …", "the machine that runs this stack" where a sentence must say where) costs nothing and is what today's directory does (its heading reads "Running stacks", `apps/devhost/src/statuspage.rs:147-157`), but the readouts' scope and the certificate line must say which machine, "the machine that runs this stack" is long for a menu item, and with stacks on two machines, which the relay docs set up ("Add apps or another machine", `stacks/remote-previews.md`), two directory tabs look the same. The relay suffix holds no name to read back: `[remote].domainSuffix` and the local `domainSuffix` are free-form suffixes (`.devbox.example.com` or `--devbox.example.com`) that exist only where the person set one (`apps/devhost/src/config.rs`, `apps/devhost/src/remote/settings.rs`).
  - Pitfalls: the certificate line reports the stacks' machine's trust store while a browser elsewhere warns by its own, so named it reads "devbox does not trust devhost's local certificate authority, …", and for a browser elsewhere the remedy is `devhost caddy trust-remote <SSH name>` on the browser's computer (macOS only, as today), not `devhost caddy trust`; first-run-setup's certificate line words both (its proposal still says "This machine" and names only `devhost caddy trust`). The name is the person's text: escaped, one line, and kept in its own spelling inside a title-style label (`WORD-3`: **All Stacks on devbox**). When no host name can be read and none is set, the labels drop the name, as q77a4 words them.
  - The run's own calls, which the product owner can override (q77 does not ask them): the name is at most 40 characters, so `machineName` takes no more and a longer host name is shortened in the middle, with the full name in its tooltip and accessible name, as `WORD-8` shortens paths; and the demo recordings, which show the toolbar's menu and the directory, give their films a fixed name instead of the recording machine's.
  - Consequence: in an authenticated remote browser in another environment, the stack name menu ends with **All Stacks on devbox**, the directory reads "Stacks on devbox" and, with nothing running, "No stacks are running on devbox. Start one with devhost start in its folder.", and the CPU readout's description names devbox; on devbox itself the words are the same; with `machineName` unset, devbox is the host name up to its first dot; `devhost start` prints "All stacks on devbox: https://devhost.localhost/" (exact words per 2.12).
  - Backend work: `machineName` in the devhost config file (`apps/devhost/src/config.rs` and its schema, with the config editor's completions), validated and reported with file, line and key when unusable (`MENU-4`), applied by the devhost config file's hot reload like the shared Caddy settings; the resolved name (the setting, else the OS host name up to its first dot) published to the toolbar and the status pages with the stack's state, written into the Caddy 404 and the stack directory's fallback page when they are rendered, and printed in `devhost start`'s directory line; contract changes: `guides/shared-managed-caddy-settings.md` documents the key, and the labels change with `CC-31`.
  - Applied by: stack-directory (the heading, tab title and empty state; the Caddy 404's link, group and empty sentence), toolbar-shell (the stack name menu's item; the readouts' scope), startup-and-recovery (the status pages' link), config-editor and proxy-settings (the devhost config file's group and reach lines), first-run-setup (the certificate line), agent-chat (the agent-CLI line), stack-run-stop (`devhost start`'s directory line); `MENU-1`, `MENU-5`, `WORD-1`, `WORD-2`, `STATE-2` and `language-02` follow this decision.
- **O62 CLI stack inspection command. Decided (product owner, 2026-11-25, owner item q80a1): extend the shipped `devhost stack info` command with manifest-reload rejection state, changed-file markers, `--json`, `--wait`, and distinct non-ready exit status (`3`), and add `devhost status` as a short alias for the exact same command (one implementation and output contract).** Rust `main` (`190a8bb5`) shipped `devhost stack info` backed by `GET /__devhost__/stack-info` (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack_info.rs`, `apps/devhost/src/services/stack/live.rs`), covering service states, PIDs, ports, URLs and recovery causes while still missing manifest-reload rejection state, changed-file markers, `--json`, `--wait` and a distinct non-ready exit code. For: extending the shipped command avoids duplicating inspection logic or breaking existing scripts that call `devhost stack info`, while adding `devhost status` as a short alias keeps the ergonomic top-level command people and agents expect (`FB-11`, `WORD-2`, `CC-23`). Against: two spellings (`stack info` and `status`) exist in `--help`, mitigated by sharing one implementation, one flag set and one output contract.
  - Consequence: `devhost stack info [--json] [--wait]` and `devhost status [--json] [--wait]` execute the same code path and produce identical human, `AGENT=1` and JSON output — listing each service's state, cause, since when, changed-file markers, manifest-reload rejection state, URLs and last operation outcome — exiting `0` when every started service runs, `3` when a started service is not running, and `1` on command failure.
  - Backend work: extend `GET /__devhost__/stack-info` (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/live.rs`) and `apps/devhost/src/services/stack_info.rs` with manifest-reload rejection state, changed-file markers, operation record summary, `--json`, `--wait` and exit code `3`; register `status` as a top-level alias for `stack info` in `apps/devhost/src/cli.rs` and `apps/devhost/src/main.rs`; update `reference/cli.md` and `skills/devhost/SKILL.md` (`CC-23`).
  - Applied by: stack-run-stop, manifest-hot-reload, services-panel, stack-directory, notices-and-feedback.
- **O63 Mistyped and recently stopped HTTPS hostnames under configured-hosts-only TLS. Decided (product owner, 2026-11-25, owner item q81a1): keep strict configured-hosts-only TLS for arbitrary unknown HTTPS hostnames (zero on-demand certificates for typos); keep configured Caddy routes and HTTPS certificates active for recently stopped stacks (`O15`: ran at least 1 minute for the directory, hostnames kept 24 hours) so revisiting a stopped HTTPS tab renders the stopped-stack recovery page over HTTPS with its start command; keep close-hostname typo suggestions (`O46`: up to 3 close running hostnames, 2 edits, 1 edit for 4 characters or fewer, no number swaps) on plain HTTP (when `httpEnabled = true`) and in the stack directory.** Rust `main` (`190a8bb5`) hardened Caddy's TLS policy in `apps/devhost/src/caddy/files.rs:539-552` so Caddy issues internal TLS certificates only for explicitly configured hostnames rather than minting on-demand certificates for arbitrary hostnames during handshakes. For: keeping strict configured-hosts-only TLS prevents unbounded on-demand certificate churn and TLS-handshake abuse from arbitrary hostnames; keeping explicit Caddy site entries for recently stopped stacks during `O15`'s 24-hour window lets a browser tab revisit a stopped stack over HTTPS and read its stopped-stack recovery page and start command cleanly without any wildcard or on-demand TLS; keeping `O46`'s close-hostname suggestions on plain HTTP (when `httpEnabled = true`) and in the stack directory preserves typo recovery without weakening TLS. Against: typing a brand-new mistyped `.localhost` URL directly over `https://` gets a TLS handshake error rather than an HTML suggestion page, directing the person to the stack directory or plain HTTP instead.
  - Consequence: revisiting `https://shop.acme.test/` the morning after `Acme Shop` stopped renders the stopped-stack recovery page over valid HTTPS for 24 hours (`O15`); typing an unconfigured typo over plain HTTP (when `httpEnabled = true`) or searching it in the stack directory shows `O46`'s close-hostname links; typing an arbitrary unconfigured hostname over HTTPS never mints an internal certificate.
  - Backend work: keep strict configured-hosts-only `tls internal` in `apps/devhost/src/caddy/files.rs:539-552`; emit configured stopped-stack Caddy routes for active `O15` stopped-stack records under the route mutation lock (`apps/devhost/src/caddy/stacks.rs`, `apps/devhost/src/caddy/files.rs:112`); keep `O46` typo suggestions on the plain-HTTP `sync_not_found_site` 404 page and in the stack directory (`CC-27`).
  - Applied by: stack-directory, stack-run-stop, proxy-settings, startup-and-recovery.

### 5.1 Product owner review of revisions 3 and 4

Revision 3's open decisions went to the product owner as owner items, each with its evidence, its question and its options, the recommended option marked: the run's own decisions (a1 to a10 and q1 to q12, some of them product calls), the product decisions (q13 to q45, which are O3 to O35), the flow design decisions (q46 to q61, which are O36 to O51) and two more (q62 and q63, which are O52 and O53); Phase 7 then put new items to the product owner: q64 and q65 (O54 and O55) under `HOLD-8`, q66 to q68 (O56 to O58) under `FB-12`, q69 and q70 (O59 and O60), the two values the network rework left open, and q77 (O61), how devhost's pages name the machine that runs the stacks; from q77 on, an owner ID is no longer its entry's O number plus ten, since q71 to q76 are owner items on other work, so each bullet names its entry. On 2026-11-18 the product owner first answered Run decisions round 1 and stated three rules (`HOLD-8`, `ACT-14`, `FB-12`), then submitted all three review pages (Run decisions round 2, Product decisions and Flow design decisions), settling 52 more entries alongside the 3 already decided (`O1`, `O2`, `O44`), withdrawing 2 (`O50`, `O51`), retiring 1 (`O60`), leaving 3 awaiting follow-ups (`O7`, `O36`, `O39`), and setting vocabulary rule `WORD-11`. Each item that decides an entry or a rule here has one bullet, in owner-ID order, with its one-line verdict (approved, reversed with the replacement, decided by comment, settled by default, awaiting owner with its follow-up, withdrawn, or retired). An item awaiting the owner keeps its recommended default in section 5, which flows assume. The product owner's other answers on the run's decisions (a3 to a6, a8 to a10, q1, q6, q11, q12, q74, q75 and q78) and the two items closed without an answer (q2 and q3) concern publishing, the tracker, upstream reports and the run's own operation; the run tracks them, and they decide nothing here. q4 and q5, answered on 2026-11-16, are `O1` (superseded in part by q10a6) and `O2`.

- **a1** (`ACT-11`, `ACT-14`): Approved (a1a1, 2026-11-18): build the process-identity fix now on its own branch, working on macOS and Linux (`ACT-14`).
- **a2** (`O36` to `O40`, `O1`'s network reach, `HOLD-7`, `review-16`): Reversed recommendation (a2a2, 2026-11-18): no standalone loopback-only stopgap; the agent-chat redesign makes remote execution safe while use from another environment keeps working (`HOLD-8`), and `bindHost` on macOS ships with it.
- **a7** (`HOLD-4`): Approved (a7a1, 2026-11-18): build the standalone read-stops-nothing worktree fix now ahead of worktree-switch's redesign.
- **q7** (`O36` to `O40`): Moot with a2a2, which ships no standalone loopback-only stopgap.
- **q8** (`O1`, `CC-10`): Approved (q8a1, 2026-11-18): the stopgap prompt that edits the active worktree in place may change the documented default prompt in the same release.
- **q9** (`O44`): Approved (q9a1, 2026-11-18): prefix routing (`path = "/api"` routes `/api` and everything below it), stated in docs and release notes; closed q54.
- **q10** (`O1`, `O3`, `O19`, `O20`, `O22`, `O35`, `FB-12`, `CC-10`): Decided by comment (q10a6, 2026-11-18): no worktree mode — the agent always edits whichever worktree the user has active (main's included) and creates no additional worktrees; supersedes q10a2 and q4's New worktree mode.
- **q13** (`O3`): Approved (q13a1, 2026-11-18): a message without page context keeps its conversation's last mode, defaulting to **This worktree (live)** (now two modes per q10a6).
- **q14** (`O4`): Approved (q14a1, 2026-11-18): with nothing marked changed, **Restart Changed** / Alt+Ctrl+R restarts the page's service, else the stack's primary service, named in the label.
- **q15** (`O5`): Approved (q15a1, 2026-11-18): app-produced 404 and 5xx responses keep the app's own document with devhost's notice instead of a devhost recovery page.
- **q16** (`O6`): Approved (q16a1, 2026-11-18): `devhost start <names>` starts the named omitted services with their dependencies and prints them.
- **q17** (`O7`, `WORD-11`): Awaiting owner on 2026-11-18 (blank with comment questioning "checkout", which established `WORD-11`); resolved in section 5.2 (2026-11-25, `q17a6`).
- **q18** (`O8`): Approved (q18a3, 2026-11-18): design down to 900x900 and 640x400 CSS pixels, plus a 320 px phone layout for chat, approval prompts and notices.
- **q19** (`O9`): Approved (q19a1, 2026-11-18): manifest edits keep today's restart scope and name each restarted service and why.
- **q20** (`O10`): Approved (q20a1, 2026-11-18): browser adds **Start** on a not-started service's hostname (gated by `O36`) and nothing more.
- **q21** (`O11`): Approved (q21a1, 2026-11-18): **Make tickets** files tickets directly and lists each filed ticket's title and link with no extra review step.
- **q22** (`O12`): Reversed recommendation (q22a2, 2026-11-18): decision card adds **Allow for This Session** (session-only, never persisted across restart) alongside Deny, Allow Once and Stop Turn.
- **q23** (`O13`): Approved (q23a1, 2026-11-18): browser-started turns follow the agent CLI's approval configuration and state it before the first Send (as a warning when it never asks).
- **q24** (`O14`): Approved (q24a3, 2026-11-18, calibrated): success notices stay max(4 s, 1.5 s + 0.3 s/word) capped at 15 s, Undo stays 4 s; refined by q63a3 for unfocused arrivals.
- **q25** (`O15`): Approved (q25a1, 2026-11-18, calibrated): stopped stacks are remembered for 24 h (or until a hostname is claimed or Caddy stops) and listed if they ran at least 1 minute.
- **q26** (`O16`): Reversed recommendation (q26a2, 2026-11-18): a waiting agent or failure prefixes the tab title of both devhost's own pages and app pages (`ACT-7`).
- **q27** (`O17`): Approved (q27a1, 2026-11-18): manifest key bindings are the team default and the devhost config file overrides them binding by binding.
- **q28** (`O18`): Approved (q28a1, 2026-11-18): `devhost stack restart` becomes `devhost stack replace` (old name kept as alias), with an open follow-up on replacing stacks during an upgrade (superseded in section 5.2, 2026-11-25, `q79a2`).
- **q29** (`O19`): Approved (q29a1, 2026-11-18): **This worktree (live)** edits in place without committing, stashing, restoring, resetting or checking out files, building on uncommitted changes.
- **q30** (`O20`): Approved (q30a1, 2026-11-18): mode changes within a conversation send replacing instructions and show a composer reminder (narrowed by q10a6 to live vs tickets).
- **q31** (`O21`): Decided by comment (q31a3, 2026-11-18): when the agent cannot file tickets, **Make tickets** changes no files and says it cannot make tickets; no drafts.
- **q32** (`O22`): Approved (q32a1, 2026-11-18): finished turns list changed files with a copyable reverse patch and an undo follow-up; devhost runs no Git command (`UNDO-5`).
- **q33** (`O23`): Approved (q33a1, 2026-11-18): ended conversations keep metadata only (never message text) while the stopped stack is remembered.
- **q34** (`O24`): Approved (q34a1, confirmed 2026-11-18): after a Disable Reloads turn, the notice names the request, what devhost observed, and **Restart** for the affected service.
- **q35** (`O25`): Approved (q35a1, 2026-11-18): for a dev server with no verified switch, the popover says so, leads with **Hold Reloads**, and keeps an editable generic request.
- **q36** (`O26`): Approved (q36a1, 2026-11-18): Disable Reloads may edit a service's `command` only for a command-line flag, never `watch`, naming every restarted service.
- **q37** (`O27`): Approved (q37a1, 2026-11-18): Disable Reloads defaults to a new conversation and offers each open conversation of the worktree by title.
- **q38** (`O28`): Approved (q38a1, 2026-11-18, calibrated): a `process` health check must stay up 1 s after spawn before a start, restart or reload counts it started.
- **q39** (`O29`): Approved (q39a1, 2026-11-18): **Revert** reloads the version on disk; returning to what runs stays **Restore Running Version**.
- **q40** (`O30`, `CC-35`): Reversed by comment (q40a3, 2026-11-18): keeps q40a1's progress line, Ctrl-C/second-stop force and never killing a stopping supervisor, with a 30 s configurable default wait; open follow-up on fitting shutdown inside 30 s (settled in section 5.2, 2026-11-25).
- **q41** (`O31`): Approved (q41a1, 2026-11-18): `devhost stack replace --all` replaces every manifest stack (foreground included) and leaves serve sessions running with their restart commands.
- **q42** (`O32`): Approved (q42a1, 2026-11-18): devhost runs nothing new before switching worktrees and reports setup failures with the install command to run there.
- **q43** (`O33`): Approved (q43a1, 2026-11-18): `devhost stop --worktrees` and `devhost stack replace --all` ask only when a working or waiting turn would end, and refuse without a terminal or under `AGENT=1` unless `--yes`.
- **q44** (`O34`): Approved (q44a4, 2026-11-18, calibrated, provisional until human holds are measured): holding Alt alone for 400 ms starts Select Elements; pointer move starts it at once.
- **q45** (`O35`, `CC-10`): Approved (q45a4, 2026-11-18): `prompt` becomes team guidance sent first, followed by mode instructions that decide edit behavior; `[devtools.chat.modes.<mode>].prompt` narrowed to `live` and `tickets` (`worktree` withdrawn by q10a6).
- **q46** (`O36`): Awaiting owner on 2026-11-18 (blank with passkey question); resolved in section 5.2 (2026-11-25, `q46a6`).
- **q47** (`O37`): Approved (q47a4, 2026-11-18): this machine and paired browsers alike switch worktrees, stop other stacks and restart services (recovery and receipt restarts included); unpaired clients get no paths or PIDs.
- **q48** (`O38`): Approved (q48a3, 2026-11-18): paired browsers read configuration when the editor is on; unpaired clients see the unpaired state.
- **q49** (`O39`): Awaiting owner on 2026-11-18 (blank with question about why loopback bind was disabled on macOS, answered on its thread); resolved in section 5.2 (2026-11-25, `q49a5`).
- **q50** (`O40`): Reversed recommendation (q50a2, 2026-11-18): `devhost serve` keeps chat on by default, stated on the first line, with `--no-chat` to turn it off (replaces proposed `--chat <folder>`); `--port` still never hands the shell's current folder.
- **q51** (`O41`): Settled by default (q51a4, blank without comment on 2026-11-18, calibrated): shared 20 s deadline for daemon status read and stop, with per-service manifest override.
- **q52** (`O42`): Approved (q52a4, 2026-11-18, calibrated): an unseen save reports after 5 s while no operation holds the stack; a superseded save ends as replaced.
- **q53** (`O43`): Approved (q53a1, 2026-11-18): when a running worktree is deleted, its services keep running until recovery while the repository is marked blocked and devhost starts nothing new.
- **q54** (`O44`): Closed by q9a1 (2026-11-18, prefix routing).
- **q55** (`O45`): Reversed recommendation (q55a3, 2026-11-18): neither `publicUrl` nor `DEVHOST_PUBLIC_URL`; keep today's behavior; proxy-settings Backend 10 and contract change to `guides/service-references.md` withdrawn.
- **q56** (`O46`): Approved (q56a4, 2026-11-18, calibrated): Caddy 404 names stopped stacks first, then offers up to three routed hostnames within two edits (one for ≤4 chars, never different numbers).
- **q57** (`O47`): Approved (q57a3, 2026-11-18): paired browsers read the stack directory and press **Check Again** (relayed too); `stackDirectoryAllowRemote`-only readers see **Check Again** unavailable.
- **q58** (`O48`): Approved (q58a4, 2026-11-18, calibrated): CPU 20 s average (70%/65%, 90%/85%), RAM per reading (75%/70%, 90%/85% held 10 s), Disk fixed floors (<20 GiB low, <10 GiB very low, 1 GiB band).
- **q59** (`O49`): Approved (q59a4, 2026-11-18, calibrated): keep latest 50 changes per Zustand store within 16 MB (2 bytes/char), with guard for oversized stores and window count notice.
- **q60** (`O50`): Withdrawn by comment (q60a4, 2026-11-18, “drop this feature”): devhost never starts an editor; component-source Backend 2 and its proposed contract changes withdrawn.
