- **q66** (`O56`, `O2`, `FB-12`): Reversed recommendation (q66a2, 2026-11-18, comment “just reload to see the change”): **Disable Reloads** wins — devhost never auto-reloads after a later turn; each turn that changed files ends with `"Reload to see the change"` and **Reload Page**.
- **q67** (`O57`, `UNDO-3`): Approved (q67a1, 2026-11-18): while Hold Reloads is on (and reloads are not disabled under `O56`), a turn's end applies held reloads once unless focus is in a text field.
- **q68** (`O58`, `FB-5`, `CC-10`): Reversed recommendation (q68a2, 2026-11-18): the person restarts changed services from the turn's summary (**Restart Changed**); the agent does not restart as the turn's last step; live-mode agent-restart clause of `CC-10` withdrawn.
- **q69** (`O59`): Settled by default (q69a1, blank without comment on 2026-11-18, calibrated): paired until revoked, ending after 30 days unused, with `devhost pair` list/revoke and **Unpair This Browser** (`O60`'s host column dropped).
- **q70** (`O60`): Retired (2026-11-18, by q61a4 / `O51` dropping editors completely): no SSH-host question, session-store field, or contract change.
- **q77** (`O61`, `MENU-1`, `MENU-5`, `WORD-1`, `WORD-2`, `STATE-2`): Settled by default (q77a1, blank without comment on 2026-11-18): pages name the stacks' machine via `machineName` else host name up to its first dot; remote-editor sentences removed per `O50` and `O51`.

The product owner's rules, added in revisions 4 and 5:

- `HOLD-8` (added in revision 4, completed in revision 6): using devhost from another environment is a primary use case (devhost started in one environment, its pages used from a browser in another), so no protection confines a feature to this machine; remote execution is made safe for that use instead, this machine stays trusted, and `bindHost` is honored on macOS; from the owner's comment of 2026-11-18 on a2. Decided under it: q18, q20, q46 (`q46a6`, 2026-11-25), q47, q48, q49 (`q49a5`, 2026-11-25), q50, q57, q64, q65, q69 and q77; withdrawn: q60 and q61 (retiring q70); zero items awaiting follow-up.
- `ACT-14` (added in revision 4): the process-identity fix, under which stopping a service signals only the processes devhost started and never a later process that reuses a PID, works on macOS and Linux (`process-containment-01`, now landed on Rust `main` `190a8bb5`); from the owner's comment of 2026-11-18 on a1.
- `FB-12` (added in revision 4, updated in revision 5): every chat turn that changes code ends with the person seeing the change in the running app, or with the one step left named; from the owner's comment of 2026-11-18 on q10. In revision 5, q10a6 simplified chat to edit only the active worktree (removing worktree mode), q66a2 had **Disable Reloads** win with `"Reload to see the change"` and **Reload Page**, q67a1 approved turn-end held-reload flush, and q68a2 had the person restart changed services from the turn receipt with **Restart Changed**.
- `WORD-11` (added in revision 5): `worktree` names every working copy of a repository, the main worktree included; `checkout` is never used as a noun for a working copy in devhost's UI, CLI, docs or designs (keeping `git checkout`, finding IDs and app URL examples intact); from the owner's comment of 2026-11-18 on q17.

### 5.2 Product owner review of revision 5

On 2026-11-25 the product owner answered the final Phase 7 review round covering the three items carried over from revision 5 (`q17`, `q46`, `q49`), the process-replacement and upgrade follow-up (`q79`, superseding `q28a1`), and the two new product calls raised by the Rust `main` cutover at `190a8bb5` (`q80` and `q81`), while `O30` (`q40a3`) was settled through the parallel-shutdown design. Every product decision in section 5 (`O1`–`O63`) is now Decided, Withdrawn or Retired, with zero entries awaiting the owner.

- **q17** (`O7`, `SEL-5`, `ACT-9`, `selection-05`, `status-11`): Reversed revision 5's default (`q17a6`, 2026-11-25, owner comment verbatim: “No need to warn about external services”): external services (`managed = false`) never block their repository's worktree switch, and switching managed services needs no warning about external services staying put; managed services restart from the target worktree's folder while external services stay untouched (still marked external in the Services panel).
- **q40** (`O30`, `O41`, `ACT-11`, `CC-35`, `CC-39`): Settled parallel-shutdown design (`q40a3`, 2026-11-25): foreground and daemon stops run in parallel from `t = 0` with one shared process snapshot, a single termination signal fan-out (`interrupted-operations-06`), 10 s `SIGTERM` grace + up to 5 s post-`SIGKILL` verify + shared 2 s late-listener scan concurrently with `O41`'s 20 s daemon deadline (25.64 s combined worst case, 4.36 s margin inside 30 s default), configurable `stopTimeout` (`5s`..`10m`, `--timeout` > `DEVHOST_STOP_TIMEOUT` > `devhost.toml` `stopTimeout` > `config.toml` `stopTimeout` > `30s`), automatic widening to `max(stopTimeout, max_daemon_override + 10s)` when a daemon override exceeds 20 s unless CLI `--timeout` caps the run, unified across all stop callers without ever killing a stopping supervisor.
- **q46** (`O36`, `HOLD-7`, `HOLD-8`, `O59`, `CC-39`, `review-16`): Reversed revision 5's pairing-only default (`q46a6`, 2026-11-25, owner comment verbatim: “I'm guessing the pass get here but the important thing is that it has to work on my phone as well”): one shared WebAuthn passkey under a single owned parent HTTPS domain (non-secret public key synced in `config.toml`; phone support is a hard requirement via synced passkeys and hybrid/QR sign-in; pure-Rust `webauthn-rs` `0.6.x` backend with zero OpenSSL), local machine stays trusted, 30-day idle host-only `__Host-` session cookies (`O59`), and one-time `devhost pair` links kept as fallback for bootstrapping, non-domain hostnames and lockout recovery.
- **q49** (`O39`, `HOLD-8`, `review-16`): Decided (`q49a5`, 2026-11-25, owner comment verbatim: “Pick whichever as long as it preserves a current functionality from the end user experience”): on macOS, honor `bindHost = "127.0.0.1"` from port 1024 upward (`default_bind 127.0.0.1 [::1]`); below port 1024 (including default 443), keep listening on all interfaces so existing port-443 setups continue starting without error, while printing an explicit warning on every start naming the fix (`httpsPort = 4443` or a privileged launcher).
- **q79** (follow-up to `q28`; `O18`, `SEL-6`, `REV-7`, `WORD-2`, `CC-12`, `CC-24`, `CC-31`, `CC-36`): Reversed `q28a1`'s `devhost stack replace` rename (`q79a2`, 2026-11-25): add built-in `devhost upgrade` (download latest release excluding `media`, verify `SHA256SUMS`, swap user-writable binary or report read-only/externally managed installs without breaking them, replace running manifest stacks) and keep shipped `devhost restart-all` and project-scoped `devhost stack restart` on one verb for dotfiles, package managers and local rebuilds while stating clearly in help and output that they replace the running devhost supervisor process.
- **q80** (`O62`, `SEL-7`, `ACT-1`, `FB-11`, `WORD-2`, `CC-23`): Decided (`q80a1`, 2026-11-25): extend shipped `devhost stack info` with manifest-reload rejection state, changed-file markers, `--json`, `--wait` and distinct non-ready exit status (`3`), and add `devhost status` as a short alias sharing the exact same implementation and output contract.
- **q81** (`O63`, `SEL-10`, `ACT-10`, `STATE-2`, `O15`, `O46`, `CC-27`): Decided (`q81a1`, 2026-11-25): keep strict configured-hosts-only TLS for arbitrary unknown HTTPS hostnames (zero on-demand certificates for typos); keep configured Caddy routes and HTTPS certificates active for recently stopped stacks (`O15` 24-hour window) so revisiting a stopped HTTPS tab renders the stopped-stack recovery page over HTTPS; keep close-hostname typo suggestions (`O46`) on plain HTTP (when `httpEnabled = true`) and in the stack directory.

Run-scope decisions recorded on 2026-11-25 (affecting run artifacts rather than adding new `O` entries): `q82a1` freezes the 19 historical current-flow diagrams and 5 research notes at pre-Rust commit `edb5c0fc` while migrating `patterns.md`, all 19 proposals, `wireframes/notices-and-feedback.html` and all 19 proposed diagrams to Rust `main` (`190a8bb5`, retiring fixed findings `process-containment-01`, `worktree-switch-14`, `process-containment-02` and `manifest-hot-reload-2-01`); `q83a2` adds a live mini-review of command-mode `devhost serve -- <command>` before Phase 8 reconciliation.

## 6. Review notes

Each issue from the revision 1 critique, by lens, and each rule conflict the exemplar check found in revision 2, with what was done. "Applied" names the rules that now carry the fix; "rejected" gives the reason.

DEV (a developer running local stacks with coding agents):

- **DEV-1** The crash view opened by itself as a modal that took focus to its lead action and made docked chat inert, so an exit caused by a live-mode agent's edit could turn a keystroke in flight into Restart and strand the agent's pending decision card. Applied: `SURF-3` (it opens minimized, as the crash notice, while the person types or chat holds a draft, a decision card or a working agent, and otherwise focuses its heading, never a button; docked chat and the notice stack stay operable over it), `KEY-6`, `KEY-7`, 2.4's Accessibility and States, the 2.11 collisions paragraph and States, `CC-2`, `keyboard-10` (now its option (c)) and startup-and-recovery's keys line.
- **DEV-2** Escape in the chat composer still stopped a running turn when no mode was active and no card waited, so a press meant to leave the field ended an agent's work mid-edit (`keyboard-and-screen-reader-06`). Applied: `KEY-8` (Escape leaves a mode, else returns to the page, and never stops a turn), `KEY-9` (Stop Turn's own key, Alt+Ctrl+., configurable as `stopTurn`, only while no decision card waits), the 2.11 table, collisions paragraph, States and Backend contract, `CC-29`, `CC-31`, `keyboard-08` and agent-chat's keys line.
- **DEV-3** `REV-7` asked by whether a terminal was attached, so a person was asked on every multi-stack command while an agent under `AGENT=1` could end other agents' turns unasked, and it settled the research note's product-owner question without an O entry. Applied in part: `REV-7` (print the set and act; only when the set would end an agent's turn that works or waits, ask in a terminal, or refuse without one or under `AGENT=1`, unless `--yes`), the `REV-1` row, 2.5's States and Backend contract, `CC-12`, `review-04`, `review-05`, new `O33`, section 5's intro and the stack-run-stop and first-run-setup lines. Rejected in part: a foreground stack in another terminal as a trigger, because `O31` replaces such a stack with a report in its own terminal and `SEL-6` already names it in the set, so counting it would make the documented post-upgrade `devhost stack restart --all` ask whenever any foreground stack runs, against the fix's own aim.
- **DEV-4** The queue of starts and restarts lived in the page, so queued work vanished without an outcome on a reload, a closed tab or a lost stream, and other tabs and `devhost status` never saw it. Applied: `SEL-7` and 2.1's Backend contract (the services runtime holds the queue and publishes each queued operation in the operation record, cancellable from any surface), `CC-8`, `UNDO-2`, `HOLD-2`, `HOLD-3` and `REV-12`.
- **DEV-5** `HOLD-4` kept today's read that stops a repository's services when it finds the selected checkout deleted, so opening the picker or a status page stopped a stack nobody asked to stop (the same read stops it after a rejected edit, `worktree-switch-14`). Applied: `HOLD-4` (a read stops nothing; the repository is blocked while its processes keep running from the deleted folder until the person's recovery restarts it or they exit), `ACT-2`, 2.6's Backend contract (the GET side effect removed), `status-10`, and the worktree-switch and startup-and-recovery lines.
- **DEV-6** The way back from a live-mode edit was the checkout's Git, whose whole-file restore also discards the person's own uncommitted changes in a file the agent touched. Applied: `UNDO-5` and `O22` (the turn's record offers the reverse patch of the agent's own edits and a follow-up asking the agent to undo the turn, never a whole-file restore; a file it cannot reverse carries that warning; devhost still writes no project file itself), 2.9's Backend contract, `feedback-12` and agent-chat's line.
- **DEV-7** The composer said **This checkout (live)** without naming the checkout, so after a switch, or with another checkout's conversation open, a live edit could land in a checkout the page does not run, with nothing to reload. Applied: `SEL-8` (the mode control shows the `SEL-4` label of the checkout the message edits; another checkout's conversation is named before Send with **New Conversation** offered; nothing moves silently), `REV-6`, `WORD-10`, `UNDO-8` and agent-chat's line.
- **DEV-8** `REV-3` did not say whether restarting services of a working agent's checkout counts as ending its work, so services-panel and agent-chat could read the restart loop of live mode either way. Applied: `REV-3` (only a stop or replacement of its stack ends an agent's work and only a switch or Return to Configured Checkout hides it; a start or restart never asks on its account, with the Alt+Ctrl+R example) and the `REV-1` row.
- **DEV-9** **Edit** in the crash view closed the view holding the exit's evidence and opened the modal Configuration window, whose banner had no stated content, so the person edited without the error they were fixing. Applied: `SURF-4` (an exited service's banner carries its `FAIL-3` evidence with **Show All Output** expanding the shared log in place; a rejected reload's banner carries the structured error and, after a crash, its last lines), 2.4's States, `WORD-2` and config-editor's line.
- **DEV-10** `SURF-16` reloaded an app's own 404 or 5xx by re-requesting the URL until it answered, which reruns the failing route, its logged trace and any side effect of a GET (`error-pages-05`), and ignored **Hold Reloads**. Applied: `SURF-16` (no timer; one reload per sign that the answer changed, held like any reload) and `ACT-12` (its back-off is for devhost's own status pages), and startup-and-recovery's line.
- **DEV-11** `REV-2` asked before every Restart Stack and every Retry of a failed switch, also in the crash view and on recovery pages where the page's service is already down and the restart is the remedy on offer. Applied: `REV-1`, `REV-2` (the question stays in the Services panel beside Restart Changed; elsewhere one press with its reach named, and Retry on one press), 2.5's States, `CC-17`, `review-01` and the startup-and-recovery and worktree-switch lines.
- **DEV-12** The second-agent question also stood before messages in **New worktree (review before merge)** and **Make tickets**, which edit no file of this checkout. Applied: `REV-3`, `REV-6` (asked only for **This checkout (live)**, once when the conversation's agent would start), `CC-14`, `review-12` and agent-chat's line.
- **DEV-13** `UNDO-3` kept a held reload pending only while focus was in a devhost text field when the hold was turned off, a test the toggle's own press defeats in most browsers, and it ignored the page's own half-typed form. Applied in part: the focus-dependent branch is removed from `UNDO-3`, with 2.9's States, `CC-20`, section 5's intro and reload-control's line. Rejected in part: both proposed replacements, testing the element focused before the toggle and always reloading, because `A11Y-13` shows that a toggle that reloads the page changes context on input (WCAG 3.2.2); turning the hold off now never reloads by itself and keeps "1 reload pending" with **Reload Now**, the reload-control research note's default for its question 6.
- **DEV-14** The persona's most frequent action, talking to the agent about the page, had no key. Applied in part: `KEY-2` and the 2.11 table gain `chat`, Alt+Ctrl+C, which opens docked chat with focus in the composer, with `KEY-6`, the collisions paragraph, States and Backend contract, `CC-29` and agent-chat's keys line. Rejected in part: pressing the chord again inside the composer to return, because `KEY-1` suspends chords in text entry, where AltGr+C types a character on Polish and Czech layouts; Escape returns focus there instead (`KEY-8`).
- **DEV-15** `devhost stop` could wait up to a minute plus 20 s per service with no way to force a detached stack except `kill -9`, while a foreground stack's second Ctrl-C forces. Applied: `KEY-14` (the waiting line names what still stops and that Ctrl-C forces it; Ctrl-C or a second `devhost stop` forces the rest, signalling only verified process trees), the 2.11 table and Backend contract, `ACT-11`, `CC-35` and stack-run-stop's line.
- **DEV-16** A manifest save's restart set could be seen beforehand only in the Configuration window, which is off by default, so editor users and agents learned it from the outcome. Applied in part: `FB-11` and `CC-23` gain `devhost reload --dry-run`, which prints what a reload would do from `REV-10`'s check and changes nothing, in both outputs, with 2.5's Backend contract and stack-run-stop's line. Rejected in part: checking only the files on disk before the watcher applies them, because every save applies through hot reload as soon as it lands (`REV-10`, owner rule), so the disk text is already applied or rejected by then; `--stdin <file>` checks a candidate text before it is written instead.
- **DEV-17** The worktree mode's report named a branch and a worktree but no way to run it, so reviewing the change meant finding the worktree in the picker and back again. Applied: `UNDO-5` (the report offers **Switch and Restart Stack**, opening the checkout list with that worktree picked as `REV-2`'s first step, and **Return to Configured Checkout** after the switch), `SURF-15`, and the agent-chat and worktree-switch lines.
- **DEV-18** An agent waiting in a background stack surfaced nowhere the person looks across stacks, and the stack directory ordered cards by failure only. Applied: `ACT-6` (stacks with a waiting agent come first with the failed ones and count in the totals and title; each waiting agent is a link to its decision card in one activation), `SURF-8`, `REV-9`, 2.10's Backend contract and stack-directory's lines.

A11Y (accessibility and keyboard):

- **A11Y-1** The crash view, the one surface that opens by itself, sent focus to its lead action, so a Space or Enter typed into the composer or a field as a service exited ran Restart and replaced the exit's evidence (`keyboard-and-screen-reader-01`). Applied in part: by `DEV-1` (`SURF-3`, `KEY-6`, `keyboard-10` as its option (c), the collisions paragraph and 2.11's States: the view focuses its heading, described by the cause, with the lead action as the next Tab stop, and opens minimized while the person types), and `SURF-3` now focuses the heading also when the person expands the view from its notice. Rejected in part: the lead action as first focus when the view is opened from its notice, because `A11Y-9` shows that a write focused after a change runs on a repeated Enter before the person reads it, and `DEV-11` made the view's Restart Stack run on one press; one first-focus rule keeps the lead action one Tab away either way.
- **A11Y-2** `FB-9` made one polite region the only voice while every notice also carried a live role, so each report was spoken twice, failures both politely and assertively, and the activity line, a status region ticking every second, was read again each second. Applied: `FB-9` (one announcer per page owns a polite and an assertive, failures-only region and is the only live output; notice cards carry no live role; ticking text sits outside every live region; a merged report that holds a failure goes to the assertive region), 2.7's Accessibility and Component primitive (`Alert` takes no live role), 2.8's Accessibility and `ACT-7`.
- **A11Y-3** Reports went silent under every modal dialog: devhost's Configuration window made the page-level announcer and the notice stack inert, the rules knew only a native modal of the page while scripted modals (Radix and similar) hide devhost with `aria-hidden` and pull its focus back, and the paused bar's name sat on an inert element. Applied: section 1 defines the page's modal dialog (native, `role="dialog"` or `"alertdialog"` with `aria-modal="true"`, or the devtools host made inert or hidden), `SURF-11` (the notice stack and the announcer move into it; the paused state's reason is a line in the moved stack), `FB-9` (the announcer moves into the topmost modal, devhost's or the page's), `SURF-4` (a report that needs the person shows as one line in the Configuration window's header), 2.4's States and Accessibility, `CC-3`, `feedback-14`, and section 4's universal items (a scripted-modal fixture) and notices-and-feedback's line.
- **A11Y-4** `KEY-10` made the checkout picker a combobox whose options hold no controls while `SEL-4`, `SEL-5`, `HOLD-4` and `MENU-4` put remedies on blocked rows, so **Open**, **Copy Error** and **Check Again** could not be reached from the keyboard, and badge reasons hid behind a hover. Applied: `KEY-10` (the highlighted option's remedies show in a details line below the list as the Tab stops after the find field, updated as the highlight moves; badge reasons are text in the option's description, WCAG 1.4.13), `SEL-4`, `MENU-4`, 2.1's States and the collisions paragraph.
- **A11Y-5** `KEY-12` exempted drawing from the keyboard requirement as path-based input, but Arrow, Line and Box depend only on their two ends, which WCAG 2.1.1 does not exempt, and only a drag drew them (WCAG 2.5.7). Applied: `KEY-12` (two clicks set a shape's ends as a drag does; with a drawing tool active the arrows walk the highlight, Enter sets the start and a second Enter the end, Box boxes the highlighted element on one Enter, Escape cancels), `keyboard-14`, the 2.11 table, Rationale and States, and `CC-29`.
- **A11Y-6** The keyboard model for picking elements could not be built as written: it highlighted the focused page element while focus sat on devhost's toggle, gave the arrows to the toggle's group, let arrows pressed with Alt held reach the browser's Back and Forward, and announced no highlight move. Applied: `KEY-1` (Mode's keys act before the focused scope's, except in text entry), `KEY-12` (the arrow walk only in latched Select Elements; the highlight starts on the element focused before devhost took focus, else the main landmark, else the first visible element; focus stays on the toggle, described by the highlighted element; each move announced politely with role, name and position; arrows with Alt held do nothing), the 2.11 table and 2.1's Accessibility.
- **A11Y-7** Once focus left an open docked surface, no rule led back to it: Alt+Ctrl+B reached only the bar, Alt+Ctrl+O closed an open log panel, and **Chat** on the bar was undefined while chat was docked. Applied in part: `KEY-3` (Alt+Ctrl+B goes on from the bar to the open log panel and docked chat's composer, then back; one DOM and Tab order: notice stack, bar, log panel, docked chat), `KEY-2` and `KEY-6` (**Chat** or its chord focus the composer while chat is docked; chat closes by its own **Close**), `KEY-12`, the 2.11 table and States, and `CC-29`. Rejected in part: docked chat before the log panel in that order, because chords are suspended in the composer (`KEY-1`), so a cycle could not go on from it, and the composer comes last, where Escape completes the cycle (`KEY-8`); and Alt+Ctrl+O focusing an open panel from the page, because `A11Y-12` needs a key that hides the panel without moving focus (WCAG 2.4.11), and Alt+Ctrl+B's cycle already reaches it.
- **A11Y-8** In the composer, Escape stopped a running turn when no mode was active, so the key people press to leave ended an agent's edit depending on state a screen-reader user could not perceive (`keyboard-and-screen-reader-06`). Applied: by `DEV-2` (`KEY-8`: Escape leaves a mode, else returns to the page, and never stops a turn; `KEY-9`: **Stop Turn** keeps its visible control and gets its own key; the 2.11 table, collisions paragraph and States, `CC-29`, `keyboard-08`). Its key is Alt+Ctrl+., not the suggested Mod+Shift+Backspace, which Chrome and Firefox take for Clear Browsing Data on macOS.
- **A11Y-9** Focus was lost between confirming a question and seeing its outcome: the focused confirm button went away with the question, sending focus to the page body and closing its panel, and an outcome then put focus on its first action, a write such as Retry that a repeated Enter would start. Applied: `REV-4` and `KEY-11` (the confirm button stays in place as the busy control and keeps focus, its progress beside it), `FB-9` and `KEY-11` (an outcome that replaces focused content takes focus on its sentence, never on an action, with its actions next in Tab order), and 2.5's and 2.11's States.
- **A11Y-10** The named components could not give the focusable unavailable state the rules require while 2.3 and 2.11 said "library work: none": Radix's menu removes a disabled item from focus and type-ahead, and the project's `Button` passes only the native `disabled`, which drops focus to the body. Applied: the Component primitive paragraphs of 2.3, 2.6, 2.7 and 2.11 (`Button` gains an unavailable state with `aria-disabled`, ignored activation and `aria-describedby` to the visible reason; menus use Base UI's Menu or Radix items with `aria-disabled` and a guarded `onSelect`; bar items use Base UI's `Toolbar.Button` with `focusableWhenDisabled`) and 2.11's States (a focused control that becomes unavailable keeps focus).
- **A11Y-11** The chord sweep missed Windows: Magnifier uses the documented Alt+Ctrl+R to resize its lens, and AltGr, which is Ctrl+Alt there, types characters on B and O in several layouts, which devhost's capture listener would take as chords in an editing widget it does not recognise as text entry. Applied with the critique's second option for Alt+Ctrl+R: `KEY-2` (a chord event carrying another printable character is typed text; the promise names the Magnifier collision that Alt+Ctrl+R keeps, rebindable per `O17`), the collisions paragraph (the Windows sweep and its source), 2.11's States, `keyboard-02` and `O17`'s For (VoiceOver and Magnifier users need a per-person override).
- **A11Y-12** The acceptance criteria left out three WCAG 2.2 AA checks that hit an overlay toolbar hardest: 200% zoom, which turns 1280x800 into 640x400 CSS pixels, below the narrowest viewport, and reflow of full pages; a focused page element hidden under the dock, docked chat or the log panel (2.4.11); and pointer targets below 24 CSS pixels (2.5.8). Applied: `SURF-9` (1280x800 at 200% zoom as an acceptance viewport, full pages reflowing at 320 CSS pixels, the dock and docked chat stepping aside from a focused page element and Alt+Ctrl+O hiding the log panel without moving focus, 24-pixel targets or 2.5.8's spacing), `KEY-12`, `O8` (its default, For and Against) and section 4's universal items (an Accessibility line with forced colors and the contrast contract). Docked chat steps aside instead of closing by its chord, because Alt+Ctrl+C moves focus to the composer (`KEY-2`).
- **A11Y-13** Turning **Hold Reloads** off reloaded the page at once, a change of context on input (WCAG 3.2.2) that a keyboard or screen-reader user always got, and no rule said where focus starts after a reload devhost causes. Applied: `UNDO-3` and `CC-20` by `DEV-13` (turning the hold off never reloads; "1 reload pending" waits for **Reload Now**, the reload-control research note's default for its question 6), and `KEY-6` (after a reload devhost caused from one of its controls, focus starts on that control, else on the sentence of the outcome that reports it, else on the bar item whose surface held it, never at the top of the document), with 2.11's States.
- **A11Y-14** `KEY-5` put devhost's text entry in a same-origin frame, which breaks the ID references its own rules depend on: a combobox's field in a frame cannot point at its list in the Shadow DOM, nor a composer or a Deny note at what names or describes it. Applied: `KEY-5` (a classic script injected first in `<head>` stops keys typed into devhost's text entry at the window in the capture phase, after devhost's own handling, and all text entry stays in the Shadow DOM, so every ARIA reference stays in one tree), `KEY-10`, 2.11's Accessibility, Component primitive and Backend contract, `CC-28`, `keyboard-05`, `keyboard-08` and toolbar-shell's line.
- **A11Y-15** `FB-9` announced no end of a chat turn, a regression from today's polite "Latest agent response" region, and no rule kept streaming output out of live regions: a `role="log"` log panel would read every line of a busy server, and its resize handle had no keyboard or single-pointer way. Applied: `FB-9` (each ended turn announced once with its outcome and the reply's first sentence or "reply ready"; streaming text never announced, the transcript `aria-busy` meanwhile), `SURF-13` (lines never a live region, **Jump to Latest** with the count of new lines, the resize handle a focusable separator moved by the arrows, Home, End and click steps; the recovery view's logs alike), 2.4's Accessibility and `WORD-2`.
- **A11Y-16** `REV-9`'s promise that moving to a decision card focuses its question held only for the waiting markers: Shift+Tab from the composer, where focus already is, landed on **Stop Turn**, a write, before the question was read. Applied: `REV-9` and `KEY-10` (the card is one Tab stop entered on its question; the arrows move between Details and its buttons; Tab leaves), 2.5's Accessibility and States, and the 2.11 table.
- **A11Y-17** `KEY-1` kept holding Alt as the start of Select Elements in the composer while selection starts on the Alt keydown itself, so every character typed with Option on German, Swiss, French and Nordic Mac layouts (@, braces, brackets, |, backslash, ~) flashed selection over the page and let a click pick an element, and `KEY-2` gave no test to tell a hold from a chord still being pressed. Applied: `KEY-2` and `KEY-1` (selection starts on the first pointer move while Alt alone is held, or after Alt alone has been held for the time new `O34` sets, 300 ms by default; any other key before that cancels it without drawing), section 5's intro, the 2.11 table, collisions paragraph and States, and `CC-29`.
- **A11Y-18** Actions in notices that close by themselves were time-limited with no untimed equivalent guaranteed (WCAG 2.2.1), although `O14` itself cited 2.2.1 and outcome notices carry links. Applied: `FB-6` (every action or link in a notice that closes by itself has a named untimed equivalent: its log line, **Show Reload Notice**, the Worktrees panel; the notice's description says where it stays) and `O14` (its number is a reading-comfort default, not a time limit).
- **A11Y-19** Focus return depended on "opened with the pointer", which no rule defined, so a screen reader's synthesized click could count as pointer use and send focus on close to the page element focused before instead of the trigger, losing the person's place in the toolbar. Applied: `KEY-5` (only a `pointerdown` with `pointerType` mouse, pen or touch on the trigger makes an opening pointer use; any other activation returns focus to the trigger), which `KEY-6` cites, and 2.11's States.

ENG (feasibility and contracts):

- **ENG-1** `SEL-7`, `HOLD-3` and `CC-8` put the queue of starts and restarts in the page, although the services runtime already makes a request wait for the operation before it, so queued work was lost on a reload, each tab kept its own queue, and `devhost service restart` behaved differently from the toolbar. Applied: by `DEV-4` (`SEL-7` and 2.1's Backend contract: the services runtime holds the queue, publishes each queued operation in the operation record and lets any surface cancel it; `CC-8`, `HOLD-3`, `REV-12`, `UNDO-2`), and new `CC-37` adds queued operations and their cancel route to `reference/control-api.md`.
- **ENG-2** (blocker) `SEL-5`, `SEL-8` and `REV-9` made every checkout's conversations listed and answerable from any page of the stack, while `reference/chat-api.md`, a binding contract, lists only the page's directory and re-checks it before every command, and no contract change said otherwise. Applied with the critique's second option: new `CC-38` (the session list covers the stack and a socket attaches to any of its sessions; `respond` and `interrupt` work across the stack's checkouts, while `send` keeps the check of the page's checkout), `SEL-5`, `SEL-8` (another checkout's conversation shows its transcript, its cards and **Stop Turn**, takes no message, and offers **New Conversation** in the page's checkout), `UNDO-5` (the request to merge is sent after **Return to Configured Checkout**), `REV-9`, `ACT-6`, 2.1's Backend contract and agent-chat's line; the first option, a read-only list linking to a page of that checkout, would strand an agent waiting in a checkout a switch left, because the stack then serves no page of it.
- **ENG-3** `SEL-11`, `WORD-1` and `O17` moved the person's editor and key bindings into the devhost config file and introduced `DEVHOST_EDITOR`, but no contract change quoted the glossary's definition of that file and no Backend contract named its new keys, the variable or their precedence. Applied: new `CC-39` (`CONTEXT.md`'s definition of the devhost config file; `DEVHOST_EDITOR` and the file's row in `reference/cli.md`), 2.1's Backend contract (`[devtools.editor]`, and `[devtools.shortcuts]` under `O17`, in the file's schema; the control server sends each merged value with its source), `SEL-11`, `WORD-1`, `O17`, `selection-14` and component-source's line.
- **ENG-4** `SURF-7` kept the toolbar's corner and collapsed state in the record behind chat's panel route, which answers 404 while chat is off and keeps nothing without a manifest, so a stack with chat off could not save or read its placement and a serve session had none. Applied: `SURF-7` and 2.4's Backend contract (a layout route of its own, `/__devhost__/layout`, that answers whether chat is on or off, per manifest or per served folder, in place of the chat panel route), `CC-5` (`reference/control-api.md` and `reference/chat-api.md`'s Panel position) and the line under Work no flow owns.
- **ENG-5** `SURF-16`, `FB-11` and 2.4's Backend contract promised devhost's short 503 for requests that are not page loads, but Caddy proxies those straight to the app and returns its own empty 502 when the app is down, and no Backend contract named the routing work. Applied: 2.4's Backend contract (each host route gains a `handle_errors` for requests that are not page loads, handing a request the app could not answer to the service's document injection server, which answers 503 with `Retry-After` and the phase), `SURF-16`, and `CC-4` (the Request routing table of `reference/control-api.md`).
- **ENG-6** `REV-7` asked `[y/N]` whenever standard input was a terminal and promised that scripts and installers never meet the question, although a script started from an interactive shell inherits that terminal, and `CC-12` left the documented upgrade step unchanged. Applied with the critique's second option: by `DEV-3` (`REV-7` asks only while the set would end an agent's turn, replacing the false promise), and `REV-7` now asks only when standard input and output are both a terminal and `CI` is unset, refusing otherwise; `CC-12` gives the upgrade step in `guides/stack-lifecycle.md` `--yes` for scripts, and `O33` records the choice.
- **ENG-7** `REV-9` and `CC-16` let **Deny** carry a note for every agent, but Codex's command, file-change and permission answers carry no text, so nothing said how a note reaches Codex. Applied: `REV-9`, 2.5's Backend contract and `CC-16` (Claude Code gets the note as the denial's message; Codex gets the decline, then the note as a message into the running turn, shown as the person's message with its delivery state and without devhost's added instructions).
- **ENG-8** `REV-6`, `UNDO-4` and `STATE-1` let a queued follow-up be cancelled back into the composer, and `KEY-9` and the 2.11 table added Mod+Shift+Enter to steer the running turn beside Enter, but every message sent during a turn already goes into it at once as a steer, `queued` means the agent's CLI has it, and only `interrupt`, which stops the turn, takes it back. Applied with the critique's second option: `REV-6` and `UNDO-4` (only a message still waiting on the page for an earlier send's acknowledgement can be taken back; one the CLI has is cancelled only with its turn), `STATE-1` ("Sent into the running turn · the agent reads it at its next step"), `UNDO-2`, `CC-19`, 2.5's Backend contract, 2.9's and 2.13's States and agent-chat's line; the Mod+Shift+Enter row and `KEY-9`'s clause are removed.
- **ENG-9** `REV-12`, `FB-2` and `ACT-1` took a lost response's outcome from the published state and showed a missed outcome on the next page load, but no request carried an identity, the write routes answered only when the operation ended, and a record kept until the next operation of its kind would greet every later page load with an old outcome. Applied: `ACT-1` (the caller's operation ID, a lowercase UUID as chat's `commandId`, on every write; `202 Accepted` with the record before the operation runs; its end time; a loaded page shows a missed outcome only for an ID it stored before reloading or an unhandled failure, never a success older than `O14`'s time), `REV-12` (a repeated ID returns its record and runs nothing again), `FB-2`, 2.7's Backend contract and `CC-37`.
- **ENG-10** `ACT-1`, `FB-7` and `WORD-4` reported origins such as "saved in an editor" and "by Claude Code", which the backend cannot observe: the file watcher cannot tell an editor's save from an agent's edit, a checkout or a formatter, the control API knows no caller, and the CLI under `AGENT=1` does not know which agent runs it. Applied: `ACT-1` (origin by the channel the backend sees: this tab or another by a page ID sent with each request, the terminal, an agent under `AGENT=1`, named with its conversation only when devhost started it and set `DEVHOST_CHAT_SESSION`, and a file change on disk), `FB-1`, `FB-7`, `WORD-4`, `FB-11`, section 1's Operation, 2.7's Backend contract and States, and `CC-13`.
- **ENG-11** `UNDO-2` and `CC-19` kept chat's unsent text and pasted images through a reload "as today", and page context with its marks, but named no store: today only the text survives, `reference/chat-api.md` keeps selections and drawings out of browser storage, and browser storage cannot hold the 8 MiB a send may carry. Applied: `UNDO-2` (the text stays in browser storage as today; pasted images and page context go to a draft record on the control server per stack, page origin and conversation, capped at 8 MiB, read under the config routes' `Host` rule and removed on an acknowledged send or at stack stop), 2.9's Backend contract and `CC-19` (the draft route and its `Host` rule in `reference/control-api.md`, and `reference/chat-api.md`'s Browser state).
- **ENG-12** `UNDO-2` and `CC-19` moved Configuration window drafts to one server record per stack and file without saying what happens when two tabs edit the same file, where the last write would silently replace the other's typing, a race today's per-tab drafts do not have. Applied: `UNDO-2` (each draft carries a revision; a write from an older revision is refused, and the window shows "Changed in another tab" with **Compare**, **Keep Mine**, which asks, and **Use Other Version**, `UNDO-3`'s conflict view), 2.9's Backend contract and States, `WORD-2`, `CC-19` (the draft routes under the config routes' `Host` rule) and config-editor's line.
- **ENG-13** `REV-9`, `UNDO-2`, `UNDO-8` and the Backend contracts of 2.5, 2.9 and 2.12 put each answer with its note, each message's mode and page context and the text devhost added in "the chat log", which is the activity log, whose documented privacy rule excludes prompts and answers, while the replay journal holds only native events, and no contract change covered either reading. Applied: `UNDO-8` and the Backend contracts of 2.5, 2.9 and 2.12 (devhost writes these records as frames of its own into the session's journal, sequenced with the native events and replayed in `snapshot`; activity log lines keep only IDs and outcomes), `O22` (the per-turn file list reads the journal's native events, not the log), and new `CC-40` (the `record` frame in `reference/chat-api.md`, whose Activity logs sentence stays unchanged).
- **ENG-14** `ACT-8` cleared a service's published changed mark when the page saw its dev server apply an edit in place, and 2.10's Backend contract computed the mark from the hot updates the page reports, so the state every tab, the terminal and `devhost status` read depended on whether some page was open, through a route that does not exist and a per-client detection `O24` records as unverified. Applied: `ACT-8` (changed is computed on the server alone from each watched file's content at launch against now; the page adds its own "applied in place" note beside the mark without clearing it), 2.10's Backend contract and `CC-25`.
- **ENG-15** (blocker) `ACT-11` let a health check pass only for the process tree devhost started, but devhost documents services whose listener lives outside that tree: Docker-backed services behind Docker's port proxy, daemon-lifecycle services started by `lifecycle.start`, and services whose wrapper later opens the port, so those stacks would never become ready. Applied: `ACT-11` (a killed run's recorded leftovers are found, named and refused before launch, `REV-11`'s probe names any other program on a fixed port, and a health check certifies the endpoint, not process ancestry; verified ownership stays for signals, `process-containment-01`), `FB-5`, 2.10's Backend contract and `status-14` (Decision (revision 2)).
- **ENG-16** `ACT-10`, `STATE-2`, `O15` and `CC-27` had a stopped stack's hostnames and the stack directory read a stopped-stack record, but with no stack running both are static files Caddy serves, so nothing reads the record at request time or expires it, a killed stack cannot write its own, and the directory site refuses every path but its root, which `ACT-12`'s live directory needs. Applied: 2.10's Backend contract and `O15` (the stopping process, or the next devhost command that finds a killed stack's owner dead, writes the record and re-renders the static 404, stopped-stack routes per `O63`, and `stacks.html` under the route mutation lock with each stop time embedded, so the page hides entries older than `O15`'s limit; the directory site gains one path the open page polls; the files in `apps/devhost/src/caddy/` (`files.rs`, `stacks.rs`) that change are named).
- **ENG-17** `KEY-5` and `CC-28` isolated keys by putting devhost's text entry in a same-origin frame with "library work: none", but moving the mount into a page's modal discards a frame's document with the draft, input-method composition and focus, the frame needs its own styles and fonts and cross-frame forwarding of every devhost key, and a same-origin page can still read its document. Applied: by `A11Y-14` (the frame gave way to an early script, with text entry kept in the Shadow DOM), and now `KEY-5`, 2.11's Component primitive and Backend contract and `CC-28` settle the mechanism: a classic script of its own, first in `<head>`, registers window capture listeners for `keydown`, `keypress`, `keyup` and `beforeinput` before any page script, stops an event whose `composedPath()[0]` is a devhost text field, dispatches at that field a clone that stays inside the shadow root for devhost's own handlers, CodeMirror's included, and copies the clone's `preventDefault()` onto the original.
- **ENG-18** 2.14 changed `reference/control-api.md` only for the health row and the disconnect sentence, although the rules add or change routes and responses it documents for integrations and scripts: a service stop with no route or runtime operation today, operation IDs and queued operations, `config/validate` returning the restart set, draft routes, the layout route, and `MENU-6`'s documented status and one JSON error shape, while the handlers answer every failure as a plain-text 500 the Status codes table does not list. Applied: `CC-37` (operation IDs and `202 Accepted`, queued operations and their cancel route, `start-service` and a new `stop-service` route, `config/validate`'s reach, `409` for another operation, `422` for an unknown or inapplicable service, and one JSON error body `{error, service}`), with the draft routes in `CC-19` and the layout route in `CC-5`; 2.3's Backend contract (the stop operation in the services runtime, and the `devhost service` commands' channel, the control server's loopback port recorded in `apps/devhost/src/caddy/stacks.rs`), `MENU-6`, `HOLD-3` and 2.5's Backend contract.
- **ENG-19** `O1`'s backend work and `CC-10` gave the built-in workflow prompt one text per mode without saying what a manifest's `[devtools.chat].prompt`, which today replaces the built-in instructions for every send, does with three modes, so a team prompt could contradict the mode shown under a message, and `send` gained a mode `reference/chat-api.md` does not document. Applied: new `O35` (recommended default: each mode's instructions are built in and always sent, `prompt` becomes the team's guidance after them, `[devtools.chat.modes.<mode>].prompt` replaces one mode's instructions, and a `prompt` set today is reported once with the new keys), `O1`'s backend work (it points to `O35` and records the mode in the session's journal), `CC-10` (the example manifest's `prompt` text, `reference/chat-api.md`'s Workflow instructions and `send`'s `mode`), `REV-6` and section 5's intro.
- **ENG-20** No contract change amended `reference/chat-api.md`, although `O23`, `UNDO-4` and `FB-4` keep ended conversations listed and resumable after their stack stops, `UNDO-2` keeps delivery states across reloads, and `HOLD-5` keeps a running conversation's routes answering while chat is off, each against a sentence that contract states. Applied: `CC-38` (ended sessions stay listed from `O23`'s record and resume with `resumeId`; with chat off, the list, the socket, `respond`, `interrupt` and close answer for running sessions while starting one answers `404`), `CC-40` (each `send`'s acknowledgement kept as a `record` frame), 2.14's intro (every change to `reference/chat-api.md` and `reference/control-api.md` listed), `UNDO-2`, `UNDO-4`, `HOLD-5`, 2.6's Backend contract and `O23`.
- **ENG-21** `STATE-2` and `CC-33` kept the running agents control on the bar with "No agents in this checkout" when none runs, but its route answers 404 while chat is off, so that sentence would be a guess, against `STATE-3`. Applied with the critique's second option: `STATE-2` and `CC-33` (with chat off the control reads "Running agents are shown while chat is on" and never claims none, and a switch's reach line says agents were not checked, `REV-3`), because `guides/devtools.md` documents that the list needs `[devtools.chat].enabled`.

Exemplar check of revision 2 (rule conflicts found while the services-panel exemplar was designed against it):

- **EXC-1** `BAR-2` put a set's count in the label of every set action, Restart Stack named among them, and `WORD-5` agreed, while `WORD-2` labels it **Restart Stack**, `REV-2` states its count in the first press's statement or the crash view's reach line, and `SEL-1` allows one line beside the control, so the services-panel exemplar and the crash view could follow only one reading. Applied: `BAR-2` (the count goes in a label that is a verb with its target; a stack-wide write keeps its operation's name and states the count in `REV-2`'s statement or reach line, its members in its tooltip and accessible description), `WORD-2`, `WORD-5`, `CC-17` (the reach is named beside the button, not on it), and the services-panel and startup-and-recovery lines. No decision changed: selection-02 decided that the control states the count in its first press's statement, and language-14's button count came from Restart Changed (`services-panel-2-02`).
- **EXC-2** `REV-4` asked a question to name each target by name, which `REV-2`'s own example ("Restart all 6 services …") did not do and `SEL-1` does only for one or two members. Applied with the check's fix: `REV-4` (a question names its targets as `SEL-1` names a set: by name for one or two, otherwise by count and unit with every member in the question's accessible description), `REV-2`'s example (the six services named in that description), 2.5's Accessibility and services-panel's line.
- **EXC-3** `REV-4` said Escape or clicking away cancels a question and returns focus to the control that asked, but a click outside a toolbar panel closes the panel (`SURF-2`) and Tab past its last control closes it with focus on its trigger (`KEY-7`), so for a question inside a panel the control that asked is gone, and no rule said what a click elsewhere inside the panel does. Applied with the check's fix, narrowed: `REV-4` (Escape, or a click elsewhere in the question's surface, cancels it with focus back on the control that asked; whatever closes that surface cancels the question with it, focus going where that close sends it, `KEY-6`), 2.5's Accessibility, the 2.11 table's rows for Escape in a question and Tab past a panel, and the services-panel and worktree-switch lines. The fix also sent focus back after a click on another control in the surface; that control takes focus and acts instead, because sending focus back would undo the click the person made.
- **EXC-4** `SEL-7` let any surface cancel a queued start or restart, but `WORD-2` gave that action no label and kept **Cancel** for a question's choice that changes nothing, so the exemplar labelled the queued row's action **Cancel** with no target in its accessible name (`KEY-10`). Applied with the check's fix: `WORD-2` (**Cancel Restart** and **Cancel Start**, the accessible name adding the target, "Cancel Restart of worker", and the outcome "Cancelled the queued restart of worker"; a bare **Cancel** stays a question's choice), `SEL-7`, `CC-8` and services-panel's line.
- **EXC-5** `WORD-8` wrote a signal by name and number ("terminated by SIGTERM (143)") while `WORD-7`'s example left the number out ("Terminated by SIGTERM at 21:53:34, not sent by devhost"), so one exit had two formats. Applied with the check's fix: `WORD-8` (the signal by name, then the status a shell reports for it, 128 plus its number, as `stacks/recovery.md` documents it), `WORD-7`'s example ("Terminated by SIGTERM (143) at 21:53:34, not sent by devhost") and `FAIL-3`.
- **EXC-6** `UNDO-1` gave every rollback the sentence "Restored the accepted manifest: …", and `FAIL-1` attached it to a Restart Stack, although Restart Stack runs the accepted manifest before and after (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:373`, `restart_stack`) and its rollback relaunches the previous resolved manifest, the services as they ran with their previous automatic ports (`apps/devhost/src/services/stack/runtime.rs:717`, `apply`), while a switch's rollback puts back the previous checkout. Applied: `UNDO-1` (one sentence per operation, in progress and as the outcome: "Restored the accepted manifest" after a reload, "Restored the services as they ran before Restart Stack" after Restart Stack, "Returned to main ~/acme" after a switch, each followed by what runs now), `FAIL-1`'s example, `ACT-3`, and the services-panel, worktree-switch, startup-and-recovery and manifest-hot-reload lines. The check's "Restored the previous run" was not used, because "the run" names a stack's whole run from start to stop elsewhere in this reference (`SEL-7`).

     - **A11Y-1** (blocker) What was wrong, in the past tense. Applied: `KEY-2` (how).
